# Constitution drafting review log (critic actions applied/rejected per topic)

> **Constitution catch-up — 2026-11-15 (independent review; no owner decision or Q/PV/X status change):** the source tasks are STR-03 string removal (`65885c4f`), the Q-10 decimal-places extension (`1d3e1354`), declaration-level output spreading (`6c736e3f`), its certain-read follow-up (`899789d8`, `4562d74c`), the two E-18 F1 closeout runs (`local-33d87e2c`, `local-56b601db`; `73d6256e`), E-15 finite-loop widening (`20742ba8`), the ordinary-call production bridge (`f4bc7ac1`), and intermediate fallback/capture-depth repairs (`da209fa0`). The draft's dated note maps the changed entries to these sources and current `main` @ `9ec6bf3b`. The review scoped LOOP-05's index and summary marker to non-integral count rejection, preserving its correct law and Decimal128 qualification. Complete corrected report and final documentation checks: `../constitution-catch-up-20261115/review/`.

> **SEC-02-FU independent review, 2026-10-21 (C#-only runtime protection; no new semantic rule or Lean cost model).** RUN-03 is amended in both editions to cover operation-local structural equality/hash work in collection builtins, comparison links, repeated-name compatibility and literal string patterns. The review reproduced missing literal payload charges/cancellation and final-flush comparer reuse, added regressions, and repaired both. Native string calls remain bounded and non-interruptible internally; reference sharing affects work, never equality. No historical rule, question, lineage, status or inventory is reclassified. Fresh validation and performance evidence is recorded separately in `artifacts/validation/sec02fu-review*`.

> **Independent R3 adversarial review addendum, 2026-10-15:** the earlier R3 counts describe the implementation snapshot. The reviewed sources now have 69 BinderTrace theorems (one added local allocator-freshness law), 25 FamilyDispatch theorems, 63 BinderCorrespondence theorems, 55 BinderGuards guards and 7 correspondence instance guards. Together with the 26 BinderCore and 12 adapted FORMAL-07 laws, the R3 axiom-audit inventory is 195 theorems. `allocateNeed_fresh_for_valid_supply` proves that one allocation avoids all addresses valid in its incoming heap and preserves their validity; it does not prove whole-binder freshness. The static plain-cell noninterference corollary still assumes no allocation reuses the excluded cells: initial supplied-address validity and nonshrinking channels would discharge that assumption, but the whole-run bridge and the partial production channels' heap-monotonicity proof remain outstanding. Trace erasure quantifies over arbitrary original EvalM capabilities lifted through the protected interface, not arbitrary trace-aware capabilities. Mode correspondence is one-directional: differing outcomes imply a family non-match and ordinary binder incompatibility; a channel raising an incompatibility-shaped error still raises in both modes. New guards pin this distinction, state retention after failed VALUE/allocation, emitted-count inequality, nested repeated names, equal callable identities and allocator reuse. The stale C# repeated-name test comment is corrected; executable C# is unchanged. FORMAL-06, Q-66 (OPEN), R4-R7, opaque-helper limitations and all prior decisions/statuses remain unchanged. This note records proof scope and guard coverage; validation and the final review verdict are recorded separately in `artifacts/validation/r3-independent-review/`.


> **Q-67 COMPLETED — 2026-10-15 (approved implementation, final frozen V3 passed):** FORMAL-06's neutral success/raw-value/root-count/innermost-category contract is UNCHANGED. Separate reviewed shared-payload guards, public failure-phase checks, route payload checks, exact demand/terminality witnesses and executable generator claims settle Q-67. Current Q81 totals: **53 resolved + 1 superseded + 2 partially resolved (Q-35, Q-44) + 25 fully open = 81** (27 with an open part; zero before release). PV50 and X49 classifications/totals are unchanged. Q-66 remains OPEN; X-43 remains SUPERSEDED by Model C. Earlier dated Q-67-open statements and totals are historical snapshots. Full mapping and evidence: `docs/design/differential-observations.md`, `artifacts/validation/q67-differential-observations/`.

Correction: in root emission it is NOT k items at the value level; the root value keeps one slot (`repeat(Fib, 1, 0, 1), 5` is `S[S[1, 1], 5] n=3`, displayed `(1, 1)` and `5`, `k03`), and only the emitted count and a lone row's display reflect k (VAL-07, LOOP-07). Crossrefs to LOOP-03 and LOOP-07 added.

12. **owner decision — Q-01, at-most-once argument value evaluation: applied (2026-09-28).** The owner decided: "Within one call, a written argument slot is evaluated at most once for its value. If that evaluation succeeds, that is its value. If it fails, that is its failure. Reading the bound parameter never causes the argument expression to execute again." It was implemented in Lean and C# in the working tree over `3e57a08` (not yet committed).
    - CALL-06 is rewritten as the one normative statement of the law (retitled, same ID, status Current). CALL-02's caveat, PAR-01, VAL-10, VAR-04, PROP-04, PROP-05, PROP-07, PROP-09, HO-01, HO-04, RUN-07, RUN-08, RAND-01 and RAND-02 defer to it; the CALL, PROP, ALGHO and RAND open-question lists and coverage row H06 record the resolution. PV-04 moved to `open-questions.md` § Resolved violations with its original record intact; X-14 is closed; Q-01 moved to § Already resolved; Q-05 and the ⟂Q-01 documentation items were updated.
    - Every changed example was re-run with the five-path probe runner rebuilt from the fixed tree (`scratchpad/probes/q01-constitution/`): all AGREE.
    - Hostile post-fix review (probes in `scratchpad/probes/hostile/` and the regression suite): aliases, opened properties, structural members, dot calls and the dot fallback, forwarding (three levels, and a recursion 50 calls deep), collecting, mixed, nested-pattern and clause-family binders, repeated names, callbacks and parameters captured by callbacks, planned loops and loop temps, the fused filter-count pipeline, deconstruction inside the callee, implicit lifting, explicit `f()` versus value reads, random draws, sync and async host operations, and ordinary and resource-limit failures. No remaining route evaluates a written slot twice or lets a failed slot's parameter produce a value, and a parameter's reads never disagree. The only DISAGREE observations (`Deep = Deep`, recursion 50 deep) are the pre-existing twin host-stack headroom difference (X-34 / PV-36), identical on the pre-fix and fixed trees — a success control without any failure shows the same split.
    - Validation (V3): `validate-all.ps1 -Phase DotNet` — KatLang.Tests 23,410, KatLang.CLI.Tests 296, KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests 133 passed, 0 failed, build 0 warnings; `-Phase Lean` — all seven targets built; the repository was unchanged by both phases.

7. **add-rule OP-14 `.string` text (evaluator-kinds): applied.** It is added as OP-14. The code is `Evaluator.ResultToString` (`Evaluator.cs:2091`) → `ValueTextRenderer.FormatNumberInvariant` (`ValueTextRenderer.cs:216`). The stated example reproduces exactly, AGREE: s01, s02, and s03 with `DisplayDecimals = 2`. One refinement from the renderer's doc comment and probe s06: "quantum kept" holds only for non-positive exponents. `1e3.string == 1000.string` is `true`, and a zero with a positive exponent writes `0`, so the rule says "every trailing FRACTIONAL zero" and the caveat states the limit. The non-constitutional "Quantum propagation" bullet now adds "and in `.string` values (OP-14)". The "Number text" bullet is restricted to DISPLAY text. The value-identity open question now names `.string` as the in-language observer. Probes: `critique-builtins-lean/s02.kat` re-run AGREE (`1.0.string == 1.string` false, `(3 - 1.0).string` `'2.0'`), and my `OP-final/s04.kat` AGREE.
9. **amend-rule OP-07 narrowing (builtins-lean): applied.** The title and rule now restrict "value, not error" to a Math function's NUMERIC domain. They list the errors that remain: the zero divisor, the zero base, `round` digits (`Evaluator.Expressions.cs:817-821`), `random`/`randomInt` bounds (RAND-03), the certification cap (`Evaluator.cs:3278-3284`), and non-numeric arguments. Probes AGREE: `d01` `S[NaN ×7, Infinity, 0]`; `round(1.5, 0.5)` and `round(1.5, -1)` → `IllegalInEval`; `random(2, 1)` and `randomInt(1.5, 3)` → `IllegalInEval`. I added a caveat that WHICH special a degenerate input returns is the library's (platform `Decimal128.Log`) verdict, because `log(8, 0)` is NaN where a naive IEEE `ln 8 / ln 0` would be `-0`. Related fix: the "Integer control arguments" bullet no longer assigns `round` digits to COLL, which has no such rule. It is Math library surface, with the error-versus-value fact in OP-07.

## Q-05 decision, implementation and Constitution synchronization (2026-09-29)

The owner decided open question Q-05 on 2026-09-29 with its option (b), extended to every occurrence: *repeated-name matching may restrict a binding; it must never manufacture a binding that no individual argument supplied. A repeated parameter name is a compatibility constraint over independently supplied arguments; it never merges incomplete contributions into a new binding.* This entry records the implementation task's synchronization. The implementation is in the working tree over `abe1383` (not yet committed); Lean and C# changed together.

- **Decision recorded:** Q-05 moved to § Already resolved (the question as posed kept below the decision; options (a) and (c) rejected). PV-13 moved from § Intentional exceptions to § Resolved violations as "RESOLVED BY DECISION" (it was an intentional exception with a lens disagreement, never an active violation), with a Resolution block (precise classification, decision, mechanism removed, fix, Lean, consequences, pins, validation, residue). X-17 is resolved.
- **Rules:** PAT-09 is retitled (same ID, class and status category) and now states the law: every occurrence supplies its own value; values compare equal; a failed required value propagates; a callable-only contribution cannot satisfy the constraint; several algorithm channels share one identity; channels are never spliced across occurrences; matching restricts, never manufactures. PAT-10 (a valueless occurrence is a binding failure that precedes every verdict; the within-merge tie-break is value before callable identity) and CALL-06 (a repeated parameter name demands the slot's value; the former "binds the value" caveat) cite it. The PAT open questions on the family asymmetry (PV-43) and the Decimal128 representative (Q-28) stay open; the latter records the one residue of the no-splicing rule.
- **Examples verified:** every new or changed example is a row of `RepeatedNameConstraintTests` (six routes: the sync and async engines, the async twin with a suspending host operation, the generic and optimized evaluators, the forced twin; host-call logs and error locations compared) or a canonical spec case, and the headline programs were re-run with the CLI built from the final tree. "Formerly" figures are observations on `abe1383`.

## Post-audit open question Q-72 (2026-09-29)

The Q-05 hostile review flagged, and a follow-up investigation confirmed, that implicit lifting collapses a callee's repeated parameter name: `P(x, x) = x` / `Alias = P` elaborates to `Alias(x) = P(x, x)` (by-name forwarding, PAR-05 "skipping names already present"). No semantics were changed. Recorded as open question Q-72 in `open-questions.md` (root cause, Lean position, the FWD-02 conflict beside PV-08, options (a) keep and document, (b) lift the repetition, (c) refuse the implicit form) and as a FWD-02 known-tension bullet in the draft. Current behavior is pinned, explicitly as undecided, by `RepeatedNameCalleeLiftingTests` (11 cases, passing). Counts: 72 questions, 67 open and 5 resolved.

*Superseded the same day:* the owner decided Q-72 with option (c); the undecided pins were rewritten to the decided rule. See § Q-72 decision, implementation and Constitution synchronization (2026-09-29) below.

Nothing was staged or committed.

## Q-72 decision, implementation and Constitution synchronization (2026-09-29)

The owner decided the post-audit open question Q-72 on 2026-09-29 with its option (c): *a callable whose parameter patterns contain repeated binding names must not participate in implicit parameter forwarding/lifting; the programmer must write an explicit wrapper/call.* The invariant it protects, beside Q-05: *repeated occurrences represent independently supplied inputs whose compatibility is checked at binding time; no implicit transformation may collapse those occurrences into one input unless the source explicitly supplies that same input to each occurrence.* The owner also accepted Q-05 as implemented, keeping a callable-only argument's own value-demand error ("Property 'Inc' expects 1 parameter, but was called with 0 arguments.") rather than a type mismatch. The implementation is in the same working tree as Q-05, over `abe1383` (not yet committed).

- **Decision recorded:** Q-72 moved from § Decide before release to § Already resolved (the record as posed kept below the decision, its "Current behavior" relabelled "Behavior before the decision"; options (a) and (b) rejected). It has no X, PV or oq source, so no violation or contradiction entry changes. Q-28 (the Decimal128 representative) and Q-01 are untouched.
- **Rules:** PAR-07 states the refusal (rule condition, a "No forwarding into a repeated-name callee" paragraph, examples with "formerly", a Q-72 decision record, C# and Lean evidence, pins); FWD-02 restricts its "behaves like" promise to callees that bind each name once, explains the carve-out in its lifted-parameters paragraph and in why-fundamental, and its Q-72 known-tension bullet is removed; PAR-05 notes that the by-name merge never joins two occurrences of one callee; PAT-09 gains the invariant ("No implicit collapse"). No rule's ID, class or status category changed.
- **Mechanism:** the C# front end refuses a bare lifting-position reference to such a callee with the new front-end error `RepeatedParameterNotForwardable` (`DiagnosticCode` 47, `KatLangErrorCode` 69) at the reference, before the forwarding-source decision, so the refusal is the same at inferring and closed levels and an existing same-named binding never feeds the occurrences (`Q(x) = P` is refused, never `Q(x) = P(x, x)`); the refused reference gives its owner no parameter. Lean specifies the decision (`refusesImplicitForwarding`, `bareValueReferenceTreatment`; laws `repeated_name_callee_is_never_lifted`, `lifted_callee_binds_each_capture_name_once`, `bare_reference_is_read_iff_cacheable`, `refused_bare_reference_was_never_a_value_read`) and evaluates the written forms (`CoreTests/RepeatedNameConstraints.lean` sections 15–16); it models no signature construction.
- **Examples verified:** every new example is a row of `RepeatedNameCalleeLiftingTests` (its 11 former "current behavior" pins rewritten to the decided rule; 237 tests), a canonical spec case (`repeated-name-callee-is-never-forwarded-implicitly`, parse-level; `repeated-name-callee-is-called-explicitly`, Lean-derived), or a `TutorialSemanticContractTests` pin; the headline programs were re-run with the CLI built from the final tree. "Formerly" figures are observations on the Q-05 tree over `abe1383`.
- **Hostile review:** tried to erase a repeated-name constraint through every implicit route — every lifting position, alias of alias, transitive siblings, nested blocks and properties, deconstruction right-hand sides, closed lists and branch patterns (with and without the same name, strict Math positions included), inferred same-named parameters, captured enclosing parameters, nested and three-occurrence shapes, fixed-plus-collecting shapes, module text, host-shared nodes reached neutrally then strictly, the Math and host-operation arms, dot-call receivers, callbacks, planned loop steps, single-clause family flattening, clause families, deconstruction lowering, and Grace — plus a generated family of twelve repeated-name shapes × ten positions (refused; renaming one occurrence apart makes the same program lift) and the positional written wrapper reproducing the direct call on satisfying and violating arguments. Mutation: removing the refusal fails 177 tests, reporting without refusing 10, refusing without reporting 177, and dropping the report dedup 1 (the host-shared node).
- **Lineages:** conditional-algorithms chain G step 6 and implicit-parameter chain G step 13, their surviving semantics, and a superseded-decision entry (the by-name collapse of a callee's repeated name).
- **Counts:** 175 active rules plus 7 retired stubs (unchanged); PV and X entries unchanged; 72 questions, 66 open and 6 resolved.
- **Validation:** V3 on the final candidate (2026-09-29), the Q-05 and Q-72 work together. `validate-all.ps1 -Phase DotNet`: full solution build succeeded with 0 warnings and 0 errors; KatLang.Tests 24,236 of 24,236 (23,971 at the Q-05 validation), KatLang.CLI.Tests 296 of 296 and KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests 133 of 133 passed (the public API baseline records the two new enum members); `git diff --check` clean. `validate-all.ps1 -Phase Lean`: all seven targets built without warnings (CoreTests, KatLangArityLaws, AstDemo, CoreArityAlgebra, CoreArityAlgebraProofs, SemanticExplorerCases, LanguageSpecCases). Both phases left the repository unchanged. At the owner's request the Q-05 and Q-72 focused suites were then rerun explicitly (`RepeatedNameConstraintTests`, `RepeatedNameCalleeLiftingTests`, `PatternBindingErrorPrecedenceTests`, `ArgumentValueOutcomeTests`, and the tutorial contract): 622 of 622, tree unchanged. Logs: `artifacts/validation/q72/`.

## Independent staged Q-05/Q-72 review (2026-09-29)

Reviewed the complete staged candidate on `main` over `abe13834d7c85fd8223d8b444703e234b44fd091` independently. No accepted missing-value/failed-value/callable-only splice or implicit repeated-occurrence collapse was found. A concrete performance regression was repaired: the repeated-value gate rescanned the complete pattern level for every unrelated valueless callable slot. A 258-pattern binder required 66,822 pattern visits. `RepeatsAtLevel` now builds one lazy name index per binding level, shared across prefix and suffix; each top-level pattern contributes a name at most once to the repetition predicate. This preserves Lean's predicate and Q-01's permission to retain an unused failed callable argument.

The review also found an overstatement in PAT-09's explanation: "before every verdict of the level" contradicted PAT-10's already-decided range order. The requirement is checked when its pattern binds; it precedes the verdicts of that range. `P(x, x, *r, f, f) = 0` / `Bad = 1 / 0` / `P(1, 2, Bad, 7)` remains the prefix's arity conflict, while `P(*r, x, x)` binds its suffix before demanding a failed middle. C# and Lean witnesses now pin both boundaries and exact evaluation counts. This corrects the explanatory scope; it does not change the owner's no-splicing decision or D1/PAT-10. First-failure selection within an ordinary repeated-name range remains in argument order. Illegal collector/name collisions were rejected by the parser before runtime; they were not classified as runtime defects.

Strengthened evidence: seeded random failures that would succeed on retry, tick and callback failures, independent effects/identities, 2,048 nested pattern levels with cached repetition detection, legitimate unique valueless inputs beside repeats, explicit source wrappers, rename-apart AST/signature assertions, CLI spans, direct binder visits and allocations. The tests cover sync, async, forced async, generic and optimized execution where applicable. All seven targeted mutations were killed (value requirement, whole refusal, dependency refusal, rewrite refusal, repetition detection, diagnostic deduplication, per-level indexing); source bytes were restored after each mutation.

PAT-09, PAT-10, CALL-06, PAR-05, PAR-07, FWD-02 and Q-05/Q-72 lineages were cross-checked. Q-05, Q-72, X-17 and PV-13 remain resolved. Q-01 and Q-28, PV-08/PV-14/PV-26 and the family callable-identity asymmetry PV-43 remain separate and unchanged. No question or classification was added: 72 question IDs, 66 open, 6 resolved; 44 X entries, 6 resolved and 38 not resolved. The final frozen-tree validation and per-mutation evidence are recorded in `../q05-q72-adversarial-review/review-report.md`.

## Q-72 reversal: implicit forwarding is by binding name (2026-09-29)

The owner reversed the Q-72 decision (option (c), committed with Q-05 as `515ed3c`) the same day in favor of the general rule: *implicit forwarding is by binding name, regardless of how many times that name occurs in a callee's parameter patterns.* A caller owns one binding for a given parameter name; if a callee uses that name in several parameter positions, implicit forwarding supplies the caller's one binding to every occurrence — the rule formulas already follow across callees (`H = F + G` with `F(x)` and `G(x)` is `H(x) = F(x) + G(x)`), now without exception within one callee (`P(x, x) = x` / `Some = P` is `Some(x) = P(x, x)`). Its relation to Q-05, which stands unchanged: *repeated names constrain independently supplied arguments; when implicit forwarding supplies the same caller binding to several occurrences, each occurrence receives that same complete binding, and no synthetic value/callable merge occurs.* Implemented in the working tree over `515ed3c` (not committed).

- **Cleanup map, `515ed3c` against its parent `abe1383`, classified by purpose (no whole-commit revert).** *Q-72 only — removed:* `ImplicitArgumentResolver.RefusesImplicitForwarding` with its gates in `CollectImplicitDepsCore` and `RewriteBareReference`, the report and its per-region dedup memo (`ResolverWalkMemos.TryBeginRepeatedParameterReport`), `CallableSignature.RepeatedParameterName` with its cache and `TemplateFacts.FirstRepeatedName`, the public `DiagnosticCode` 47 / `KatLangErrorCode` 69 members with their mapping (their numbers are left unused; KatLang is pre-release and nothing else used them; no other member is renumbered), the two public-API baseline lines, the `DiagnosticCodeTests`, `KatLangErrorCodeTests` and CLI rows, `RepeatedNameCalleeLiftingTests`, the adversarial signature-detection cost test (its "2,048 nested pattern levels with cached repetition detection" evidence measured the removed query), the parse-level spec case with its runner-inventory line, the Lean `refusesImplicitForwarding` / `BareValueReferenceTreatment` / `bareValueReferenceTreatment` with the four laws `repeated_name_callee_is_never_lifted`, `lifted_callee_binds_each_capture_name_once`, `bare_reference_is_read_iff_cacheable` and `refused_bare_reference_was_never_a_value_read`, the CoreTests decision table, and the refusal prose of AGENTS.md, the rules docs, the binder docs, the alignment manifest, the tutorial and both generator prompts. After the removal the resolver, the signature and template facts, both enums, `KatLangError` and the diagnostic/error-code/CLI tests are byte-identical to `abe1383`. *Q-05 — kept unchanged:* the binder change (`RepeatsAtLevel`, the lazily built per-level repetition index that fixed the quadratic scan, and Lean `ParameterPattern.repeatsAtLevel`), the deleted algorithm-only verdict, the Q-05 laws and CoreTests sections 1–14, `RepeatedNameConstraintTests`, `PatternBindingErrorPrecedenceTests`, the Q-05 adversarial rows (including the linear-visit binder test), the Q-05 spec cases, tutorial example and Semantic Explorer specials, the D1/PAT-10 corrections, and the version bump (0.8.226). *Mixed — edited surgically:* `RepeatedNameAdversarialReviewTests` (the nested-shape test now pins one binding per name; `Alias = P` rows added to the seeded-failure and retained-failure tests), `CoreTests/RepeatedNameConstraints.lean` (sections 15–17 now evaluate the elaborated forwarding trees), `TutorialSemanticContractTests`, the maintained-documentation guards (the three Q-72 patterns forbade the now-correct wording; four new ones forbid the refusal's), the written-wrapper spec case (renamed `repeated-name-wrapper-keeps-independent-arguments`), and the Q-05 paragraphs of the docs that mentioned the refusal.
- **One correction beyond restoring the pre-Q-72 merge.** The by-name merge recorded names only after each whole pattern, so a name repeated INSIDE one group lifted per occurrence (`P((x, x))` gave the caller pattern `(x, x)`). `MissingCapturePattern` now records each kept capture as it walks, so the merge is purely by binding name (`P((x, x))` lifts `(x)`, the existing partial-group shape rule). No other lifted signature changes: across patterns and across dependencies the names were already recorded. The FE-3 single-dependency fast path returns a template unchanged only when its captures are unique, so it can never bypass the merge.
- **Rules:** PAR-05, PAR-07 (rule condition; the by-name paragraph replacing "No forwarding into a repeated-name callee"; examples; the Q-72 decision record rewritten to record the reversal; C#, Lean and test evidence), FWD-02 (lifted parameters, why fundamental) and PAT-09 ("Forwarded occurrences" replacing "No implicit collapse"), in the draft and the summary; a reversal revision note in both, the Q-72 revision note kept as history. No rule's ID, class or status category changed.
- **Questions, lineages, counts:** Q-72 stays under § Already resolved with the reversal recorded (first decision, option (c), reversed in favor of option (a) stated as the general rule); no question, PV or X entry added; 72 questions, 66 open and 6 resolved; 44 X entries, 6 resolved. The repeated-name chain gains step 7 and the implicit-parameter chain step 14; their surviving and historical semantics are updated. Adjacent records are unchanged and none referenced Q-72: Q-01 (the forwarded argument is evaluated once; every occurrence reads that outcome), Q-28 (open for independently supplied arguments; forwarding cannot exhibit it, since every occurrence reads one binding), PV-08 (the `(x)` group lifted from `P((x, x))` is rebuilt as `(x, x)` in the call, so nothing is opened twice), PV-14, PV-26 and PV-43.
- **Hostile review.** Tried to make identical callee binding names produce several caller parameters: every lifting position (alias, operators, comparisons, unary, lists, index target and selector, row spread, strict Math argument and dot-fallback receiver, deconstruction right-hand side, blocks, nested properties, closed list and branch pattern that bind the name, transitive aliases, sibling formulas, loop step, callback), fifteen generated shapes (top level, three occurrences, inside one group, across groups, beside and inside collectors, nested groups) in ten positions, two repeated-name callees in one formula (`H = P + Q2` with `P((x, x))`, `Q2(x, (x, y))` lifts `H((x), (y))`), transitive group aliases, a reused enclosing binding inside a group (Q-04: `G = P3` inside `F(x)` with `P3((x, y), x)` forwards only `(y)`), Grace, dot-call receivers, module text, and an invariant over every clean program of the executable specification — no inferred signature repeats a name. Tried to resurrect the Q-05 chimera through forwarding: `Some = PC` with `PC(x, x) = x, x(5)` and `Some(Inc)` is Inc's own value-demand failure; `Some(Bad)` is Bad's failure, evaluated once (host-call logs); a callable accompanies its own argument's value (`Some(A)` with `A = 5` and `PF(f, f) = f, f()` is `(5, 5)`); a written block argument is one callable identity at both occurrences. The explicit/implicit equivalence (`Alias = P` against the written `Alias(<lifted signature>) = P(<callee patterns>)`) held for every shape on satisfying, traced, failing and callable-only arguments, and every lifting position matched the explicit `P(x, x)` on six routes for satisfying, traced, failing, callable-only, ticking and seeded-random arguments. Removing the refusal widened nothing: neutral positions, opened and structural names, bare root rows and clause families behave as before, and a closed list lacking the name still follows PAR-04 exactly as for a callee with distinct names. No defect was found beyond the within-group merge corrected above.
- **Performance:** the per-signature repetition scan and the per-region report memo are gone; the merge records names in the walk it already makes (no extra traversal of implicit arguments); the Q-05 binder's per-level index is unchanged and `ValuelessUnrelatedCaptures_InspectThePatternLevelOnlyLinearly` still pins linear visits.
- **Pins:** `ImplicitForwardingByBindingNameTests` (293), the rewritten adversarial rows, the spec cases `implicit-forwarding-is-by-binding-name` (Lean-derived) and `repeated-name-wrapper-keeps-independent-arguments`, ten Semantic Explorer specials `forwardByName*` (Lean-derived from the elaborated trees; 2,319 surface cases, 2,277 Lean-representable), CoreTests sections 15–17, the tutorial's § Formulas That Use Formulas and § Equal Arguments with their contract test.
- **Validation:** V3 on the final candidate (2026-09-29). `validate-all.ps1 -Phase DotNet`: full solution build 0 warnings, 0 errors; KatLang.Tests 24,339 of 24,339, KatLang.CLI.Tests 296 of 296, KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests 133 of 133 (the baseline without the two enum members); `git diff --check` clean. `validate-all.ps1 -Phase Lean`: all seven targets built (CoreTests, KatLangArityLaws, AstDemo, CoreArityAlgebra, CoreArityAlgebraProofs, SemanticExplorerCases, LanguageSpecCases; the two regenerated case files compiled fresh). ## PV-05, PV-19 and PV-20: builtin call assembly respects argument roles (2026-09-29)

PV-05, PV-19 and PV-20 — three confirmed violations in the collection-builtin argument adapter — were fixed together on 2026-09-29 without a new decision: CALL-03 already said callback slots are never demanded during assembly, HO-04 named `reduce`'s `initial` a value slot, and SUP-01, SUP-02 and CALL-04 required a spread to supply its items, or fail, before arity. The guiding rule: *builtin call assembly respects argument roles — a demanded value is established once, a callback remains an algorithm until invoked, and a spread either supplies its items or propagates its failure.* The implementation is in the main checkout's working tree over `c719f43` (not yet committed; developed in worktree `g3-div-truncation-fix-fcba23` over `515ed3c`); Lean and C# changed together.

- **Violations resolved:** PV-05, PV-19 and PV-20 moved to § Resolved violations in `open-questions.md`, each with a Resolution block (root cause, C# and Lean change, before/after probes, pins, validation; the commit is a placeholder). Q-06 stays open, narrowed to its part (1) — what a non-callable value in a callback slot should report; its part (2) is answered by CALL-03. X-30 is narrowed to Q-06(1).
- **Rules:** CALL-03 states the role model in its collection-builtin bullet (a VALUE slot is demanded once and its outcome is final; a CALLBACK slot is never value-evaluated during assembly; a SPREAD slot supplies its items or fails the call before arity; a SURPLUS position keeps eager evaluation), with examples, caveats and evidence. SUP-02 (a failed spread in a call), CALL-04 (a failed spread supplies no item), HO-04 (invoking and value slots; its known tension narrowed to the value-to-thunk lift), COLL-02 (the spread and value/control bullets; its known tension), CALL-06 (the builtin-adapter caveat), RUN-03 (no "callback fall-through"; the fused-pipeline evidence), RUN-07 (c) and RUN-08 (the deferral and demand-point lists) cite it, and FORMAL-07's count of proved arity laws is now 202. The PV references were removed from twelve rules: SUP-01, SUP-02 (keeps PV-46), CALL-02, CALL-03, CALL-04, PROP-05, HO-04, COLL-02, LOOP-08 (keeps PV-12), RUN-07, RUN-08 and RAND-01. No rule's ID, class or status category changed.
- **Mechanism:** Lean `SequenceBuiltinSlotRole` / `SequenceBuiltinMetadata.slotRole` and a role-aware `collectSequenceCallableCallItems`: a spread slot's failure propagates at once; a callback item is carried unevaluated; a value item is evaluated once and its outcome retained. `reduce`'s `initial` is registered as a `.value` control, and `evalReduceCounted` receives it as a value. C# mirrors it: `SequenceBuiltinSlotRole` / `SlotRole` (`BuiltinRegistry.cs`), `BuildCallableCallItems(…, metadata)` and its async twin, `UnevaluatedCallItem`, `EvalReduceCounted(…, Result initial, …)`. The fused `PrepareFilterPredicateArgument` evaluates nothing, and `SequencePipelineOptimizer.SourceFailureVerdict` is deleted.
- **Examples verified:** every new or changed example is a row of `BuiltinArgumentRoleTests` (121 cases on six routes: the sync and async engines, the async twin with a suspending host operation, the generic and optimized evaluators, the forced twin; host-call logs compared) or of the canonical spec case `builtin-call-assembly-respects-argument-roles`. A 61-program probe set was run with a five-path runner built from the final tree, and every program AGREES across the paths. "Formerly" figures are observations on `515ed3c` and agree with the audit's at `a2700f4`.
- **Lean/C# correspondence:** 55 new guards in `CoreTests/BuiltinArgumentRoles.lean`, ten slot-role laws in `KatLangArityLaws.lean`, and the regenerated `LanguageSpecCases.lean`. A 28-program Lean/C# differential (Lean programs derived from the C#-elaborated ASTs) agrees 28/28, including the self-referential callbacks (`L = L + 1` / `map([], L)`) that did not terminate in Lean before. Mutation checks: against the pre-repair evaluator 94 of 120 `BuiltinArgumentRoleTests` cases fail, and the other 26 are preserved-behavior controls that pass; against the pre-repair Lean model 25 of the 44 terminating new guards fail.
- **Hostile review:** searched for any remaining retry, eager callback evaluation or phantom spread. Checked: every `EvalArgumentAlgOutputCounted` site (three, all value demands), the loop optimizer (it plans only `if`), the fused filter→count pipeline, dot receivers, the Lean user-call and non-collection spread paths, and the semantic model (independent of the adapter). The metamorphic families (a value slot against its property-bound form, a callback against the zero-iteration loop `repeat(A, 0, 5)`, `B(X*)` against `F(X*)`) agree on six routes. Nothing was found. Intentional laziness is unchanged (the unselected `if` branch and the other lazy slots of the non-collection builtins), and so are the Q-01 to Q-05 decisions.
- **Lineages:** calls-arguments chain H step 7 (with its surviving semantics, probes and a historical-only entry); builtin-surface chain 5 step 13 and its § 5 items 6, 8 and 12 (12, the stale `Evaluator.DotCalls.cs` comment, is resolved); loops chain G step 8; supply/spread chain F step 10.
- **Counts:** 175 active rules plus 7 retired stubs (unchanged); 50 PV entries = 13 active (8 confirmed, 5 likely), 8 resolved violations, 2 entries resolved by decision (PV-01, PV-13), 25 intentional exceptions and 2 refuted; 72 questions, 66 open and 6 resolved (unchanged; Q-06 narrowed); 44 X entries, 6 resolved and 38 not resolved (unchanged; X-30 narrowed).
- **Validation:** V3 on the candidate over `515ed3c` (2026-09-29). `validate-all.ps1 -Phase DotNet`: full solution build with 0 warnings and 0 errors; KatLang.Tests 24,418 of 24,418, KatLang.CLI.Tests 298 of 298 and KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests 133 of 133 passed; `git diff --check` clean. `validate-all.ps1 -Phase Lean`: all seven targets built without warnings. Both phases left the repository unchanged. Logs: `artifacts/validation/pv-adapter/` in the worktree. After the move onto `c719f43`, V3 was repeated on the combined tree: full solution build with 0 warnings and 0 errors; KatLang.Tests 24,507 of 24,507, KatLang.CLI.Tests 296 of 296 and KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests 133 of 133 passed; `git diff --check` clean; all seven Lean targets built without warnings. Both phases left the tree unchanged, and the main checkout's working tree was proved identical to it (same HEAD, diff and untracked-file hashes). ## PV-08: implicit forwarding preserves the exact item supply (2026-09-29)

The reviewed checkout is `main` at `bb0e543` (the PV-11 / PV-24 / PV-25 / PV-49 repair, now committed; the working tree was clean at the start). This task's changes are unstaged and uncommitted.

- **Scope:** one confirmed violation with an existing oracle (FWD-02, PAR-07, values-stay-values), fixed without a new decision and without a new constitutional rule. FWD-02 — the owner of implicit forwarding and of the source binding's kind — is AMENDED so that it forbids PV-08 directly: implicit forwarding preserves the exact structural item supply its bindings stand for; each fixed binding contributes one item and each re-spread collecting binding its collected items; reconstruction preserves cardinality, zero and one item included, and never opens a matched item again. The amendment states semantics, not the carrier; the C# list carrier is recorded as implementation evidence.
- **Root cause (C# front end):** `ImplicitArgumentResolver.BuildPatternArgument` rebuilt a callee's sequence-value group as a normalizing `Capture`; a one-item capture is its item (`CombineOutputSlots`), so the callee's group opened a value the forwarding pattern had matched as ONE item. Lean evaluated the same wrong tree, so the differential could not see it (FORMAL-02's front-end-mistake caveat).
- **Repair:** the group is rebuilt as an exact `ListLiteral` over the same item slots; a list keeps its cardinality and the user binder opens it one level. No front-end stage synthesizes a capture any more, so FORMAL-08's clause (e) holds for every elaborated tree. No syntax, no change to `(…)` patterns, no list/sequence conversion; VAL-03, SEQ-01, SEQ-03 and SUP-06 are unchanged. Lean: no semantic edit; the two hand-encoded forwarded trees in `CoreTests/RepeatedNameConstraints.lean` now carry the exact carrier, and the derived artifacts were regenerated (one new spec case; three explorer specials change only their encoded carrier).
- **Collateral routes (same mechanism, now consistent):** a closed list's own binding (`G(x) = S` / `G([7])`: `7` → `[7]`), a reused enclosing binding (Q-04's nested helper `F([[7]])`: `[7]` → `[[7]]`) and a by-shape collecting stream (`Use([1, 2])`: `[1, 2]` → `[[1, 2]]`). At `bb0e543` the same reused binding reached a two-capture group whole but was opened by a one-capture group, so the re-baseline's "Q-04 fixed the nested-helper route" was partial. Q-04's choice of the source binding is unchanged.
- **Re-baseline:** the post-Q1–Q5 manifest (201 probes, five paths) was re-run on `bb0e543` and on the final tree. Exactly the eight PV-08 witnesses (`p1`, `c13`, `b1`, `c1`, `c5`, `f1`, `f2`, `f3`) change; the other 193 are byte-identical, the live PV-07, PV-12, PV-14, PV-15, PV-21, PV-26, PV-46 and PV-50 witnesses among them, with the same five route/headroom disagreements. A 324-pair alias-versus-direct differential (12 callees × 27 values, alias and list lift): 42 mismatches at `bb0e543`, none after.
- **Constitution:** FWD-02 (retitled, same ID, class and status: rule text, lifted parameters, forwarding by shape, why fundamental, examples, boundary, evidence); PAR-07 (one cross-reference sentence to FWD-02, nothing else); PAR-05's pattern-shape note; FORMAL-02's front-end-mistake caveat; FORMAL-08's clause (e) and its formerly UNCERTAIN caveat. PV-08 is removed from PAR-07, FWD-02, FORMAL-02 and FORMAL-08 and moved to `open-questions.md` § Resolved violations; the summary mirrors the rule-text changes; `decision-lineages.md` gains Chain G step 15 of the parameter-inference lineage. `issue-ledger.md` is unchanged.
- **Counts:** 175 active rules plus 7 retired stubs (unchanged: no ID, class or status category changed). 50 PV entries = 8 active (3 confirmed, 5 likely), 13 resolved violations, 2 resolved by decision, 25 intentional exceptions and 2 refuted. Questions and X entries are unchanged.
- **Validation (V3):** the full .NET phase on the candidate — build with 0 warnings, PublicApi 133/133, CLI 296/296, KatLang.Tests 24,802/24,812; the 10 failures were one theory (`ConditionalBranchElaborationTests.HostBranch_MixedBinderProjection_MatchesExplicitForwarding`) pinning the Lean encoding of the old capture carrier, updated and re-run 14/14 — then all seven Lean targets on the final tree; both phases left the repository unchanged. ## The structural-pattern model: decision, implementation and Constitution synchronization (2026-09-29)

The reviewed checkout is `main` at `bb0e543` with the uncommitted PV-08 work of the previous entry in the working tree; that work was modified in place. This task's changes are unstaged and uncommitted.

- **Decision (the owner's):** *structural pattern delimiters select the value kind they destructure.* `(p1, …, pn)` matches sequence values only and `[p1, …, pn]` list values only; neither structurally matches the other kind, neither treats a scalar as a one-item structure, and a bare pattern binds the whole value. A sequence pattern with exactly one non-collecting item is invalid (KatLang has no one-item sequence value); `(*xs)` stays valid and sequence-only; `[x]` is the one-element pattern and `[*xs]` covers every list cardinality; ordinary patterns and clause families follow the same law (TypeMismatch/ArityMismatch in an ordinary binder, no match in a family). Forwarding: existing bindings forward unchanged, a copied structural pattern is rebuilt as its own kind, and no forwarding step converts between kinds.
- **Implementation (Lean and C# together):** Lean `ParameterPattern.listValue`, `Pattern.listValue`, `Result.sequencePatternItems?` / `listPatternItems?`, `structuralPatternKindMismatch`, the singleton predicates and `validateExplicitParamOutputInvariant`'s singleton check, kind-aware duplicate detection and single-clause classification, and the new `ParameterPattern.unpacking` receiver for assignment deconstruction (one node shared by every target; behavior unchanged, a right-hand side without output stays MissingOutput). C#: the matching records (`ListValueParameterPattern`, `Pattern.ListValue`, `UnpackingParameterPattern`, `ListValueParameterBindingContext`, binding-plan nodes), the parser's `[…]` pattern and `SingletonSequencePattern` diagnostic (code 48; `KatLangErrorCode` 70), the pre-evaluation violation, the binders, family matchers, forwarding (`BuildPatternArgument` / `BuildSequenceArgument`, the group merge), editor tooling and the Lean encoder. PV-08's universal exact-list carrier is gone.
- **Deconstruction audit:** assignment deconstruction is a distinct construct (the unpacking receiver) and keeps opening a lone sequence OR list; no bracketed deconstruction target was added for symmetry.
- **Constitution:** PAT-06 (retitled; the ONE owner of the kind law and the singleton rule; class + SYNTAX / GROUPING RULE; status → Current), PAT-08, SEQ-03 and FWD-02 (retitled), plus PAT-01, the PAT intro and lists, SEQ-01, VAL-03, VAR-05, PAR-05, PAR-07, CALL-06, LOOP-04, SYN-05, SYN-06, FORMAL-02 and FORMAL-08 (new clauses (n), (o)). The summary mirrors the rule texts.
- **Open questions:** PV-08's record is marked SUPERSEDED BY DECISION with its history kept; Q-20 and PV-44 are resolved by decision; X-22 is narrowed to Q-21/Q-22; PV-14, PV-21, PV-26, Q-11, Q-12, Q-13 re-baselined (identical results with a CLI built from `bb0e543` via `git archive` and from the final tree); Q-27 gains a data point; the new Q-73 records a pre-existing boundary (an alias of a callee whose structural groups bind no name changes arity).
- **Counts:** 175 active rules plus 7 retired stubs (no ID added or retired); SYNTAX / GROUPING RULE 31 → 32; "Current, not C#-only" 95 → 96 and "(open question)" 34 → 33 (PAT-06). Active PV entries unchanged at 8.
- **Validation (V3, fresh on the final candidate):** `validate-all.ps1 -Phase DotNet` — full solution build 0 warnings / 0 errors; KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests 133/133, KatLang.CLI.Tests 296/296, KatLang.Tests 25,655/25,655 (no skips; the six-route structural-pattern classes, the regenerated-artifact equality tests and the editor model included); then `-Phase Lean` — all seven targets rebuilt from source (CoreTests, KatLangArityLaws, AstDemo, CoreArityAlgebra, CoreArityAlgebraProofs, SemanticExplorerCases, LanguageSpecCases). Both phases left the repository unchanged. Logs: `../structural-patterns/v3-dotnet.log`, `../structural-patterns/v3-lean.log`.

## The alias and wrapper rules: decision, implementation and Constitution synchronization (2026-09-29)

- **Decision (the owner's specification "Finalize KatLang alias, forwarding, and parameter-inference semantics"):** `A = F` aliases F exactly; `A(params) = F` calls F using A's own parameter supply; `A = F(exprs)` infers A's parameters from the free names written in `exprs`, then executes exactly `F(exprs)`; `A(params) = F(exprs)` is an explicit ordinary call. Forwarding never inspects or reconstructs the destination callable's internal parameter patterns by matching binder names; explicit call expressions are authoritative. The structural-pattern model is unchanged.
- **Implementation:** C# front end — `ImplicitArgumentResolver.TryCompleteLoneCalleeRow` completes a non-root body whose ONE written row is a bare reference to a callable that declares parameters, before the closed-list gate and formula lifting (an open body inherits the callee's patterns verbatim; a written list or clause head forwards its own supply, `BuildSourceArguments` / `BuildBranchSourceArguments`); `Algorithm.User.InheritsCalleeSignature` exempts an alias's inherited names from the collision check; `FrontEndRegionKeys.ForwardedBranchHead` keys a wrapper branch body by its head, literals by exact value (the literal-blind closed specification stays the key of every other branch body, keeping FE-1's single region for wide shared bodies). Lean — surface support beside `liftsBareValueReference` (`aliasesLoneBareReference`, `sequenceArgument`, `ParameterPattern.sourceArgument(s)`, `Pattern.sourceArgument(s)`, `Pattern.headSourceArguments`, `sourceCall`), ten laws in `KatLangArityLaws.lean`, `CoreTests/AliasForwarding.lean`, and `CoreTests/RepeatedNameConstraints.lean` sections 15–18 rewritten (exact aliases beside by-name formulas).
- **Decisions taken while implementing (reported):** declarations and opens beside the lone row are allowed (`A = { local ⏎ F }` is an alias); Q-03's alias clause is superseded (the specification says a bare alias preserves F's signature, a collecting-only F included); an alias's inherited names are encapsulated (no `ParameterPropertyCollision` with a property of the alias or a body nested in it). Non-transparencies of an alias, all reported: its own evaluation frame and signature display in diagnostics, its own callable identity for repeated-name identity checks, and its own zero-argument cache entry. Not alias or wrapper targets (unchanged): clause families (PV-14, Q-13), opened members, dot-reached members, callable parameters, non-Math builtins and host operations (PV-26, Q-12). The composite exception is open question Q-74. A wrapper branch forwards its literals as written (`G(0) = F` passes `0` for a matched `0.0`, exactly as the explicit `G(0) = F(0)`; a C#-only Decimal128 residue).
- **Constitution:** FWD-02 rewritten (new title; the ONE owner of the alias, wrapper and written-call rules; its rule text adopts the specification's normative core; examples re-run: `ex-FWD02.kat` is the `Single`/`Alias`/`Wrapper`/`Explicit`/`Construct` program). PAR-07 retitled ("In a formula, …"; lone rows excluded; by-name forwarding stated for formulas only; examples converted to formulas and the lone-row results stated beside them). PAR-04 retitled ("… and forwarding never adds a parameter to it"; the wrapper beside by-name formula forwarding). PAR-05 (an exact alias infers nothing; pattern-shape examples converted to formulas), PAT-09 (forwarded occurrences), the OP and COLL builtin-alias tensions, and the CALL and VAR open-question lists (the Q-03 alias clause and the closed-list `[]` question superseded for lone rows; the composite exception added). Two sentences left stale by the structural-pattern revision were corrected in PAR-07 (the lift lists of `P(x, (x, a))` and `P((x, x))`; `G((1, 2))` is the `+` TypeMismatch, not `ArityMismatch`). The FWD-02, PAR-04 and PAR-07 index rows carry the new titles; no rule ID, class or status category changes, so the Summary counts are unchanged.
- **Open questions:** PV-08 gains its fourth stage (the four stages of one witness family: capture carrier, exact-list carrier, kind-preserving reconstruction, alias and wrapper); Q-73 resolved; Q-74 added; the question inventory recounted (74 IDs, 66 open, 8 resolved — also correcting the structural-pattern decision's unrecorded Q-20/Q-73 change); PV-03, PV-14, PV-21, PV-26, Q-04, Q-11, Q-12, Q-13 and Q-14 re-baselined, and the Q-72 and Q-03 records marked superseded in part. The earlier reviews that preserved alias-signature deduplication (the Q-72 reversal, its independent staged review, and PV-08's independent final review) are marked superseded where they appear, their text kept.
- **Decision lineages:** parameter-inference Chain G step 17, its surviving-semantics and historical lists, Chain H step 6, the repeated-name Chain G step 8, both "superseded decisions" lists, and the unresolved list (item 11, the composite exception).
- **Probes:** every example added or changed was run with the CLI built from the final tree, and every "formerly" result with a CLI built from `bb0e543` (exported with `git archive`) unless a program needs list or binderless patterns, which `bb0e543` does not parse — those "formerly" results are the ones the replaced tests pinned on the pre-alias structural-pattern tree. Files: `../alias-forwarding/probes/`.
- **Hostile review:** `AliasAndWrapperHostileReviewTests` (53 cases, six routes) attacked the three laws with inherited names beside same-named properties and enclosing parameters, Grace- and dot-ordered callee signatures, a public alias of a private sibling, redundant grouping, repeated and binderless clause heads, callbacks, dot calls, effects and the bare cache. No law was falsified. It surfaced one alignment gap, now closed: Lean's collision model (`conflictingOwnedNames`) did not state the C# exemption of an alias's inherited names (`OwnerLevel` doc, two conformance rows), and one boundary, now documented and pinned: a deconstruction beside the bare callee makes the body a formula.
- **Validation (V3, fresh on the final candidate):** `validate-all.ps1 -Phase DotNet` — full solution build 0 warnings / 0 errors; KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests 133/133, KatLang.CLI.Tests 296/296, KatLang.Tests 26,038/26,038 (no skips; the six-route alias, wrapper and hostile-review classes, the regenerated-artifact equality tests and the editor model included); `git diff --check` clean; then `-Phase Lean` — all seven targets built from source with no warnings (CoreTests, KatLangArityLaws, AstDemo, CoreArityAlgebra, CoreArityAlgebraProofs, SemanticExplorerCases, LanguageSpecCases). ## Bare forwarding by name: decision, implementation and Constitution synchronization (2026-09-30)

- **Decision (the owner's specification "Finalize KatLang alias, by-name forwarding, and formula-lifting semantics"):** `A = F` is an exact alias; `A(p) = F` reuses an existing compatible binding named `p` — no renaming, no inference; `A(p) = F(p)` is an explicit ordinary call, caller and callee names need not match; `A = F(p)` infers the written `p`; `A = F + 1` is formula lifting. It supersedes the previous day's positional reading (`A(p) = F` ≡ `A(p) = F(p)`). The structural-pattern model and the exact alias are unchanged.
- **Implementation:** C# — `ImplicitArgumentResolver.TryCompleteLoneCalleeRow` keeps the alias and replaces the positional reconstruction by `BareForwardingSources` (a closed body's top-level capture kinds, literal-free top-level structural contracts, and every bound name; a branch head's literal-free items) and `BareForwardingArgument` (a capture from the same-named own top-level capture, else the enclosing Q-04 binding; a structural pattern only from an own pattern with the same `DisplayName` contract; a collector optional); an unsupplied parameter is `DiagnosticCode.UnforwardableParameter` (49) / `KatLangErrorCode.UnforwardableParameter` (71) at the bare reference, replayed per family through the generalized `BranchDiagnosticTemplate`; a lone-row branch body's region is keyed by `FrontEndRegionKeys.BareForwardingBranchHead` (literal POSITIONS, value-blind). Lean — `ParameterPattern.sameContract(s)`, `BareForwardingVerdict`, `bareForwardedBinding`, `bareForwardingArgument(s)`, `bareForwardingRow`, `Pattern.bareForwardingOwn`; seventeen `bare_forwarding_*` laws and `formula_lifting_infers_what_bare_forwarding_rejects` (with four private `ParameterKind` equality simp lemmas); `Pattern.sourceArgument(s)`, `Pattern.headSourceArguments` and `wrapper_branch_forwards_its_literals_as_written` removed; `CoreTests/AliasForwarding.lean` rewritten (68 guards).
- **Decisions taken while implementing (reported):** a structural destination is matched only against the body's OWN top-level patterns, never against enclosing bindings (plain named values); a name bound only inside one of the body's patterns is an element, so a whole-value callee parameter of that name is unforwardable; cross-kind capture pairs follow the settled FWD-01 source-kind rule (a fixed source reaches a collector as one item, a collected list reaches a fixed parameter whole); repeated callee names follow the by-name rule (`Q(x) = P` with `P(x, x)` is `P(x, x)`); a Math function forwards by its registry names (`sqrt(x)`, `pow(x, y)`, `randomInt(start, end)`); a clause family stays no target (PV-14). Reported conflict, minimized: the established by-shape stream forwarding now serves formulas only (`Use(*items) = C` with `C((*xs))` is `UnforwardableParameter`; `Use(*items) = [C]:0` still forwards by shape). Reported cascade: a rejected open whose unprovided name a nested body infers makes the enclosing closed lone row `UnforwardableParameter` too (`StaticOpenOwnershipTests`, the closed-list consequence the test already allowed as `UndeclaredIdentifier`).
- **Constitution:** FWD-02 rewritten (retitled; the six-form table, the contributor rule, the canonical examples A–J, the structural acceptance cases, collectors, repeated names, nested scopes, closed formulas, the renaming properties, the history of five stages). PAR-04 (the closed-list law; canonical rejections; the closed formula), PAR-05 (a destination's order never reorders an explicit list), PAR-07 (formula-oriented; lone rows are FWD-02's), PAT-09 (bare forwarding is by name), the FWD open questions (the composite exception resolved; the `UseFixedOther` note revised), and FORMAL-07 (recounted).
- **Open questions:** Q-74 resolved (moved with its original record); the inventory recounted (74 IDs, 65 open, 9 resolved); PV-08's final stage with the owner's six-stage numbering; PV-03, PV-14, PV-21, PV-26, Q-03, Q-04, Q-11, Q-12, Q-13, Q-14 and Q-72 re-baselined (`../bare-forwarding/probes/rebaseline-final.out`: 36 witnesses, 32 unchanged, four lone-row changes by decision).
- **Decision lineages:** the repeated-name Chain G step 9, the parameter-inference Chain G step 18, the surviving-semantics list, the superseded-decisions list, and the unresolved item 11 (resolved).
- **Hostile review (§50):** the invariants — alias transparency, no renaming, explicit-list closure, structural compatibility, explicit call authority, formula lifting, the alias/formula distinction — were attacked by the by-name matrix (13 target x 13 source signatures against a test-side oracle), the renaming tests, closed formulas, nested scopes, clause branches with a shared value-blind region, collectors of both kinds, Math targets, effects and caching (trace, tick, seeded random draws, a random property, cached and fresh reads, nested scopes), module text and the editor. ### Model-C result-boundary hostile review — 2026-10-02

Results carry no callable variant in either model, CALLABLE projection exists only for written references, and every call, selection, collection, capture, spread result and loop state is VALUE-demanded data. ### Q-71 resolution / PV-50 historical reconciliation — 2026-10-02

Source-size and acquisition limits are host admission policy, applied when the language's source-processing contract requires a source unit, before its contents are used. A deferred unit is processed only when an operation requires its compilation, including required identity information; refusal stops that operation and its run, while an undemanded deferred computation performs no source work.

The owner accepted the focused investigation. Added 88 permanent cases and ran them before editing production: all pass on unchanged runtime. PV-50's pre-Model-C `711 → 209` remains dated historical evidence; current seed-3 replay is `209 → 209`, zero fetches/materialization attempts. Q-71 is RESOLVED by decision; PV-50 is a resolved violation superseded by Model C. RUN-03 distinguishes reached source refusal from evaluator-resource classification; MOD-10 retains whole selected units/static imports; MOD-11 retains ParseFailure/EvalFailure phases. The identical draft/summary source-information annex clarifies NEED-03/06/09 without rewriting NEED laws. 79 Q IDs: 18 resolved, 1 superseded, 2 partial, 58 fully open (60 with an open part; 25 before release). PV: 50 total, 4 open, 0 active settled violations, 0 partial, 18 resolved violations, 5 resolved by decision, 21 intentional, 2 refuted. X: 47 total, 18 resolved, 0 partial, 0 still-reproducing settled defects, 1 superseded, 28 open. Q-71 leaves § Decide before release. Q-61 and unrelated statuses/history stay unchanged. Validation and fingerprints: `../q71-resolution/final-report.md`, `../q71-resolution/evidence/constitution-check.json`.

## Q-09 resolution and implementation (2026-10-04)

The owner decided Q-09 by splitting it, and the working tree over `main` @ `e2eb0d7` implements the decision. ## Q-23 resolution and implementation (2026-10-04)

The owner decided Q-23 (option Q23-B of `../q23-investigation/final-report.md`): a loop step is an ordinary callable invoked over the current state supply. Any callable is eligible as a `while`/`repeat` step; when an iteration executes, the current state slots are supplied to it through KatLang's ordinary invocation semantics, and loops specialize only the iteration protocol and the row receiver. The working tree over `main` @ `f6d978c` implements it in Lean and C#: one step invocation (Lean `runNeedStepSlots`, C# `InvokeLoopStepSupply`) dispatches a user algorithm through its binder, a clause family through the ordinary family dispatcher (`selectNeedFamilyBranch` / `BindNeedFamilySupply`), a builtin through its argument roles (`applyBuiltinCountedResolved` / `ApplyBuiltinCountedResolved`) and an alias as its target; a builtin's ONE result value is ONE next-state row. PV-12 is resolved; X-23 (resolved earlier the same day for its wording) records the decision. Rule edits: LOOP-08 replaced (title; class gains EVALUATION RULE; status; rule; examples; tension; evidence), LOOP-02 (title, status, rule), LOOP-01 (title; rule: the Model-C cleanup — initial slots are supplied, not demanded), LOOP-03 and LOOP-05 (one rule bullet each), PAT-11 (status and rule bullet), rule texts identical in the draft and the summary; HO-02 (caveat), RES-06 and COLL-01 (evidence; COLL-01's loop-step tension resolved, and its stale alias half recorded with a dated note), SYN-05 (the PV-12 reference). Every example stated in the reconciled rules was re-run on six routes against the implementation and on `f6d978c` (`../q23-implementation/probes/q23-constitution*.cases`). Counts: 79 Q IDs (28 resolved, 48 fully open, 13 before release); PV 1 open, 21 resolved violations; X 25 resolved, 22 open; 174 active rules (status 43/3/103/25). Evidence: `../q23-implementation/final-report.md`, `../q23-implementation/evidence/constitution-check.json`.

## Q-06 + Q-27 resolution and implementation (2026-10-05)

The owner decided Q-06 (option (a)) and Q-27 (policy P2 of `../q27-q06-error-policy/final-report.md`) together as one evaluation-error taxonomy: an evaluation error names the first semantic contract the executed operation cannot satisfy, in that operation's ordinary evaluation order. Missing CALLABLE capability is `NotAnAlgorithm` in every invoking position (raised by CALLABLE projection only when an invocation is required; VALUE is never demanded to diagnose it, and an unused invoking slot is never projected); a present VALUE of the wrong kind is `TypeMismatch`; supply/output cardinality is `ArityMismatch`; a selection naming no position is `BadIndex` (`first`/`last` included, so the selection laws are total); a zero divisor is `DivisionByZero` and every other valid-kind domain failure `IllegalInEval`; NEED-04's repeated-name categories are an explicit frozen binder rule. The paired Lean/C# implementation over `main` @ `927a8c4` changes the same functions in both languages (one invoking-slot projection, `projectInvokingSlot` / `ProjectInvokingSlot`; `expectInt`; unary minus; the collection-element check; the whole-number control; the empty policy as metadata, `requireSelectablePosition`), restates the laws `first_empty_is_badIndex` / `last_empty_is_badIndex` and repairs X-11's guard with a real empty collection. Q-06 and Q-27 are RESOLVED BY DECISION; X-11, X-30 and X-31 are RESOLVED; PV-30 and PV-34 are RESOLVED BY DECISION; oq EFF-03 and ERR-01 are closed. ## Q-17 + Q-18 + Q-75 resolution and implementation (2026-10-05)

The owner decided Q-17 (S-C), Q-18 (C-B3) and Q-75 (F-A) of `../q17-q18-q75-dot-semantics/final-report.md` together as one identity-oriented dot semantics. The route: an ordinary non-spread edge `R.n(args?)` selects a declared structural member of any name — `string` included — with accessibility checked after selection, a branch-only member is `LocalOnlyProperty`, and on a structural miss the edge is the `.string` intrinsic for `string` and the extension fallback `n(R, args)` otherwise; a lexical `string` is never consulted by `.string`. The identity: a dot expression has one callable identity in every algorithm-capable position, callee included — an argumentless structural path is its member (aliases normalized), every other dot expression a value; parentheses never change identity, and a call follows only a name, a graced name, an argumentless dot edge or a redundant group of one. The static obligation: under a closed explicit list or in a branch body a statically certain (`Always`) fallback name is checked like the written callee name, at the member token with the G-24 receiver-aware wording; a MAY-selected one is not; inferring bodies are unchanged. Implementation over `main` @ `ad3d7dd`: S-C — Lean `evalDotCallCounted` decides the route from ONE `lookupPropDefAny?` (the intrinsic only in its `none` arm), and the `string` short-circuits are gone from `resolveDotReceiver`, `projectNeedStructuralMember`, `Expr.staticAliasPath?` and `evalDotStringReceiverValue`; ## Q-19 + Q-29 + Q-31 + Q-32 resolution and implementation (2026-10-06)

The owner decided Q-19 (D-I), Q-29 (A-U), Q-31 (H-P) and Q-32 (I-U) of `../q19-q29-q31-q32-open-module-identity/final-report.md` together as one law system for `open` and loaded modules. Open providers: an `open` list counts the providers its targets resolve to by the one callable identity — the declaration in its declaring scope — never by spelling or position. Static ambiguity: provider overlap is legal, and a WRITTEN name that reaches an open level with two or more different providers is the front-end `AmbiguousOpen` at the occurrence, regardless of demand; a dot fallback name counts only when certain (Q-75 MUST); a receiver-decided fallback and a host-built tree keep the run-time error. Module hygiene: a loaded module is a source unit rooted at the prelude whatever holds it; its free names never see the load site, a name it cannot resolve is its member's implicit parameter, and a site parameter reaches a member only by an argument, bare forwarding or formula lifting. Module identity: one canonical URL is ONE module declaration per compiled program — one member binding, one callable identity, one open provider — while holders stay bindings of their own and distinct URLs stay distinct. Implementation over `main` @ `4960e80`: D-I — the evaluators resolve each target and drop one whose identity equals an earlier one (C# `Evaluator.ResolveAllOpens` with `SameRepeatedCallableIdentity`, Lean `resolveAllOpens` with `sameRepeatedCallableIdentity`; an unidentified hand-built Lean declaration counts as its own provider); the front end computes the same identity statically once (`OpenProviderIdentity`: the provider's declaration and its declaring owners to the root level, a written block and a loaded module declared at the root level), read by elaborated lookup (`ElaboratedScopeLookup.ResolveOpenProvider` / `GetResolvedOpenProviders`, and with it detection, the static ambiguity check and the editor), exposure settlement (`OpenLevelSettlement`), the summary channel (`ResolvedOpenCandidate`, `DistinctProviders`) and the resolver (`OpenProvider.ResolveProvider`); `Evaluator.OpenTargetDedupKey` remains only a pre-resolution optimization; A-U — one new public `DiagnosticCode.AmbiguousOpen` (value 52) mapped onto the existing `KatLangErrorCode.AmbiguousOpen`, reported by `ParameterDetector.ReportAmbiguousOpenUse` over `ElaboratedScopeLookup.FindAmbiguousOpenProviders` in the rewrite walk over the rows a body writes, at the occurrence; a dot edge's fallback name is a use only when `LexicalFallbackSelectionOf` is `Always` (member token); the message names the providers as written and the two repairs; Lean models no front-end rule (FORMAL-02); H-P + I-U — every front-end pass processes a loaded-module root through a module arm, prelude-rooted and once per operation (`ParameterDetector.ProcessModuleRoot`, `ImplicitArgumentResolver.ProcessModuleRoot`, `PropertyExposureResolver.ProcessModuleRoot`, the collision and open-provider validators, the provenance finalizer; `ModuleUnits`), error-free operations publish their units to the document loader's `ModuleUnitRegistry` so a deferred materialization reaches the same declaration, `ElaboratedPropertyScope` roots a module level at the chain root, the summary channel settles a module at the prelude and the sibling channel reads nothing in it, provider identities and the static alias chase restart at a module; at run time `Evaluator.WithParent` wires a module root under the chain's root level (Lean `Algorithm.withParent` over the new `PropertyIdentity.module`, kept by `identifyPropertyAlgorithm` and emitted by `LeanAstEncoder` for every C# module root; `staticAliasStep?` restarts its chain at a module). Q-19, Q-29, Q-31 and Q-32 are RESOLVED BY DECISION; X-20, X-26 and X-37 are RESOLVED (X-03 unchanged); PV-41 and PV-42 are RESOLVED BY DECISION; oq NAME-01, NAME-02, MOD-01 and MOD-02 are closed. Rule edits: rule texts, identical in the draft and the summary — RES-03 (providers by identity; where ambiguity is reported), RES-04 (a written ambiguous use), MOD-05 (only its pointer to RES: providers count once per identity), MOD-06 (title and rule: every loaded module is rooted at the prelude), MOD-07 (splicing; the holder binding), MOD-08 (replaced: a module has hygiene of its own; the former section kept as dated history), MOD-09 (replaced: one declaration per canonical URL per compiled program; the former section kept), MOD-10 (a materialized module is the same declaration) and PROP-07 (one binding per module member); statuses — RES-03, MOD-08 and PROP-07 become Current, MOD-09 Current (C#-only by design); caveats, tensions and evidence — RES-03, RES-04, RES-06, RES-07, PAR-03, PROP-07, PROP-08, RAND-01, and the RES and MOD open-question lists; current-state consistency — the MOD topic introduction, RES's non-constitutional dedup-key bullet, PROP's module-identity open question, the implementation-inventory row K01, and PV-47's refuted record (its static half superseded by A-U). ## Independent post-commit law-M review and repair (2026-10-06)

The independent review of committed 304aff2 found two C# defects under the existing laws: imported callable-alias module roots lost the validation anchor, and nested qualified-provider summary paths could recursively re-charge an intermediate owner. The latter stack overflow also reproduced on the true pre-M parent 4960e80. ImportSite now covers both module-root variants; member summaries expand through their declaring ancestors once, and repeated local-path equations close with the declaring algorithm, summary-map snapshot and bound-owner exclusions retained. Excluding a module owner still settles its member at the prelude. No Lean evaluation law, syntax, public API or owner decision changes.

New two-level capturing-provider sources refute the earlier M35 and M33x equivalence conclusions: the mutated settlement loses the p capture and caches 5 for both Outer(5) and Outer(6). Permanent regressions pin value, exposure, requirement ownership, open/declaration permutations, callable-name/MUST ambiguity and direct/nested module identity. M02c and M33 are re-challenged independently; their final dispositions, compatibility, derived-AST Lean checks, frozen serial V3 and isolated mutation evidence are in `../q19-q29-q31-q32-codex-review/final-report.md`.

This is implementation and evidence reconciliation, not a new semantic decision. Q-19 D-I, Q-29 A-U, Q-31 H-P and Q-32 I-U remain resolved. All Q/PV/X statuses, rule texts, historical issue dispositions and counts remain unchanged; the four pre-release questions remain Q-10, Q-16, Q-28 and Q-33. Earlier implementation results remain historical evidence for their original corpus.

## Q-28 + Q-10 resolution and implementation (2026-10-07)

The owner decided Q-28 (N-F) and Q-10 (D-F); the two decisions are independent. Q-28: the investigation `../q28-q10-numeric-identity-display/final-report.md` recommended N-F and the owner adopted it — `==` (`Decimal128.Equals` at numeric leaves, recursively structural) is the language's one "same value" relation for `==`/`!=`, `contains`, `distinct`, numeric literal patterns, duplicate clause heads and repeated-name compatibility; a number's Decimal128 representation is data the value carries, never provenance, preserved by value-carrying operations and observed by `.string`; a repeated name keeps one richest compatible complete contribution and, among equally rich ones, the first in written traversal order, representation included; permutation preserves the verdict, error kind, callable identity and the value up to `==`, and may change only that representation. It is the existing C# and Lean behavior, so the implementation is tests, C#-only spec cases and documentation only; the new rule VAL-11 states the value/representation/presentation layers. Q-10: the investigation recommended D-O (no source hook); after a product-portability discussion the owner rejected D-O and adopted D-F — every active `DisplayDecimals` setting is a presentation filter, the root's own property (source) and `RunOptions.DefaultDisplayDecimals` / CLI `--display-decimals` (host) compose by their minimum, absent filters impose no limit, and neither present is canonical. The source filter stays an engine-level convention read after successful output in the same run whatever the host filter is; the raw evaluator never reads it, an intentional boundary (X-35 resolved by decision, not "disappeared"). The one production change is `KatLangEngine.CombineDisplayDecimals`.

Rule texts changed identically in the draft and the summary: VAL-11 (new), PAT-05, PAT-09, CMP-05 (with its title), COLL-06, RUN-01 (exception 3), RUN-06, RES-06 and the NEED-04 annex wording; statuses, boundaries, tensions, evidence and topic tails elsewhere (VAL-09, PAT-10, COLL-07, OP-13, OP-14, FORMAL-04; the VAL, PAT, BOOLCMP, OP, COLL, RES and RUN lists; SYN, PROP, COVERAGE). RUN-02 and RAND-01 are unchanged. Q-28 and Q-10 RESOLVED BY DECISION; X-28 and X-12 RESOLVED; X-35 RESOLVED BY DECISION; oq NUM-01, FAM-09 and RUN-03 closed; § Decide before release 4 → 2 (Q-16, Q-33). Mechanically checked: `../q28-q10-nf-df-implementation/evidence/constitution/constitution-check.json`.

## Independent Q-28/Q-10 hostile review — 2026-10-07

> **Independent hostile review, 2026-10-07: NOT READY.** Q-28's numeric equality and richest/first compatible representative decision (N-F), and Q-10's minimum display-filter decision (D-F), remain adopted. The blanket promise that permuting incompatible repeated-name contributions preserves the error category conflicts with NEED-04's immediate first-conflict rule. With `A = 1.0`, `B = 1.00`, `P(x,x,x) = x`, `P(A,B,2)` reports `TypeMismatch`, while `P(A,2,B)` reports `ArityMismatch` on all six C# routes. The integer instance reproduces in authoritative Lean Model-C too (`CoreTests.ModelC.mixedRepeatedConflicts`). The historical total helper theorem `repeated_name_failure_is_permutation_invariant` does not prove ordered Model-C failure invariance. This is a known unmet requirement/contradiction, not an alternative numeric equality or representation decision. The frozen rules have not been silently narrowed or changed; resolving the competing requirements needs an owner decision before readiness. Q-16 and Q-33 remain the two recorded pre-release Q IDs; this newly confirmed review blocker is additional to that inventory. Evidence: `../q28-q10-nf-df-codex-review/findings.md` and its final report.

VAL-11 title and representation clause clarified: one datum carries both value and representation; no canonical representative per equality class. No adopted policy, Q/X/PV status, issue-ledger entry, or historical evidence was changed.

## 2026-10-07 — owner follow-up closes the Q-28 permutation conflict

The previous independent hostile review's NOT READY finding is preserved above. The owner now preserves NEED-04 + PAT-10 + Q-27 ordered failure and narrows the earlier implementation overclaim: Permuting all contributions of one repeated name preserves satisfiability. On success it preserves the bound VALUE up to `==`, callable identity and channel availability; among equally rich compatible contributions, the first in written traversal order supplies the retained representation. Failure reporting follows ordinary binding order (NEED-04, PAT-10, Q-27): when different incompatibilities coexist, permutation may change the error category, blamed contribution, payload, message or span, although every permutation still fails. The historical total-helper failure theorem actually equates Option Error constructors under its global priority, and remains correct within HistoricalReadyBinding; it does not describe Model-C failure order. Both theorem statements/proofs are preserved. PAT-09, PAT-10 scope/example, NEED-04 wording, Q-28 and X-28 are reconciled; Q-28 remains N-F and Q-10 remains D-F. No runtime evaluator change and no new pre-release question. Evidence: `../q28-q10-nf-df-codex-review/owner-decision-closeout/`.

## 2026-10-08 — X-49 resolution and implementation (front-first movement, postfix first)

The owner decided X-49: Grace is a movement operator, and the multi-marker order is FRONT-FIRST MOVEMENT with postfix movers first — every name with a positive weight takes one turn, from the last-occurring to the first-occurring, then every name with a negative weight, from the first-occurring to the last-occurring; one adjacent exchange per unit; the patent's strict comparison of current remaining weights; a passed name keeps its weight; unused weight stays effective; weights are exact unbounded integers. The first investigation's Target Order proposal is not adopted. PAR-06 gains a dated X-49 law line and the Movement, Order of movement, Residual weights, The lifted tail, Invariants and Not guaranteed bullets (identically in the draft and the summary); its Saturation bullet no longer claims that a saturated marker means what the unmarked program means (residual weight still blocks); the PAR topic tail's "bubble arithmetic at ties" entry is struck as constitutional; PAR-03, PAR-05 and DOT-06 evidence pointers name `GraceMovement`. X-49 RESOLVED BY DECISION (X: 38 resolved, 1 superseded, 10 open); no Q, PV or oq status changed; § Decide before release: Q-33 only. Q-16 G-O eligibility, PAR-03, PAR-04, PAR-07, SYN-12 and PAT-04 were checked and need no change. Evidence: `../x49-grace-ordering-investigation/implementation/` (validation status recorded there).

## 2026-10-09 — Q-33 resolution and implementation (P0: the current parse is law; no new diagnostic)

The owner decided Q-33, the last question in § Decide before release, as recommended by `../q33-parser-boundary-investigation/final-report.md`: a line-final star is no exception to SYN-11 — when the next significant token, including across blank lines or comments, can begin an operand and does not begin a declaration, the star is binary multiplication, and expression parsing then continues across the newline under SYN-08 with ordinary precedence unchanged — and the layout alone introduces no warning or error. A spread row that another row follows ends with `A*,`; a definition body ending in a spread is closed as `X = (A*)` or `X = { A* }`; a following declaration head also ends the expression. The owner's two wording corrections are applied: a line-final star does not multiply "by the next line" as such (only under the classification condition), and the next line is not its right operand (ordinary precedence decides: `x, y = V*` newline `x + y` is `(V * x) + y`). Rejected: P1/P1ANY (a warning), P2A/P2B (a new error), P4A/P4B (newline rules that silently change accepted values). SYN-11 (status and a new rule bullet) and VAL-07 (a reference) changed identically in the draft and the summary; SYN-11's caveat and evidence, SUP-05's caveat (SUP-05 applies only once a star is classified as a spread), the SYN-11 index row and the SYN and SUP open-question lists changed in the draft. #140, #161, F13a and F13b keep their history. Q-33 RESOLVED BY DECISION; oq SYN-01 closed; § Decide before release holds no open question; the other open questions are unchanged. Counts: 79 Q IDs (41 resolved, 1 superseded, 1 partial, 36 fully open; none before release); PV and X unchanged. No production parser, evaluator, diagnostic, grammar, AST, Lean-semantics or public-API change. Evidence: `../q33-parser-boundary-investigation/implementation/` (validation status recorded there).
