## 2026-10-09 — VAR-03 + PAT-05 reconciliation (owner decision: NEED-07/08 collectors adopted; PAT-05 corrected)

The owner resolved the two P1 Constitution contradictions of the 2026-10-09 implementation audit (M-02, M-03) by adopting the behavior C# and Lean already implement. *VAR-03:* a collecting parameter preserves the supplied demandable argument cells; collecting demands nothing; re-spreading a known collected slice transfers the original cells and their available VALUE/CALLABLE capabilities without forcing VALUE, and the forwarding edge evaluates nothing; reading the collector as a value materializes one exact list and demands its elements, each reporting its own error; the collector invents no capability; an arbitrary spread is still evaluated during supply formation; FWD-02's by-name selection, Q-04, cardinality and PAT-06 are unchanged. The superseded value-only rule text (from the eager collector of 245b10c and Q-03, never reconciled with NEED-07/08 because the Model-C annex did not list VAR-03) is preserved verbatim in `decision-lineages.md` § VAR-03 + PAT-05 — 2026-10-09. *PAT-05:* general expressions remain invalid in pattern positions; `()` and `[]` are valid, distinct structural patterns (its two contrary sentences predated the 2026-09-29 structural-pattern decision; PAT-06 is unchanged).

## Model-C Phase A3 independent-review evidence corrections — 2026-10-10

Evidence: `../constitution-model-c-a3-independent-review/constitution-review.md`, `raw/witnesses-independent.json` and six-route observations. No language decision or NEED law changes; Q-45, Q-25 and X-18 remain open, Q-37 unchanged. FORMAL-07 and the final Constitution-wide audit remain separate.

### VAL-10 — retained prior text

> - Resource limits and cancellation are host policy (RUN-03). Cancellation is a host exception, not a KatLang failure. A resource-limit failure is a KatLang failure, but a terminal one: unlike an ordinary failure it is never a computation's completed outcome (RUN-03, since the Q-02 decision of 2026-09-28; NEED-02).

Current correction: - Resource limits and cancellation are host policy (RUN-03). Cancellation is a host exception, not a KatLang failure. A resource-limit failure is a terminal KatLang failure: the demanded need cell retains that completion, while the run's terminal budget prevents restarting it or continuing evaluation (RUN-03, NEED-02/09; Q-02 unchanged). A failure is not a recoverable calculation value.

### PAR-04 — retained prior text

> - `F(y) = { G = z + y  G(5) }` / `F(2)` gives `7`, because the nested `G` still infers `z`.

Current correction: - `F(y) = { G = z + y ⏎ G(5) }` / `F(2)` gives `7`, because the nested `G` still infers `z` (`⏎` denotes a newline, required by SYN-07A).

### FWD-01 — retained prior text

> - A collected list is passed to a collection builtin whole (`values.sum`). `sum(values*)` is an arity error (see COLL).

Current correction: - A collected list is passed to a collection builtin whole (`values.sum`). `sum(values*)` instead supplies the collector's cells individually: its count is accepted exactly when there is one cell, and zero or more than one is `ArityMismatch` before ordinary VALUE demand (CALL-04, NEED-05). The accepted cell is then demanded as the builtin's collection argument; `F(*values) = sum(values*)` / `F(3)` gives `3`.

### PAR-07 — retained prior text

>   - `ownership-and-lookup.md` § Automatic parameter forwarding preserves existing bindings, § Formula lifting is by binding name, § The unified formula-lifting law, § Inferred signatures are held to the rules of written ones, § The alias and bare-forwarding rules; forwarding preserves bindings and structural kind, § Math members (lifting is identity-keyed; only the lone-row alias eligibility stays route-based).

Current correction:   - `ownership-and-lookup.md` § Automatic parameter forwarding preserves existing bindings, § Formula lifting is by binding name, § The unified formula-lifting law, § Inferred signatures are held to the rules of written ones, § The alias and bare-forwarding rules; forwarding preserves bindings and structural kind, § Math members (lifting and lone-row alias eligibility are identity-keyed, including Math targets (FWD-02, X-47)).

## Q-25 — owner decision and implementation — 2026-10-10

Owner decision: Q-25, Option B — *a `map` transform or `reduce` step returns the ordinary result of calling its callback.* (RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION). The question stood in § Can wait since the pre-release re-baseline; the focused investigation `../q25-callback-result-investigation/` compared policies A, B and C and recommended B. The superseded contract (#37, kept by #132 and #135, extended to clause families by D1) is preserved as dated history, because after ordinary calls became value boundaries, retaining the raw-output callback restriction broke compositionality, identity mapping and wrapper equivalence.

Implementation: Lean and C# together. The single-value checks (Lean `expectSingleValueWith` / `expectSingleAccumulator` / `expectSingleMappedElement`, C# `ExpectSingleEmittedValue` and its two wrappers) are deleted; every callback invocation ends at the call boundary (`evalNeedUserSupply` / `evalNeedFamilySupply`, `EvalNeedUserSupply*` / `EvalNeedFamilySupply*`); `map` stores and `reduce` threads the call result whole. The C# `recount` flag, which doubled as the variadic arity-diagnostic style, is separated into `variadicArityStyle`. Only programs the old contract rejected change. Pins: `CallbackResultBoundaryTests` (the 92-program matrix on six routes and the law families), the rewritten `ClauseFamilyCallbackCardinality*Tests`, Lean `CoreTests/CallbackResultBoundary.lean` (23 guards) and the converted guards of six CoreTests modules, and five Lean-guarded spec cases (three new, two renamed).

## Q-25 independent review - unfolding qualification (2026-10-10)

*2026-11-26 review note. The result boundary decided here still applies. The demand order and effect examples below describe October. For the superseding #203 and #204 decisions, see HO-02 and HO-03 in the current Constitution.*

Owner follow-up: "Qualify the unfolding equation; preserve existing demand rules." Q-25 Option B remains fully adopted and resolved. Each callback returns its complete ordinary call result. Operationally, reduce processes collection elements from left to right, invokes each step with the current accumulator, and preserves the complete ordinary call result as the next accumulator.

The map/reduce unfolding equations express result-value equivalence for pure, deterministic, total computations. They do not guarantee identical effect ordering, argument demand, random draws, resource consumption or error timing. Model-C demand and established evaluation order are unchanged: reduce demands its initial once and finishes each earlier step; map demands its collection; expanded ordinary calls can skip unused arguments and inner calls.

Permanent six-route counterexamples: with R(x, acc) = tick() * x + acc, reduce([1, 2], R, 0) returns 5 and R(2, R(1, 0)) returns 4, with tick starting at one. With L = [tick(), tick()] and F(x) = tick(), map(L, F) returns [3, 4] and [F(L:0), F(L:1)] returns [1, 2]. A failing unused initial or collection similarly distinguishes the builtin from expanded calls. Pins: CallbackResultBoundaryTests.Independent_Reduce* and Independent_Map*; four derived-AST canonical cases in LanguageSpecCorpus (reduce-demands-initial-before-iteration, nested-reducer-calls-may-skip-unused-initial, map-demands-collection-before-transforms, expanded-map-calls-may-skip-indexed-arguments).

Evidence correction: reduce's result count is observed directly by internal counted evaluator entry points, and also by production planned-loop handover diagnostics when reduce is a nested step output. Ordinary source value receivers normalize that count. Independent_EmptyReduceResult_PreservesPlannedLoopHandover pins the optimized hit, exact handover reason, one materialization, and generic/async value and budget agreement.

Affected current text: HO-03 unfolding premise, the Q-25 resolution banners/register, and Appendix A P34 evidence scope. HO-04 and Model-C remain authoritative. ## Q-45 — owner decision — 2026-10-10

Owner decision: Q-45 — keep the current Model-C behavior of `.string(args)` as intended (RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION). *The `.string` intrinsic accepts exactly zero supplied arguments; a written argument list is formed like any call's supply, explicit arbitrary spreads execute during that formation, ordinary non-spread computations stay suspended, a nonzero count is rejected with `ArityMismatch` before the receiver or any ordinary argument is demanded, and only a supply of zero items reaches the intrinsic, which then demands the receiver's VALUE.* Extension fallback keeps its own ordinary demand order (DOT-03); there is no special argument-first or receiver-first rule. The question was formulated against the pre-Model-C argument-first order; Model C superseded that premise, and the Phase A2 re-run (2026-10-09) recorded the current order. The original question and its observations are preserved as dated history.

Verification: no production C# or Lean change. The existing seams and six-route pins establish the law; two six-route rows (the accepting side) and five Lean guards (`CoreTests/Strings.lean` § Q-45, the order on trace-free twins) were added. In an isolated Lean copy, four mutants that reorder the check were each killed — the receiver demanded first on the value-receiver or the algorithm-receiver path, the count taken from the written slots without forming the supply, and every written argument evaluated first; the two that reintroduce a special order (receiver-first on the value path, argument-first) passed every earlier permanent Lean guard and are killed only by the new ones.

## X-18 — owner decision — 2026-10-10

Owner decision: X-18 — preserve the existing Model-C argument semantics (RESOLVED — existing Model-C semantics confirmed by owner (2026-10-10)). *the current Model-C argument formation, cardinality validation and builtin-specific demand are intended.* Calls form their supply by the Model-C rules (explicit spreads executed at formation, every other slot suspended, collector slices transferred unforced), reject a count their callee does not accept with `ArityMismatch` before any ordinary VALUE demand, and evaluate each supplied computation at most once, on its first VALUE demand; which cells are demanded, and in what order, is the callee's own — a user callee's patterns and body, a builtin's argument roles. ## Q-37 — owner decision and implementation — 2026-10-11

Owner decision: Q-37 — the outermost successful property-style evaluation of a cache key owns its entry (RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION). *A property-style read that misses while no evaluation of the same key is running owns the key; a read of the same key while the owner is still running — directly, through other properties and calls, or beneath an explicit call's frame — is re-entrant: it evaluates and returns its own, possibly intermediate, result but publishes nothing; only the owner's success is published and reused; a failed owner publishes nothing for its key while other keys' entries stay; an explicit `A()` owns nothing.* This supersedes the first-successful-completion rule of 31f7c76: `A = if(tick() < 3, A + 1, 0)` / `A, A` was `S[2, 0]` and is `S[2, 2]`; the seeded `Classify`/`tick` reproduction (seed 7) prints `2`, `2` after exactly three draws. The original question and its observations are preserved as dated history.

Verification: Lean and C# changed together (C# `ZeroArgPropertyPublications` in both run-scoped caches and both members of the async one; Lean `EvalState.activeZeroArgProperties` in `evalZeroArgPropertyAccessCounted`). `OutermostPropertyCachePublicationTests` pins the law on six routes — the exact draw count, deeper, sibling, indirect, structural, shadowed, local-only, loop and explicit-call shapes, failure / resource-limit / cancellation publication recording, genuine async suspension — and runs the Lean guards' hand-built trees on the C# routes with identical values and published entries; `CoreTests/PropertyCachePublication.lean` adds nine guards. The superseded pin `ReentrantPropertyRead_KeepsTheFirstCompletedCacheEntry` (`"11,10"`) became `ReentrantPropertyRead_PublishesTheOutermostEvaluation` (`"11,11"`). Mutants and the full V3 run: `../q37-outermost-property-cache/`.

Records: both editions' top notes; PROP-09 (title, status and rule-index row; rule identical in both editions; why, examples, boundary, tension and evidence in the detailed edition), PROP-04 (the reuse sentence in both editions; boundary and tension), VAL-10 (surviving-state bullet in both editions; example), the PROP topic item, Appendix A P01, P35 (new) and Q22, and the detailed edition's stale § Summary status line; `open-questions.md` (resolution note, totals, the Q-37 record moved to § Already resolved, inventory, the § Can wait footnote, the tutorial documentation-fix item, § Q-37); `decision-lineages.md` (property-caching overview wave 4 and current-law note, Chain 3 step 10, Chain 9 step 6 and p4 note, § 3, § 5 item 2, § Q-37 with every replaced line verbatim); the issue ledger (dated pointers); this entry. ## Q-81 — owner decision and implementation (variadic postfix selection) — 2026-10-12

Owner decision: the owner approved the GitHub issue replacing `:` indexing with variadic postfix `[]` selection: *(A) PARENTHESES INVOKE A COMPUTATION; POSTFIX BRACKETS SELECT FROM A VALUE; selection never changes how its receiver obtains that value (`Data` cached, `Data()` fresh). (B) `count`, `first`, `last` and selection share ONE immediate positional view: a list or sequence offers its immediate elements, any other value — a string included — is itself at position 0, and `()` and `[]` have none. (C) Over a value in hand, `first(v) ≡ v[0]` and `last(v) ≡ v[count(v) - 1]`, errors included — laws, never rewrites of effectful expressions. (D) One element per supplied index, kept whole; only the result grouping is normalized: zero selections `()`, one the element, several one sequence. (E) The receiver is demanded once; the selector supply is formed like a call's (explicit spreads during formation, ordinary selectors suspended); the supplied indices are demanded, validated and selected left to right up to the first failure. (F) A `[` on the receiver's physical line (spaces and tabs allowed) opens the selector list, which may then span lines; a later-line `[` begins a list literal; commas select siblings and chained brackets descend; `A*[0]` stays multiplication; a selection has no callable identity; `:` is retired with a migration diagnostic.* It is registered as Q-81 and resolves Q-52 (the selector grammar). No rule namespace is added; principles A–F are integrated into SEQ-04 (retitled "Selection chooses stored values and never opens them") and SEQ-05, and every rule that spelled or relied on the colon is amended in place.

Verification: Lean and C# changed together; the implementation, its tests (positional laws, normalization, demand and effects with deterministic host traces, supply distinctions, parsing and tooling, route and resource parity), both regenerated differential artifacts, the read-only adversarial review and the full V3 validation (`validate-all.ps1 -Phase DotNet`, then `-Phase Lean` over all seven Lean targets; logs `v3-dotnet.log` and `v3-lean.log`) are recorded in `../variadic-postfix-selection/`. Every example quoted in the amended rules was re-run with the engine built from the implementation tree.

## Four-fix independent review: deferred host parents — 2026-10-12

The parent-scope owner decision remains unchanged. The independent review found one C# host-boundary gap: the deferred-placeholder early return also skipped a complete host-built parent attached to that placeholder. An undemanded branch could therefore hide an invalid two-collector signature or static alias cycle and the run would succeed. The corrected walker validates the parent while preserving the provisional body's materialization boundary. Alias reachability and target-head metadata follow that same boundary. Lean has no deferred-module placeholder representation, so this repair needs no Lean semantic change.

Permanent evidence is `FourFixInteractionReviewTests` (invalid parent refusal with zero downloads and zero steps, scope cycles, declaring-scope alias lookup, and parent-resolved planned steps combining shadowed captures, head mutation and generic handover). `CoreTests/ParentScopeValidation.lean` adds the matching valid shadowed ancestor alias-chain guard. ## Four-fix independent review: shared-parent error order — 2026-10-12

The review also confirmed a C# first-violation ordering gap on a shared acyclic host-scope graph. An inner scope's earlier property algorithm and the inner scope itself referenced the same outer scope. C# marked the entire pending outer chain visited before entering its members, so the property's earlier edge skipped an unvalidated outer violation and reported a later inner violation. Lean's existing depth-first traversal reported the outer violation first.

C# now marks a scope only when entering that scope's content, preserving the established traversal order while retaining identity memoization. Matching C#/Lean guards pin both edge orders; two new C# scale checks pin linear scope/link visitation when an ancestor chain is also reachable through property parents. A separate process probe validates 140 such levels on a 1 MiB thread. Lean semantics, normative rules, identities, statuses, counts and historical decisions are unchanged.

The first combined V3 attempt passed 35,247 of 35,248 core tests and all 342 CLI and 133 public API tests, with one existing independent-run stress-test deadline failure and zero build warnings/errors. The unchanged failed check passed alone in approximately one second. Its whole-workload deadline and worker cleanup were hardened separately; the smaller cell-level guards remain unchanged. That failed run is not final acceptance evidence. Fresh combined validation and preservation evidence are recorded in `../f7d6-combined-codex-review/validation.md`.

All six records were inventoried and backed up again before this addendum, independently restored byte for byte, and archived separately afterward. Earlier F7-D6, parent-scope and independent-review archives and records are preserved.

## Four owner decisions applied, 2026-10-13

Authority: the owner's explicit approval of 2026-10-13. This is Constitution-only implementation of established Model-C decisions; no Lean, C#, parser, public API, generator or language behavior changes.

- **PV-35 — RESOLVED BY DECISION (Model C).** Model C was adopted and implemented on 2026-10-02 (`8f0ceb85`); the owner-approved classification is recorded on 2026-10-13. The former eager VALUE read during callable transport disappeared: NEED-01/03/06/08 and RAND-01 govern plain binding, independent CALLABLE projection and first-demand effects. The original issue, lens verdict and historical evidence remain auditable.
- **X-43 — SUPERSEDED (Model C).** The algorithm-channel arity-payload carve-out disappeared with Model C on 2026-10-02 (`8f0ceb85`); classified 2026-10-13. Every supplied slot is one need cell and cardinality checks the complete supply before ordinary VALUE demand: `Inc(x) = x + 1` / `F(x, y) = x(y)` / `F(Inc)` reports `ArityMismatch(2, 1)`. The differential-observation question remains **Q-67 — OPEN**; its separately tracked documentation overclaims remain open.
- **X-38 — RESOLVED, effective 2026-10-02.** Q-15 option (c) and the Model-C implementation (`8f0ceb85`) established runtime rejection when the consumer demands the value; the canonical explanation and both generated blocks were corrected. The owner status classification is recorded on 2026-10-13. The separate documentation item that describes an explicit `A()` call as forwarding remains open under FWD-02; it is not part of X-38's original strict-value contradiction.
- **HO-01 — precision correction only.** Its plain-binding qualification and explicit inspecting-pattern reference agree with NEED-01, NEED-04, NEED-06, NEED-08, CALL-06 and HO-04. VALUE-demand behavior and demand order are unchanged.

Mechanically reconciled totals: PV 50 = 21 resolved violations + 21 resolved by decision + 6 intentional exceptions + 2 refuted (formerly 21/20/7/2); X 49 = 42 resolved + 2 superseded + 5 open (formerly 41/1/7). Q totals and all rule IDs, classes and statuses are unchanged. Q-66 and Q-67 remain OPEN. The five owner-gated documentation items (Q-65 lexical facts, Q-34 Math scope, Q-38 deconstruction `a()`, Q-63/Q-35 Lean load model, Q-14's optional hint) are unchanged.

The earlier P3 closeout recommendation and its unchanged-status statements are historical snapshots; this dated owner record supersedes only the three classifications and HO-01 precision point. ## Five owner decisions implemented — 2026-10-13

Authority: explicit owner approval of 2026-10-13, plus the owner's subsequent choice to enforce the complete-line rule rather than retain the definition-body leading-comma exception.

- **Q-65 current rule (revised 2026-10-14):** Names remain exact case-sensitive ordinal strings; LF alone defines physical line breaks (CR is whitespace, so CRLF works); backslash is not continuation. Q-65 now adopts grammar-role-first newline priority: independent expressions/declarations win, otherwise ordinary syntactic continuation crosses LF. Leading comma continues its expression-list grammar, including definition/clause/deconstruction bodies. Unary minus and negative literal pattern signs require exact direct attachment; -2 is independent while - 2 can continue binary subtraction. Leading star can continue multiplication; a physical line-final star always announces multiplication, including EOF/declaration/later closer. Spread requires explicit same-line closure. Precedence and spread VALUE semantics are unchanged. The 2026-10-13 strict choice, initial restricted stage, star amendment, unary amendment and explicit pattern clarification remain auditable in decision-lineages.md. Evidence: artifacts/validation/q65-restricted-leading-continuation/. *(Issue #183, 2026-10-14: refined by owner decision, katlangorg/katlangnet#183 — a line-final star is classified by attachment: an attached `A*` is a spread that completes the expression, a detached `A *` is multiplication, EOF/declaration/later closer included; spread is closed by explicit same-line syntax or by the end of its line after an attached star. The sentence on line-final multiplication above records the superseded subrule)*
- **Q-34 — RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION:** The C# native Math surface, its Expr.NativeCall bodies, random natives and lowercase aliases are C#-only by design, outside the current executable Lean model. Shared exact-integer operators and core builtins, values, binding and demand remain subject to Lean correspondence and FORMAL-04's common tier. Future native Math formalization requires a separate research decision; no exclusion or core test is weakened.
- **Q-38 — RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION:** An explicit deconstruction-target call `a()` freshly invokes the target projection wrapper and bypasses a's own property-cache entry; the wrapper reads the separately cached shared RHS. It does not request fresh RHS evaluation. A first target call can cause the RHS's first demand. Successful source caching remains per run for exported sources and per owner activation for local-only sources, with Q-37 publication unchanged. C# may reuse its shared binding; Lean may rebind the same cached source.
- **Q-63 — RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION:** Loading is implemented by C# frontend/hosting infrastructure. Lean retains concise descriptive prose and executable structural postElabInvariant predicates, without a complete executable load elaborator or a proof of C# loading. LoadError, LoadCtx and LoadPosition and their exclusively generated auxiliaries are retired after compiled dependency inspection. Definition/open entry contexts pass through transparent carriers; runtime positions refuse loading. Deferred and refused regions keep their separate C# rules. Q-35's load-prose subpart is settled as descriptive; its other front-end-sketch questions remain OPEN.
- **Q-14 optional hint — NOT_PLANNED for November 2026:** Q-14's semantic decision of 2026-10-01 remains RESOLVED. Its optional forwarding editor hint is NOT_PLANNED for the November 2026 release. This is a release feature disposition, not a permanent prohibition; future usability research may propose a separate feature. No diagnostics or editor behavior are added, and DiagnosticSeverity.Hint remains supported.

Current totals: 81 Q IDs: 52 resolved, 1 superseded, 2 partially resolved (Q-35, Q-44), 26 fully open (28 with an open part; 0 before release). PV: 50 total, 21 resolved violations, 21 resolved by decision, 6 intentional exceptions, 2 refuted. X: 49 total, 42 resolved, 2 superseded, 5 open. Former Q totals were 48 resolved, 1 superseded, 1 partial, 31 fully open (32 with an open part); only Q-34/Q-38/Q-63/Q-65 close and Q-35 becomes partial. Q-66/Q-67 remain OPEN, and all other questions and the previous four owner decisions are unchanged. The five P3 documentation dependencies are discharged by the approved dispositions; historical P3 and four-decision snapshots remain intact.

Validation level V3: final frozen serial .NET then all seven Lean targets, plus focused parser/deconstruction checks, isolated wrong-RHS mutation and compiled declaration/theorem/axiom inventories. Full results, independent review and verified six-file pre/post external backups are in `artifacts/validation/five-owner-decisions-closeout/`. No staging, commit or publishing is authorized.

## Internal-join spread-leaf fix — 2026-10-14 (Europe/Riga)

Scope: dated top notes of the draft, the summary and `open-questions.md`; a dated note on the P3 documentation item on internal joins; dated notes on the draft's internal-join description, its step-row evidence line and its internal-node case counts; recounted FORMAL-07 living counts; the `open-questions.md` bookkeeping section; this entry. No rule ID, class, normative rule text, status of any Q, PV or X item, or count of rules changed.

Work: the internal `sequenceConstruct` / `Expr.SequenceConstruct` join handed a whole spread LEAF to the spread-operand helper, which re-spread the spread's captured value, so a k-star leaf acted as k+1 layers whenever it supplied one structured item (`SC[[[7]]*, 3]` gave `(7, 3)`, `SC[[()]*, 3]` gave `3`). This was a regression from `ad757205` (2026-06-17) that became visible with exact lists (`d202b746`, 2026-07-16). It is fixed in Lean, C# sync and C# async: a spread leaf now contributes exactly its spread items, read back like a spread row of a written capture. The join now reads a spread leaf exactly as a written capture reads a spread row (Lean `evalCounted` + `countedTopLevelValues`; C# `EvalCounted` / `EvalCountedAsync` + `AddCountedTopLevelValues`). `SC[[[7]]*, 3]` is `([7], 3)`, `SC[[()]*, 3]` is `((), 3)`, `SC[[(1, 2)]*, 3]` is `((1, 2), 3)` and `SC[[[[7]]]**, 3]` is `([7], 3)`, each equal to the written `(x*, 3)`. A spread-produced `()` item is kept, and only a NON-spread `()` leaf drops. The operand is still evaluated once; error kinds, spans and step charges are unchanged, and only the join's own collection reservation sees the corrected count. No source program is affected. V3.

Authority: the owner's decision of 2026-10-14 to fix the defect in Lean and C# and to restore the dependent guards to their C#-twin shapes.

Records: pre- and post-edit six-file archives, each verified by independent byte-for-byte restoration: `../sequence-construct-spread-leaf/raw/pre-backup.json`, `../sequence-construct-spread-leaf/raw/post-backup.json`. Earlier archives are preserved.

Independent hostile review follow-up (2026-10-14, internal-join spread-leaf fix): no major finding. 19 minor or nit findings were confirmed and fixed, among them:
- an output-less spread leaf (`SC[{A = 1}*, 3]`, `SpreadMissingOutput`) is now pinned in C# and as a fifth `internal__sc_spread_*` differential case;
- the async seam test reads spread leaves over lone structured properties;
- the single-row while case formerly pinned by the restored guard is kept as `variadicLoopStepWhileOneRowSlotIsNotAFlag`, with a C# `BooleanValueTests` twin;

## Q-65 owner refinement: line-final star attachment (issue #183) — 2026-10-14 (Europe/Riga)

Owner decision: GitHub issue katlangorg/katlangnet#183 ("Follow-up design decision: newline continuation and attachment-sensitive `*`"), approved 2026-10-14. Canonical newline rule: "Newlines separate complete expressions by default. An incomplete expression continues when valid syntax permits it, without consuming a new declaration. Otherwise, an independent expression or declaration begins a new construct. Syntax that cannot stand alone but can legally continue the preceding expression attaches backward. All decisions are syntactic, never based on evaluated values." Star refinement: when `*` follows a completed expression and is the final significant token on its physical line — EOF included, across trailing comments and blank lines — attachment determines its role: an attached `expr*` is postfix spreading and completes the expression; a detached `expr *` is binary multiplication, leaves the expression incomplete and requires a right operand. With a same-line operand, multiplication stays whitespace-insensitive; explicitly closed spreads are unchanged; `F(A *)` stays InvalidSpreadMarker. Consequences: `A = 1, 2, 3` newline `A*` emits three rows; `A*` newline `B` is two rows and `A *` newline `B` multiplies; `A*` newline `B = 5` is a spread then a declaration, while `A *` newline `B = 5` is a missing operand with the declaration preserved; `A*` newline `-2` is two rows and `A *` newline `-2` is `A * -2`; `A**` at EOF is two spread layers; `7*` at EOF spreads the scalar 7; `X = A*` newline `X` is the spread definition and the row `X`; `A*` newline `.F` is the fluent `F(A*)` and `A*` newline `+ B` is MisplacedSpread. The classification is exclusively syntactic (exact source adjacency, `Parser.IsDirectlyAttached`); the owner explicitly rejected evaluating multiplication and falling back to spread.

Superseded current subrules (retained as dated history — annotated in place, except SYN-11, whose rewritten text is retained verbatim in decision-lineages.md under this heading): the Q-65 subrule of 2026-10-14 "a physical line-final star always announces multiplication, including EOF/declaration/later closer. Spread requires explicit same-line closure."; SYN-11's "EOF is not a spread boundary" and "Root-final bare `Values*` is intentionally no longer valid"; SYN-12's "Attachment is checked after classification. It never influences SYN-11's decision." (now limited to stars with a same-line follower); A22's "Whitespace never turns one valid operation into another." (now limited to one physical line). This adopts, by owner decision, the attached/detached newline rule of #140 (reversed by #161, rejected as Q-33's P4A). Q-33, Q-65 and #161 remain resolved/current in their surviving parts, and no new numbered question is created.

Implementation: C#-only surface syntax. The one classifier `Parser.IsPostfixSpreadMarkerStar` returns `IsDirectlyAttached(Previous, star)` for a line-final star and is now a non-inlined leaf; the detached spread-marker recovery run stays on one physical line; the line-final continuation diagnostic, the missing-operand message after `*` and the `InvalidSpreadMarker` wording name the attachment repair. No Lean production rule, theorem or guard changes; `lean/LanguageSpecCases.lean` and the generated generator-prompt blocks are regenerated from the canonical corpus (new case `line-final-star-attached-at-eof-is-spread`; `star-before-operand-row-is-multiplication`, `line-final-star-in-definition-continues` and `line-final-star-at-eof-is-incomplete` now spell their detached star explicitly). Pins: `LineFinalStarAttachmentTests` plus the migrated `LineFinalStarLawTests`, `LineFinalStarPriorityTests`, `StarSyntaxTests`, `MarkerAttachmentTests`, `DeclarationHeadLineRuleTests` and neighbors; `MaintainedDocumentationConsistencyTests` pins the canonical rule in AGENTS.md, `syntax.md` and `tutorial.md`.

Totals remain Q81 = 52 resolved + 1 superseded + 2 partial + 26 fully open (28 open parts, zero before release); PV50 = 21 resolved violations + 21 resolved by decision + 6 intentional + 2 refuted; X49 = 42 resolved + 2 superseded + 5 open. No rule ID is added or retired. Evidence: `artifacts/validation/line-final-star-attachment/`.

## R5 total supply and collector-demand control — 2026-10-15

Evidence update for NEED-01, NEED-02, NEED-07, NEED-08, FWD-01 and FORMAL-07;
no frozen language rule or question status changes. This dated entry supersedes earlier
claims that *all* supply/materialization/completion control is only executable evidence.
The old statements remain historical evidence of the pre-R5 boundary.

The approved A2+B2 extraction is implemented in the authoritative `lean/KatLang.lean`:
`NeedSupply.slot`/`form` are one total EvalM supply core (only computed-spread evaluation
is injected), and `NeedDemand.materialize`/`suspendedValue`/`step` are total collector
sequencing and completion control. The total public `formNeedSupplySourced`,
`formNeedSupply` and `demandNeed` specialize those helpers by definition. Partial evaluator
call sites install the same formation core; the one recursive `demandNeedRecursive`
driver installs the same completion shell. That recursive connection is source/guard
evidence, not a kernel unfolding theorem about the partial driver.

`lean/KatLangArityLaws/NeedControl.lean` proves actual-helper address/source retention,
ordinary transport and fallback, exact known-slice reopening (including duplicates),
whole-written computed-spread Ready allocation, formation ordering and failure truncation,
the address projection, exact eager-list collector materialization, ordered VALUE demands,
first-error state retention, completed outcome reuse and qualified current-cell publication.
Publication preserves the latest CALLABLE cache; its address must still be present.
Local allocation freshness and backward-reference validity have explicit initial-validity
premises. Conditional whole-list heap-size laws retain their callback/slot nonshrinking
premises; heap size alone does not preserve suspension identity or ownership.

Kernel theorems, source-exact migration comparison and finite executable observations are
separate evidence in `artifacts/validation/r5-implementation/REPORT.md`, `THEOREMS.md` and
their reproducible logs. The maintained guards include malformed-state counterexamples.
R3's generic binder, trace erasure, substitution, mode correspondence and dispatcher proofs
are preserved. R5 does not prove C# behavior, host concurrency/cancellation, arbitrary
expression evaluation, whole-run heap refinement or acyclicity of expression dependencies.
FORMAL-06 remains unchanged. Q-66 remains OPEN; R6, R7, the whole-run heap bridge and
the other separate Q-66 obligations remain open. No R6 implementation is included.

> **CACHE-PUB-1 — explicit zero-supply calls establish the property cache — 2026-10-22 (RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION; Lean and C# changed together; PROP-04, PROP-06 (retitled, same ID) and PROP-09 amended in both editions; uncommitted over `main` @ `18309b9f`):** *when a property's cache is empty, its outermost qualifying evaluation owns publication, whether initiated through a property-style read or an explicit zero-argument invocation; successful completion establishes the cached value; bare reads reuse that value; explicit invocations remain fresh and never replace an established cached value.* `A` and `A()` stay different operations: `A()` never obtains its result from the entry, but an owning successful zero-supply `A()` on an empty entry establishes it (`A = tick()` / `A(), A, A(), A` is `S[1, 1, 2, 1]` after two ticks, formerly `S[1, 2, 3, 2]` after three). Reads and invocations share ONE ownership domain (Lean: the `read` / `invoke` requests of `evalZeroArgPropertyAccessCounted`; C# `ZeroArgPropertyPublications.EvaluateMiss` / `Invoke`). Qualification uses the callee's authoritative ORIGIN (Lean `CalleeOrigin` / `originBinding?`, C# `CalleeOrigin`) and the EFFECTIVE supply after formation; what is published is the invocation's own property-level counted result, before the call's value boundary. A call with arguments, a builtin, host-operation or Math-member call, a parameterized runtime-callable alias's invocation and an invoking slot's invocation never establish an entry. The owner's D4 extension (2026-10-22) includes a runtime-target alias that resolves to ordinary zero-argument property behavior: the alias's own binding, scope and activation own the entry; the target's entry is never populated through the alias; terminal calls remain fresh. The RF1-D4 alias and ordinary control both give `(10, 10, 20, 10)` with two ticks. Compatibility: call-before-read programs evaluate fewer times (fewer draws and host effects, different resource outcomes); `DisplayDecimals()` in the output now supplies the engine's display-decimals read; read-first programs are unchanged. Evidence: `lean/KatLangArityLaws/PropertyInvocationLaws.lean` (one-step publication laws), `CoreTests/PropertyCachePublication.lean`, `ExplicitCallPublicationTests` (six routes), the superseded pins listed in `decision-lineages.md` § CACHE-PUB-1. Report: `experimental/features/cache-pub-1-explicit-call-publication-report.md`.

> **SEC-01-FU host-value admission — 2026-10-21 (C#-only resource policy; RUN-03 and RUN-05 amended in both editions; no Lean change — Lean models neither host operations nor limits; no change to the value of any run that completes — a run that consumed an oversized host value now stops, the limit law's permitted direction; uncommitted over `main` @ `8c7e3033`):** a successful host-operation return is now ADMITTED in the same DAG-safe walk that normalizes it, before any consumer or the zero-argument cache sees it: every distinct sequence/list node's IMMEDIATE item count must fit the effective per-collection limit (a nested value is one parent slot) and every string the per-string limit, or the run ends with that terminal resource failure (RUN-03); admitting nested structure is SEC-02 structural work (charged to steps, observes cancellation). Admission is a size check, never a cumulative materialization charge: the host, not the run, constructed the value. Before it, a host return bypassed both per-value ceilings (a 4-item list under a limit of 3, a 100,001-item list or a 1,000,001-unit string under the default ceilings were accepted). RUN-03's scope now also records SEC-01's transient-supply bound (committed as `0d56757c`), which added no limit kind. The per-call host-ARGUMENT call-reference scan, formerly uncharged and uncancellable although it repeats for every host call, charges the same structural work (accounting: no value changes, though under a configured step budget these units can now be what reaches the limit). Host exceptions, null returns and returned call references keep their contracts. The superseded RUN-05 caveat is quoted in `decision-lineages.md` § SEC-01-FU. Pins: `Hosting/HostValueAdmissionTests`, `TransientSupplyBoundTests`. Report: `artifacts/validation/sec01-fu/`.

> **R3′ pattern helper totalization — 2026-10-21 (FORMAL-07 roadmap item R3′, not the 2026-10-14 repeated-spread work that reused the label; formal-evidence strengthening and a behaviour-preserving Lean restructuring; no rule text change; no observable behaviour change; no C# change; no issue-status change; uncommitted over `main` @ `24c554fa`):** the pure helpers the total Model-C binder and clause dispatcher reach are now structural recursion in `lean/KatLang.lean` — value-pattern matching `matchPatternInto`/`matchStructureInto` (walker `matchItemsInto`), the kind-mismatch rendering `ParameterPattern.displayName` (`displayNames`) and `resultDiagnosticString` (`resultDiagnosticStrings`), duplicate-clause comparison `Pattern.matchEquivalentWithRenaming` (`matchEquivalentPairsWithRenaming`; `matchEquivalentItemsWithRenaming` is now a non-recursive wrapper), and the adjacent `ParameterPattern.captures` (`capturesOfList`), `Pattern.parameterPattern?` (`parameterPatternsOf?`) and `plainClauseParameterPatterns?`; `KatLang.lean`'s `partial def` count is 38 → 29 (nine removed). New `lean/KatLangArityLaws/PatternHelpers.lean` (130 theorems, 10 definitions; standard axioms only) proves the former recurrences, literal matching iff the same constructor and payload, the prefix/fresh-append environment contract, the conversion boundary (eligible iff literal-free; the converted head IS the clause's binder head), the STRUCTURAL CHARACTERIZATION of duplicate clauses (`isMatchEquivalent_iff`: equality up to an injective binder renaming covering every binder; reflexive, symmetric; the check is the pairwise test over clause patterns only), and production specializations: the binder's literal arm per literal constructor, its ordinary kind-mismatch error as an explicit string, and the dispatcher's rejection of a renamed duplicate head before any effect; `PatternHelperGuards.lean` adds executable witnesses. Preservation is these kernel equations plus a bounded executable comparison against verbatim frozen pre-R3′ copies (local `artifacts/validation/r3prime-pattern-helpers/preservation/`) — "equal to the old opaque constant" is NOT claimed. Not established: recursive VALUE demand, whole-run refinement (R7 stays OPEN), Q-66 (OPEN), C#'s mutable matching accumulator, or a Lean-`Int`/C#-Decimal128 text theorem; the remaining 29 `partial` definitions (alias counts, static chains, AST validation, `ScopeCtx.declaration` behind S5's `reprStr` fallback, lookups, cache shapes, identification, `exprDiagnosticName`, post-elaboration invariants and others) are unchanged. Report: `experimental/features/r3prime-pattern-helper-totalization.md`.

> **S6c CF-4 production connection — 2026-10-20 (formal-evidence strengthening and a behaviour-preserving Lean extraction; no rule text change; no observable behaviour change; no C# change; no issue-status change; uncommitted over `main` @ `7d6a58be`):** the shared body setup of the two row readers is the ONE total activated-body boundary `ActivatedBody.readRows` (checks: duplicate property, conditional read as a value, missing output; ONE `enterAlgorithmBody`; the builtin body's atomic route); `evalAlgOutputSlots` / `whileVerdictSlots` are that boundary with their distinct readers, kernel-checked equal to the former inline bodies. `KatLangArityLaws/WhileRoundProduction.lean` (62 theorems, standard axioms only) proves: one body entry and ONE context for every row of a round (`readRows_entered`, `readRows_uses_one_context`); body entry allocates no cell and keeps the binder's parameter cells, binding context and caches, recording at most one lexical activation (`enter_run`, `entered_*`); the production `formOutputRow` IS the production `evalCounted` plus `OutputRows.items` (exact); a finite-use converse of R7's `production_unfolds` (`production_eq_of_finite`) so the production `whileVerdictSlots` / `evalAlgOutputSlots` EQUAL their activated-body runs wherever those complete; the CF-1/2/5 and no-flag laws restated about the production `whileVerdictSlots` (`production_cf*`), check failures reaching no row, the builtin body's atomic row; the round's dispatch for COMPLETED runs (`runNeedWhileRound_user/_family/_builtin/_alias`: one binding or one family dispatch, then ONE `whileVerdictSlots`); shared cells (`round_parameter_cell`, `completed_cell_reused_after`); and, SEPARATELY, cache reuse: the production access returns a published entry without evaluating (`property_cache_hit_production`), published entries survive the transitions (Q-37), so an EXPORTED binding published during discovery is reused by every later read (`exported_publication_reused`); the local-only counterpart keeps an explicit key-stability premise (`local_publication_reused`). CF-4 status: the shared-activation / one-context / production-row-formation subclaims are DISCHARGED at the production `whileVerdictSlots`; the cache-reuse subclaim is discharged for exported keys and remains OPEN for local-only keys across rows (a whole-run lexical-activation invariant; `propertyOwnerBindingContext` is `partial`) and for evaluator-internal routing of property reads; the dispatch equations keep a completion premise (binder channels not `Finite`). Whole-run heap/cache refinement, reachable-state / active-cell discipline, termination, R7, Q-66, S6a and S6b are unchanged. Report: `experimental/features/s6c-cf4-production-shared-activation.md`; backups/evidence: `artifacts/validation/s6c-cf4/`.

> **META-S3-4 implementation — 2026-10-20 (owner decisions D-1A, D-2A and D-4 of the reviewed META-S3-4-R research, with its required consequences D-3 and D-5; an INTENTIONAL language extension; Lean first, then C#; implemented, NOT yet independently reviewed; uncommitted over `main` @ `1430a6f0`):** META captures every WRITTEN INVOCATION, independently of how its callee is reached — a call through a name, a parameter or an argumentless dot path, and an argument-bearing dot edge (`Box.Step(0)`, `Math.Abs(-3)`, `(Box.Step)(0)`, `5.Add(1)`, `Box.V()`, `op.Algorithm(op.Arguments*)`); an argumentless edge (`Box.G`, `5.Inc`), a name and a property read stay refused (D-2A; no implicit provenance). Capture stays syntactic and inert. Each plan has ONE route (call, member, `.Algorithm`, extension, or non-callable intrinsic), decided by the first route-dependent operation through the ONE dot decision ordinary dot evaluation takes (Lean `selectDotEdge`, C# `SelectDotEdge`), retained, its failure never absence. Reading the argument supply first establishes the route (D-4, CR-9; before `repeat`'s count, RP-1). The `.Algorithm` route's receiver is ONE retained cell per plan read by the ordinary invocation receiver evaluator (D-3). An extension route supplies its receiver first (`R = Capture(5.Add(1))`: `R.Arguments.count` is 2, `R.Algorithm` is `Add`; D-5). An intrinsic-shaped capture (`5.string()`, `R.Invoke()`, `R.Arguments(…)`) keeps its ordinary VALUE, while invocation, `.Algorithm`, argument-supply reads and loop setup are `NotAnAlgorithm "meta: not a callable invocation"` (D-1A). So `Loop(meta op) = op.Algorithm(op.Arguments*).while` makes `Step(0).Loop` a loop (3), and `Box.Step(0).while`, `Math.Abs(-3).repeat(2)` and `Inc(0).Repeat(5)` work without naming the step first. CR-2's dot-callee refusal (and the "name the step first" rule of the META-S3-3 banner below and of LOOP-01) is superseded; naming still works. No Q, PV or X status change. Evidence: `experimental/features/meta-s3-4-implementation.md`; laws `KatLangArityLaws/InvocationRoute.lean`, guards `InvocationRouteGuards.lean`, C# `InvocationRoute/*Tests`; rules `docs/design/language-rules/call-by-need.md` § Generalized capture. *(Constitution audit, 2026-10-23: the "NOT yet independently reviewed; uncommitted" status is stale — an independent review followed, its repairs recorded in § P of the implementation report (the final verdict is not filed in the repository), and the candidate was committed as `7d6a58be`, fixing #187.)*

> **META-S3-3 implementation — 2026-10-20 (owner decisions D1, D2, D6, D7, OD-1 and RP-1 of the reviewed META-S3-3-R research; an INTENTIONAL breaking language change; Lean and C# changed together; implemented, NOT yet independently reviewed; uncommitted over `main` @ `58c56703`):** the loops are `while(meta step)` and `repeat(meta step, count)` — ordinary fixed-arity builtins (exactly 1 / 2 supplied arguments) whose step is a genuine META parameter bound through the ONE binder (Lean `builtinSignature`, `builtinDeclaresMeta` and the META branch of `applyBuiltinCounted`; C# `ApplyMetaBuiltinCounted`). The captured call is never dispatched: its plan's canonical callee cell is the step (CR-13) and its ONE sticky supply the initial state (CR-9); zero initial slots are the ordinary empty state (OD-1); `repeat` prepares that supply before it demands its count (RP-1, the precedence LOOP-01/05 already stated). The retired `while(step, *init)` / `repeat(step, count, *init)` have no compatibility form: once the builtin is selected they are exact-arity failures. LOOP-01 and LOOP-05 rule text below is amended; every other LOOP rule is unchanged in meaning, and its examples dated before 2026-10-20 spell the retired surface (read `repeat(S, n, a…)` as `repeat(S(a…), n)` and `S.while(a…)` as `S(a…).while`; a value or a dotted/anonymous step now needs a name, CR-2). D6: a property read never recovers the call in its definition; CR-2 unchanged (qualified-call capture deferred to META-S3-4-R). No Q, PV or X status change. Evidence: `experimental/features/meta-s3-3-implementation.md`; laws `KatLangArityLaws/MetaLoop.lean`, guards `MetaLoopGuards.lean`, C# `MetaLoopInterfaceTests`. *(Constitution audit, 2026-10-23: the "NOT yet independently reviewed; uncommitted" status is stale — independently reviewed PASS AFTER FIXES, `experimental/features/meta-s3-3-review.md`, 2026-10-20, and committed as `9c8623f2`, fixing #64; its "needs a name"/CR-2 sentences are superseded by META-S3-4 above.)*

> **Independent R3 adversarial review addendum, 2026-10-15:** the earlier R3 counts describe the implementation snapshot. The reviewed sources now have 69 BinderTrace theorems (one added local allocator-freshness law), 25 FamilyDispatch theorems, 63 BinderCorrespondence theorems, 55 BinderGuards guards and 7 correspondence instance guards. Together with the 26 BinderCore and 12 adapted FORMAL-07 laws, the R3 axiom-audit inventory is 195 theorems. `allocateNeed_fresh_for_valid_supply` proves that one allocation avoids all addresses valid in its incoming heap and preserves their validity; it does not prove whole-binder freshness. The static plain-cell noninterference corollary still assumes no allocation reuses the excluded cells: initial supplied-address validity and nonshrinking channels would discharge that assumption, but the whole-run bridge and the partial production channels' heap-monotonicity proof remain outstanding. Trace erasure quantifies over arbitrary original EvalM capabilities lifted through the protected interface, not arbitrary trace-aware capabilities. Mode correspondence is one-directional: differing outcomes imply a family non-match and ordinary binder incompatibility; a channel raising an incompatibility-shaped error still raises in both modes. New guards pin this distinction, state retention after failed VALUE/allocation, emitted-count inequality, nested repeated names, equal callable identities and allocator reuse. The stale C# repeated-name test comment is corrected; executable C# is unchanged. FORMAL-06, Q-66 (OPEN), R4-R7, opaque-helper limitations and all prior decisions/statuses remain unchanged. This note records proof scope and guard coverage; validation and the final review verdict are recorded separately in `artifacts/validation/r3-independent-review/`.


> **R3 binder demand-order proofs — 2026-10-15 (formal-evidence strengthening; no owner decision; no rule text change; no observable behaviour change; Lean refactor and proofs only, no C# change; no Q, PV or X status change; totals unchanged; uncommitted over `main` @ `091ddca4`):** the Model-C binder and the clause-family dispatcher are now ONE generic core (`NeedBinder`, `lean/KatLang.lean`) over `NeedCapabilities` (VALUE demand, CALLABLE projection, cell allocation); every production name (`bindNeedPatterns`, `selectNeedFamilyBranch`, …) is by definition its `EvalM` specialization, and kernel proofs against a verbatim copy of the HEAD binder (evidence only) show the refactor preserved every outcome and the complete state. Kernel theorems in `KatLangArityLaws/BinderTrace.lean` (68 theorems), `KatLangArityLaws/FamilyDispatch.lean` (25 theorems) and `KatLangArityLaws/BinderCorrespondence.lean` (63 theorems), built by the `KatLangArityLaws` target, run that core in an external boundary trace that erases exactly to the production run (`trace_erasure`) and prove its demand order: the syntax-indexed event schedule (cardinality before inspection, written order, a collector allocated before its pattern, plain non-repeated bindings silent, an inspecting occurrence's own VALUE first, ready allocations before the nested gate, depth first, truncation at the first failure or non-match), the per-occurrence repeated-name order and verdict, the first-failure laws, and the family search and arbitrary-context activation; results depend on the channels only through the cells the run invokes (channel substitution, per channel), a head invokes its channels only on cells at inspecting positions or on cells the run allocated (its static form), and for every head a clause attempt and the ordinary binding differ only where the ordinary binding raises an incompatibility error (a literal-free head's ordinary binding is never a non-match). Not proved: Q-28's permutation laws (roadmap R4); the outcomes of the opaque helpers (literal matching, callable identity, the kind-mismatch payload, the duplicate-clause check — symbolic in the theorems); `demandNeed`, supply formation, the `partial` callers and C#. "R3" is the FORMAL-07 roadmap's demand-order item; that roadmap's own "R3′" (binder-helper totalization) remains open and is distinct from the 2026-10-14 "R3′" repeated-spread work. PAT-10's evidence, FORMAL-07, the Model-C annex, Appendix A O07/O14 and the Q-66 register row carry dated notes; FORMAL-06 and Q-66 are unchanged (Q-66 stays OPEN). > **Line-final star attachment — Q-65 owner refinement, issue #183, 2026-10-14 (owner decision; no Q, PV or X status change; totals unchanged; uncommitted over `main` @ `2cc6b56b`):** the owner approved katlangorg/katlangnet#183: when `*` follows a completed expression and is the final significant token of its physical line (EOF included; comments and blank lines change nothing), an ATTACHED `expr*` is the postfix spread marker and completes the expression, while a DETACHED `expr *` is multiplication and leaves it incomplete. The canonical newline rule — "Newlines separate complete expressions by default. An incomplete expression continues when valid syntax permits it, without consuming a new declaration. Otherwise, an independent expression or declaration begins a new construct. Syntax that cannot stand alone but can legally continue the preceding expression attaches backward. All decisions are syntactic, never based on evaluated values." — remains the authority; the star rule is a narrower syntactic classification feeding it. This supersedes the Q-65 subrule of the same day that every physical line-final star announces multiplication (EOF included), and narrows SYN-12's "attachment never influences classification" and A22's whitespace invariant to stars with a same-line follower; it adopts, as a refinement of resolved Q-65 rather than a new question, the attached/detached newline rule that #140 introduced, #161 reversed and Q-33 rejected as P4A. C#-only surface syntax: no Lean production rule, theorem or guard changes; spread/capture VALUE semantics, repeated spread, demand and arithmetic are unchanged. SYN-11 (title, status, rule, bullets, examples, evidence), SYN-08, SYN-12, VAL-07, VAL-08, SUP-01, SUP-05, OP-01, OP-02, A21, A22 and the Q-65 current-rule restatements carry dated notes; superseded text is retained. Evidence: `artifacts/validation/line-final-star-attachment/`.

> **Internal-join spread-leaf fix, 2026-10-14 (owner decision; no rule text change; Lean and C# changed together; host-built ASTs only; uncommitted over `main` @ `a366f888`):** the internal `sequenceConstruct` / `Expr.SequenceConstruct` join handed a whole spread LEAF to the spread-operand helper, which re-spread the spread's captured value, so a k-star leaf acted as k+1 layers whenever it supplied one structured item (`SC[[[7]]*, 3]` gave `(7, 3)`, `SC[[()]*, 3]` gave `3`). This was a regression from `ad757205` (2026-06-17) that became visible with exact lists (`d202b746`, 2026-07-16). It is fixed in Lean, C# sync and C# async: a spread leaf now contributes exactly its spread items, read back like a spread row of a written capture. The internal-join description and FORMAL-07's living counts carry dated notes: CoreTests has 2,237 guards, and SemanticExplorerCases has 2,649 guards over 19 internal-node cases. Evidence: `artifacts/validation/sequence-construct-spread-leaf/`.

> **Builtin-argument dead-infrastructure retirement, 2026-10-13 (no owner decision; no rule text change; no observable behaviour change; Lean and C# changed together; uncommitted over `main` @ `feb983a4`, on top of the uncommitted `resolveArgAlgsWithSequenceSpread` retirement and `reduce` initial-accumulator alignment):** every argument a builtin receives has been a supplied need cell since Model C (`8f0ceb85`), so the machinery for hand-built spread-marked arguments and for a named callable carried beside a value side had no production writer. A compiled-environment audit of the starting tree found every production construction writing `spreadsSequence = false` (only two `CoreTests/ParityGuards.lean` fixtures wrote `true`) and `callable? = none` (but for one copy of a callback argument's own callable, itself always `none`), `skipMissingValue` only `false`, and no referencer of `applyBuiltinResolved` or `requireCallableValues`. Retired: Lean `applyBuiltinResolved`, `requireCallableValues`, `expandSequenceSpreadBuiltinArguments`, `ResolvedArgumentAlgorithm.spreadsSequence` / `.callable?` / `.invoked`, `CallableCallItem.skipMissingValue` and the callable of the `.algorithm` callback argument; C# `ExpandSequenceSpreadBuiltinArguments` and its async twin, `ResolvedArgumentAlgorithm.SpreadsSequence` / `Callable` / `InvokedAlgorithm` / `PreparedValue`, the write-only `VariadicCallItem.PreparedValue`, `AlgorithmArg.Callable` / `InvokedAlgorithm` and `BuildCallableCallItemsAsync`. Lean's call-item assembly `collectSequenceCallableCallItems` is now a total definition (law `call_item_assembly_evaluates_nothing`), and a spread operand's known collector slice is found without allocating a cell (`spreadCollectorSlice?`; C# `FormSpreadSupplySlot`). The current evidence that cited the retired code as live is corrected: CALL-03, PROP-06, BOOL-05, HO-04 and LOOP-01, and CALL-06's description of a parameter read without a supplied cell (the algorithm-channel tier it named was retired earlier the same day). After an independent review, PROP-05, HO-04 and Appendix A row H02 name `formNeedSupplySourced` for the builtin arm, BOOL-05's Lean demand is `demandNeed` (the `if` arm never calls `argumentNeed`), and CALL-03's and LOOP-01's C# loop evidence says the loop demands its initial state itself only as a zero-iteration `repeat`'s final state. Evidence: `../retire-builtin-argument-dead-infrastructure/`.

> **`reduce` initial-accumulator report alignment, 2026-10-13 (no owner decision; no rule text change; Lean aligned to PV-05 and to C#; uncommitted over `main` @ `feb983a4`, on top of the uncommitted `resolveArgAlgsWithSequenceSpread` retirement):** Model C (`8f0ceb85`) left Lean's builtin call items with neither an argument's written source nor the CALLABLE projection of a cell whose VALUE demand failed, so Lean's builtin value-slot rejection reports never judged the callable the argument names: `reduce([1, 2], Add, Inc)` reported Inc's property-arity error instead of reduce's dedicated initial-accumulator report (PV-05), a written block initial even `unresolvedImplicitParams`, and `count(Lib.M)` kept the member's dot-call frame. C# kept them (`ResolveNeedSupply`'s `Source`, `FinishNeedCallItemDemand`). Lean now mirrors C#: the one supply formation records written sources (`formNeedSupplySourced`), the builtin arm passes them on, and a failed VALUE demand records the cell's projection unless the written slot is a parameter (`demandSequenceBuiltinCallItemValue`). A 288-program cross-engine probe changed exactly the 45 programs that differed from C#. No evidence locus changed. Evidence: `../reduce-initial-report-alignment/`.

> **Parameter-tier retirement, 2026-10-13 (no owner decision; no rule text change; no observable behaviour change; Lean and C# changed together; uncommitted over `main` @ `7ca8737b`, on top of the uncommitted P3 and `.string`-receiver candidates):** the algorithm-channel tier and the counted callback-parameter tier were retired in both engines — Lean `AlgBinding`, `AlgEnv`, `CountedParamEnv`, the `EvalCtx` fields `algEnv` / `countedParamEnv` with `withAlgEnv` / `withCountedParamEnv`, the `ParameterActivation` fields `algorithms` / `counted` and the two cache-key fields; C# the `AlgEnv` / `CountedParamEnv` aliases, the `EvalCtx` members, `ShadowAlgEnv`, `ShadowCountedParamEnv`, `LookupAlg`, `LookupCountedParam`, the activation and cache-key identities of both tiers, and the loop planner's dead `CountedParamSlot` plan. A built-environment analysis (every `EvalCtx.mk` / `ParameterActivation.mk` argument of the project) and a C# reference audit showed that no production binding site ever wrote either tier after Model C (P3 follow-up (2)); a probe over 4,334 corpus programs found every tier identity in a cache key to be the one shared empty instance, so dropping them merges no entry. A parameter's VALUE and CALLABLE identity are its supplied cell's; a parameter read without a cell reads its value binding, else `unknownName` (formerly `badArity` for an algorithm-only binding, which only a hand-built context could hold). CALL-06's evidence, the bind-shadowing evidence of the recursion rule, Appendix A P18 and Q42 record it; `issue-ledger.md` closes P3 follow-up (2). Evidence: `../retire-algenv-counted-tiers/`.

> **`.string` receiver alignment, 2026-10-13 (no owner decision; no rule text change; Lean aligned to CALL-06 and to C#; uncommitted over `main` @ `7ca8737b`, on top of the uncommitted P3 candidate):** Lean's `string` arm of `evalDotCallCounted` now reads a receiver that is a parameter bound to a supplied cell through that cell, before (and instead of) the zero-argument value-demand law (`hasSuppliedParameterCell`), as C#'s `EvalDotStringReceiverAlgOutput` and its async twin already did. The rules decide the order: CALL-06 lists the `.string` receiver among the slot's VALUE demands and states that a callable read as a value reports what the written argument raises. Before, Lean judged the PARAMETER's callable first, so with `Show(v) = v.string`: `Show(Inc)` lost Inc's property frame, `Show({x + 1})` was the parameter-shaped `ArityMismatch(1, 0)` instead of the block's `UnresolvedImplicitParams` (an error-category divergence from C#), and a clause family's `NoMatchingBranch` named the parameter. Values never differed. CALL-06's examples and evidence, DOT-08's evidence, Appendix A P18 and I14, and the CoreTests count record it; `issue-ledger.md` closes P3 follow-up (1). Evidence: `../string-receiver-frames/`.

> **Q-81 owner decision and implementation, 2026-10-12 (variadic postfix selection; RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION, resolving Q-52; Lean and C# changed together; uncommitted over `main` @ `3e244f91`):** the owner approved the GitHub issue replacing `:` indexing with variadic postfix `[]` selection: *PARENTHESES INVOKE A COMPUTATION; POSTFIX BRACKETS SELECT FROM A VALUE; selection never changes how its receiver obtains that value. `receiver[s1, …, sn]` demands its receiver ONCE through the ordinary value path, forms its selector supply like a call's (explicit spreads during formation, ordinary selectors suspended), demands, validates and selects the supplied indices LEFT TO RIGHT up to the first failure, and normalizes only the RESULT GROUPING — zero selections `()`, one the chosen element, several ONE sequence of the chosen elements kept whole; `count`, `first`, `last` and selection share ONE immediate positional view (a string is one position); over a value in hand `first(v) ≡ v[0]` and `last(v) ≡ v[count(v) - 1]`, never as rewrites; a `[` on the receiver's line opens the selector list, a later-line `[` is a new list literal, `A*[0]` stays multiplication, a selection has no callable identity, and the colon form is retired with a migration diagnostic.* No rule is added or retired: SEQ-04 (retitled "Selection chooses stored values and never opens them") and SEQ-05 carry principles A–F, and SEQ-02, SEQ-03, SUP-03, SUP-05, VAL-02, VAL-06, VAL-09, CALL-04, PAR-05, PAR-07, FWD-02, PROP-05, MOD-07, OP-01, HO-03, COLL-04, COLL-05, SYN-05, SYN-07, SYN-08, SYN-09, SYN-12, SYN-15, RUN-08, FORMAL-02 and NEED-07 are amended in place; this edition also respells the examples, probes and evidence of the SEQ topic, VAL-04, VAL-09, SUP-02, SUP-05, CALL-04, PAR-04, PAR-07, VAR-03, FWD-02, DOT-03, MOD-07, PROP-04, PROP-05, PROP-09, OP-01, OP-14, COLL-03, COLL-04, COLL-05, PAT-09, LOOP-03, LOOP-06, LOOP-07, LOOP-08, SYN-07, SYN-12 and FORMAL-06. Each sentence whose meaning changed keeps its former text inline as `*(Q-81, 2026-10-12: formerly "…")*`, and every replaced line is quoted verbatim in `decision-lineages.md` § Q-81. Q-52 (the selector grammar) and the SEQ and SYN selector-grammar open questions are resolved; the SEQ open question on the one-level views is partly settled (Lean proves `builtinCollectionItems = projectionItems`). § Summary keeps its counts (177 active rules); Appendix A gains rows F18 and P36 and amends A16, A25, B08, D08, E11, H18, H22, I09 and Q14. Implementation: Lean `Expr.index : Expr → List Expr → Expr`, the TOTAL `selectPosition` / `selectWith` / `selectionResult` with the § Variadic postfix selection theorems and `CoreTests/VariadicSelection.lean`; C# `Expr.Index(Target, Selectors)`, `Parser.ParseSelectionContinuation` and the retired-colon recovery (`DiagnosticCode.RetiredColonSelection` 58, `KatLangErrorCode.RetiredColonSelection` 80), the Index case of the spine machine and its async twin, every front-end pass, the semantic model and the renderer; tests, both differential corpora, the generated artifacts, the EBNF, the tutorial, the generator guidance and the language-rule documents. Report: `artifacts/validation/variadic-postfix-selection/`.

> **Historical-state clarification, 2026-10-12 (Q-81):** the dated notes below — and the history they quote — spell selection with the colon (`A:i`, `A:0:1`, `():0`), call the selector a primary, reject `A[1]` as a same-line separator error and `A*:0` as `MisplacedSpread`, and leave the selector grammar open. Those statements describe their dates; since Q-81 (above) selection is the variadic postfix `A[s1, …, sn]` and the colon is retired with a migration diagnostic. The original notes remain verbatim.

> **Q-80 owner decision and implementation, 2026-10-11 (task STR-02 — string interpolation; RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION, superseding the STR-01 investigation; Lean and C# changed together; committed as `3e244f91`, original implementation over `main` @ `ce23d48b`):** the owner approved the STR-02 contract: *a single-quoted string stays RAW; a double-quoted string has exactly the escapes `\"` `\\` `\n` `\t` `{{` `}}` and interpolation holes `{expression}`, each ONE ordinary expression of the enclosing owner, evaluated LEFT TO RIGHT, EACH ONCE per evaluation of the string, each converted at once to its value's structural text — a number its canonical text (`.string`'s, never display decimals), `true` / `false`, a string verbatim and unquoted at every nesting level, a sequence `(a, b)`, a list `[a, b]`, `()` and `[]` distinct — into ONE string value; the first failure stops the string, and a hole-free double-quoted string is the single-quoted literal.* Two rules are new — **SYN-15** (the two spellings, the six escapes, the one-line token structure, the one-expression hole and the fixed-text positions; C#-only by design) and **VAL-12** (one string value, hole order and demand, the structural text; Lean-modeled) — and SYN-02, SYN-03, SYN-05, SYN-07, SYN-08, SYN-11, VAL-01, VAL-02, VAL-09, VAL-11, BOOL-03, DOT-08, OP-03, OP-04, OP-14, PAT-05, MOD-02, MOD-07, PAR-03, PAR-07, SUP-05, SUP-06 (why), ALG-01, RUN-03, RUN-06, RUN-08, FORMAL-02 and FORMAL-04 are amended in place: each sentence whose meaning changed keeps its former text inline as `*(Q-80, 2026-10-11: formerly "…")*`, and every replaced line is quoted verbatim in `decision-lineages.md` § Q-80. Q-64 (strings without escapes) is resolved by this decision; the SYN topic's open question on escapes is resolved and the VAL topic's string question partly (ordering and the other string operations of #36 stay open). § Summary is recounted (177 active rules) and Appendix A gains rows A03b, B20 and F17. Implementation: Lean `Expr.interpolatedString` / `InterpolationLink`, `Result.canonicalText`, the TOTAL `interpolateWith` with its `interpolate_*` theorems and the `.interpolatedString` arms; C# lexer (`DoubleQuotedStringLexer`), parser, AST, the iterative spine-machine evaluation on the sync route and the async twin, every front-end pass, the semantic model, the diagnostic renderer and the C#-only string limits; tests, both differential corpora, the generated artifacts, the EBNF, the tutorial, the generator guidance and the language-rule documents. > **Q-37 owner decision and implementation, 2026-10-11 (RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION; Lean and C# changed together; uncommitted over `main` @ `43292ed7`):** the owner decided Q-37, the re-entrant read of a zero-parameter property: *the OUTERMOST successful property-style evaluation of a cache key owns its entry.* A property-style read that misses while no evaluation of the same key is running becomes the key's OWNER; a read of the same key while its owner is still running — directly (`A → A`) or through other properties and calls (`A → B → A`), an explicit call's frame included — is RE-ENTRANT: it evaluates the body and returns its own, possibly intermediate, result but publishes nothing. Only the owner's success is published, and every later ordinary read reuses it; publication never replaces an entry. A failed owner publishes nothing for its key (a re-entrant result computed during the failed attempt never becomes the entry) while entries other keys' owners published stay, and an explicit `A()` owns nothing. This supersedes the first-successful-completion rule (`31f7c76`, 2026-09-12), under which the innermost completion was stored first: `A = if(tick() < 3, A + 1, 0)` / `A, A` was `S[2, 0]` and is now `S[2, 2]` after the same three ticks; the seeded reproduction (`Classify(r) = if(r == 796, 1, if(r == 339, 2, 3))` / `tick = Classify(randomInt(0, 1000))` / `A = if(tick() < 3, A + 1, 0)` / `A, A`, seed 7) prints `2`, `2` after exactly three draws (796, 339, 489; formerly `2`, `0`). C# `ZeroArgPropertyPublications` (both run-scoped caches, both members of the async one, so the planned exported loop temp too); Lean `EvalState.activeZeroArgProperties` with `evalZeroArgPropertyAccessCounted`. Pins: `OutermostPropertyCachePublicationTests` (six routes, the exact draw count, failure / resource-limit / cancellation publication recording, and the Lean guards' hand-built trees on the C# routes) and `CoreTests/PropertyCachePublication.lean`. Rules: PROP-09 (title, status, rule — identical in both editions — why, examples, boundary, tension, evidence and its rule-index row) and PROP-04 (the reuse sentence, identical in both editions; the boundary caveat; the known tension, now resolved), with VAL-10's surviving-state bullet (both editions) and example, the PROP topic item, and Appendix A P01, P35 (new) and Q22. The rule status tally moves from 46 / 1 / 116 / 12 to 46 / 1 / 117 / 11 (recounted mechanically; PROP-09 leaves the open-question bucket). FA-OQ-1, Q-25, Q-45 and X-18 stay resolved; FORMAL-07 remains a separate project. Evidence: `../q37-outermost-property-cache/`. *(Historical read-only publication rule; CACHE-PUB-1, 2026-10-22, supersedes the explicit-call exclusion while preserving outermost ownership.)*

> **X-18 owner decision, 2026-10-10 (RESOLVED — existing Model-C semantics confirmed by owner (2026-10-10); no C# or Lean behavior change — documentation, decision records and one documentation regression guard only):** the owner closed X-18 — the conflict between the builtin argument regime and the former AGENTS.md claim that call assembly is "SHARED by every callable shape … EXACTLY ONCE left to right" — as a documentation and decision-record closeout: *the current Model-C argument formation, cardinality validation and builtin-specific demand are intended.* A call forms its written supply by the Model-C rules (NEED-01, NEED-05, NEED-07): an explicit arbitrary spread executes during formation, every other non-spread slot stays a suspended computation, and a known collector slice is transferred unforced. A supply count the callee does not accept is `ArityMismatch` before any ordinary VALUE demand (explicit spreads may already have run). A supplied computation runs at most once, on its first VALUE demand, and never if nothing demands it. Which cells are demanded, and in what order, is the callee's own: a user callee's inspecting patterns and body; a builtin's argument roles — `if` demands its Boolean condition, then only the selected branch; a callback is projected for its CALLABLE identity only when invoked, never VALUE-demanded; `reduce`'s initial is a VALUE slot. Shared supply machinery with differing demand policies is intentional; there is no universal eager, left-to-right, exactly-once argument rule, and no new builtin regime is introduced. Q-07 (one acquisition regime) was already resolved by Model C on 2026-10-03; X-18 tracked only the remaining documentation, now corrected: the tutorial's `if` section and builtin table now state the spread exception (and § Opening a Value with `*` shows it), the AGENTS.md call-assembly bullet and `sequences-lists-and-calls.md` § Call argument assembly state that formation is shared and demand is not, and the public XML documentation of `Expr.Call` and `Algorithm.Conditional` no longer describes eager argument evaluation; `MaintainedDocumentationConsistencyTests` gains five X-18 stale-shape guards. Re-verified: 36 witnesses on six C# routes, each against an expectation written before execution, all agree; Lean agrees on all 20 Lean-comparable ones (the other 16 reach the host `trace` or `randomInt`, which Lean does not model, each confirmed executed by C#); an isolated Lean mutant whose supply formation demands every ordinary slot written before a spread is killed by five existing guards (`CoreTests/BuiltinArgumentRoles.lean`, among them `take(Bad, BadType*)`, whose first raised failure is the later spread's, and `CoreTests/HigherOrderCalls.lean`), so no new Lean guard was needed. Constitution: BOOL-05's documentation tension and the BOOLCMP item "User documentation of the spread-first exception" are resolved and CALL-03's documentation evidence is current (detailed edition only); no rule text, rule ID or status changes — BOOL-05 keeps its other open question, Q-68 — so the rule status tally stays 46 / 1 / 116 / 12 (recounted mechanically). X-18 is RESOLVED (X: 40 resolved, 1 superseded, 8 open); the question and PV counts are unchanged. FA-OQ-1, Q-25 and Q-45 stay resolved, Q-37 is unchanged, FORMAL-07 remains a separate project. Evidence: `../constitution-x18-closeout/`.

> **Q-45 owner decision, 2026-10-10 (RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION; the current Model-C behavior is kept as intended; no C# or Lean behavior change — two C# test rows and five Lean guards now pin it; committed as `a024eeaf`, original resolution over `5041bffe`):** the owner closed Q-45, the evaluation order of `.string(args)`: *the `.string` intrinsic accepts exactly zero supplied arguments, and a written argument list follows the ordinary Model-C call steps.* The list is formed like any call's supply; an explicit arbitrary spread executes during that formation; every ordinary non-spread slot stays suspended; a supply of one or more items is rejected with `ArityMismatch` before the receiver or any ordinary argument is demanded; only a supply of zero items — no list, `x.string()`, or spreads that supply no item — reaches the intrinsic, which then demands its receiver's VALUE and converts it. There is no argument-first or receiver-first rule, and an extension fallback `R.F(args)` keeps the ordinary demand order of the call `F(R, args)` (DOT-03). The question was posed against the pre-Model-C order (the arguments evaluated before, and instead of, the receiver); Model C (2026-10-02) superseded that premise, and the owner accepted the order the Phase A2 re-run recorded (2026-10-09). Rules: DOT-08 (status — no open question remains; **Arguments** and the Q-28 consequence sentence, identical in both editions; two examples, the evaluation-order tension, the evidence and the DOT topic item) and RUN-08 (the `.string` receiver joins the closed list of demand points, and the explicit-spread strictness names the intrinsic's list; identical in both editions; evidence), with Appendix A P11 and Q42. FA-OQ-1 and Q-25 stay resolved, X-18 remains open, Q-37 is unchanged, FORMAL-07 remains a separate project. Evidence: `../q45-string-args-resolution/`.
