> **Q-25 owner decision and implementation, 2026-10-10 (Option B; RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION; Lean and C# changed together; committed as `5041bffe`, original implementation over `c74cd117`):** the owner decided Q-25, the result contract of `map`/`reduce` callbacks: *a `map` transform or `reduce` step returns the ordinary result of calling its callback.* Several written rows arrive as ONE sequence value, `()` and `[]` are ordinary values, `map` stores the result whole as one element and `reduce` passes it whole as the next accumulator; there is no implicit spread and no cardinality or non-empty check, so for pure, deterministic, total computations, `map(L, F)` has the result value `[F(L:0), …]` and `reduce([a, b], R, i)` has the result value `R(b, R(a, i))`. Errors propagate as in the ordinary call (an output-less callback is still `MissingOutput`, VAL-05); `filter` keeps its Boolean contract and a loop step's rows are still its next state. Options A (keep the strict raw-output contract) and C (accept the call result but reject `()`) were not adopted. The superseded contract — #37's single-value result, kept by #132 and #135 and extended to clause families by D1 — is preserved as history: after ordinary calls became value boundaries, retaining the raw-output callback restriction broke compositionality, identity mapping and wrapper equivalence. *Implementation:* Lean deletes `expectSingleValueWith` / `expectSingleAccumulator` / `expectSingleMappedElement` and re-counts every callback at the call boundary (`evalNeedUserSupply` / `evalNeedFamilySupply`); C# deletes `ExpectSingleEmittedValue` and its two wrappers, and `EvalMapCounted` / `EvalReduceCounted` with their async twins store and thread the call result whole (the former `recount` flag is separated into the diagnostic style `variadicArityStyle`). Rules reconciled: HO-03 (rewritten; title, status, rule, examples, caveats, tension, decision record, evidence), HO-02, HO-04, HO-05, VAL-04, VAL-06 (status), VAL-08, SEQ-04, COLL-04, PAT-11, LOOP-07, FORMAL-05, Appendix A (E09, I12, P24, the new P34, Q24) and the VAL, SEQ and ALGHO open-question lists; the rule status tally moves from 46 / 1 / 113 / 15 to 46 / 1 / 115 / 13 (HO-03 and VAL-06 lose their open question; recounted mechanically). X-32 is RESOLVED, PV-32 and PV-33 are RESOLVED BY DECISION. FA-OQ-1 is unchanged (resolved), Q-45 and X-18 remain open, Q-37 is unchanged, FORMAL-07 remains a separate project. Evidence: `../q25-callback-result-investigation/implementation/`.

> **FA-OQ-1 owner decision and implementation, 2026-10-10 (Option B; RESOLVED BY OWNER DECISION; Lean and C# changed together; committed as `c74cd117`, original implementation over `24883d06`):** the owner decided FA-OQ-1, which the final Constitution-wide audit recorded: *an exact callable alias, whether declared by name or written as an inline block `{ F }`, delegates a rejected zero-argument VALUE demand to its target's ordinary contract.* An inline alias has no signature and no report of its own, so its rejected demand is reported exactly as its written target reference `F` reports it — `Inc(x) = x + 1` / `K(z) = count({ Inc })` / `K(1)` is Inc's `ArityMismatch` ("Property 'Inc' expects 1 parameter, but was called with 0 arguments.", at the `Inc` inside the block), exactly as `count(Inc)`; a clause family keeps `NoMatchingBranch` (named by the reference), a member path its bare arity, a builtin its own arity error — never the written block's `UnresolvedImplicitParams`, which a block reports only over parameters it infers itself (`{x + 1}`, `{ Inc + 0 }`). > **Model-C final reconciliation (Phase A3), 2026-10-10 (no owner decision; no C# or Lean behavior change):** the five rules that neither Phase A1 nor Phase A2 reconciled — CALL-04, PAR-04, PAR-07, FWD-01 and FWD-02 — now state the adopted Model-C law (NEED-01..10, NEED-FWD) in their own text, and the residue a targeted sweep found elsewhere is corrected the same way. A parameter list is satisfied by the number of supply cells, and a count the selected callee does not accept is `ArityMismatch` before any non-spread argument is demanded — for a clause family too, which reports `NoMatchingBranch` only for an accepted count that no clause matches; binding a fixed parameter attaches its cell and demands nothing; an arity payload is (the signature's minimum, the complete supply count), so `F(x, y) = x(y)` / `F(Inc)` reports `(2, 1)`; a formula's VALUE role is a static classification that decides lifting, never a run-time demand; a reference that a closed list blocks is its run-time zero-argument demand in every position, rejected only when executed (Q-15: no static strict-Math diagnostic remains); forwarding — a collector's re-spread, bare forwarding and every synthesized call — transfers the very supply cells, unforced and with both channels; a callable alias is binding indirection with no signature of its own; and a demanded cell that reaches a resource limit stores it as its terminal completion (RUN-03, now matching CALL-06). > **Model-C propagation reconciliation (Phase A2), 2026-10-09 (no owner decision; no C# or Lean behavior change):** the 18 rules the Phase A1 note left carrying superseded eager wording — DOT-03, DOT-08, VAR-04, VAR-06, PAR-01, PAT-07, PAT-09, PAT-10, PROP-02, PROP-04, PROP-05, HO-01, HO-04, COLL-02, COLL-09, RUN-07, RAND-01 and RAND-03 — now state the adopted Model-C law (NEED-01..10, NEED-FWD) in their own text. > **VAR-03 + PAT-05 reconciliation, 2026-10-09 (owner decision; the C# and Lean behavior is unchanged):** the owner adopted the existing Model-C NEED-07/08 collector and forwarding semantics for VAR-03, superseding its pre-Model-C value-only text. *The law (VAR-03):* a collecting parameter binds a slice of the supplied argument cells; collecting demands nothing; re-spreading a known slice (`xs*`) transfers the original cells unforced, with their available VALUE and CALLABLE capabilities (`Inc(x) = x + 1` / `Apply(f) = f(9)` / `Fwd(*fs) = Apply(fs*)` / `Fwd(Inc)` is `10`); reading the collector as a value materializes one exact list and demands every element, which reports its own error (`Collect(*xs) = xs` / `Collect(Inc)` is Inc's `ArityMismatch`, never a collector `TypeMismatch`); no capability is invented, and an arbitrary spread is still evaluated during supply formation. *PAT-05 corrected:* expressions such as `1 + 1` remain invalid patterns, while `()` and `[]` are valid, distinct structural patterns; its former claims that `F(())` and `F([])` are parse errors and that empty and list patterns do not exist predate the structural-pattern decision (PAT-06, unchanged). Rules changed identically in the draft and the summary: VAR-03 (title, status, rule), PAT-05 (rule), PAT-11 (title, rule), CALL-05 (rule); in the draft only: their examples, caveats and evidence, VAR-04's reference, the VAR topic lists, the PAT-11 index row and the status tally (Current, not C#-only 112; (open question) 16); the Model-C reconciliation note in both. Evidence: `../var03-pat05-reconciliation/`.

> **Historical Q-33 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-09 (conflicting line-final subrule superseded 2026-10-14; current SYN-08/SYN-11 govern) (committed as `d7dae7d8`; original implementation over `main` @ `4d7cac19`):** the owner decided Q-33 — P0: the current parse is law; no new diagnostic — the recommendation of `../q33-parser-boundary-investigation/final-report.md`. *The law (SYN-11):* a line-final star is no exception: a `*` at the end of a physical line is classified by SYN-11 exactly like any other `*`; if the next significant token, including across blank lines or comments, can begin an operand and does not begin a declaration, the star is binary multiplication, and expression parsing then continues across the newline under SYN-08 with ordinary precedence unchanged (`X = A*` newline `X` is `X = A * X`, which leaves no output row; `x, y = V*` newline `x + y` is `(V * x) + y`, never `V * (x + y)`); the layout alone introduces no warning or error. A spread row that another row follows ends with `A*,`; a definition body ending in a spread is closed as `X = (A*)` or `X = { A* }`; a following declaration head also ends the expression. Rejected: a warning (P1, P1ANY), a new error (P2A, P2B), and newline rules that would silently change accepted values (P4A, P4B). *(Issue #183, 2026-10-14: the attached/detached line-final newline rule that P4A prototyped was later adopted by owner decision as a refinement of Q-65 — see "Line-final star attachment" above)* *Implementation:* no production parser, evaluator, diagnostic, grammar, AST, Lean-semantics or public-API change — documentation (AGENTS.md, `syntax.md`, `KatLang.ebnf`, `tutorial.md`, `SEMANTIC-ALIGNMENT.md`), the new `LineFinalStarLawTests`, the repaired `ModuleCoordinateSpaceTests` spread fixture, and the regenerated spec case `line-final-star-in-definition-continues`. **Q-33 RESOLVED BY DECISION; § Decide before release now holds no open question** (the other open questions stay open). Rules reconciled: SYN-11 (status, rule, caveat, evidence), SUP-05 (caveat), VAL-07 (a reference), the SYN and SUP open-question lists. 79 Q IDs: 41 resolved, 1 superseded, 1 partial, 36 fully open (37 with an open part; 0 before release). > **X-49 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-08 (historical inventory; superseded by the Q-33 resolution above; committed as `4d7cac1`):** the owner decided X-49 — FRONT-FIRST MOVEMENT, POSTFIX FIRST, the law selected after `../x49-grace-ordering-investigation/movement-followup/final-report.md` (the earlier Target Order proposal is not adopted). *The law (PAR-06 Movement, Order of movement, Residual weights):* an owner's OWN inferred names start in first semantic occurrence with their exact summed weights (unbounded integers); every name with a POSITIVE weight takes one turn, from the LAST-occurring to the first-occurring, then every name with a NEGATIVE weight, from the FIRST-occurring to the last-occurring; a turn exchanges the name with its neighbour one unit at a time until its remaining weight is zero, it reaches the end of the own list, or the neighbour moves the same way with at least as much REMAINING weight (the patent's strict comparison of current remaining weights); a passed name keeps its weight; unused weight stays and keeps blocking; nothing crosses the own/lifted boundary. `K = s * 100 + ~x * 10 + ~y` is `K(x, y, s)`, `Z = s~ * 100 + x~ * 10 + y` is `Z(y, s, x)`, `K = a * 100 + b~ * 10 + ~~c` is `K(c, a, b)` (formerly `K(a, c, b)`: the 2021 position cursor skipped the displaced `c`), `K = a~ * 100 + b * 10 + ~c` is `K(b, c, a)` (prefix-first would give `(c, a, b)`), and `F = ~a * 100 + b * 10 + ~~c` is `F(a, c, b)`. *Implementation (C#-only by design):* the internal `GraceMovement` replaces `ParameterDetector`'s position cursor; weights are exact (`System.Numerics.BigInteger`; the int-saturating memo effect is deleted, the shared-node memo keeps exact per-visit deltas); no eligibility (Q-16 G-O), grammar, lifting, evaluator, Lean-semantics, diagnostic or public-API change; a standalone Lean model is `lean/CoreTests/GraceMovement.lean`. **X-49 RESOLVED BY DECISION.** Rules reconciled: PAR-06 (title, status, law, rule, examples, caveat, tension, evidence), PAR-03/PAR-05 and DOT-06 (evidence pointers), the PAR topic tail. 79 Q IDs: 40 resolved, 1 superseded, 1 partial, 37 fully open (38 with an open part; 1 before release). PV: 50 total, 0 open, 0 active settled violations, 0 partial, 21 resolved violations, 17 resolved by decision, 10 intentional, 2 refuted. X: 49 total, 38 resolved, 0 partial, 0 still-reproducing settled defects, 1 superseded, 10 open. § Decide before release: Q-33. Implementation and validation evidence: `../x49-grace-ordering-investigation/implementation/` — validation PASSED 2026-10-08 on the final tree: V3 `validate-all.ps1 -Phase All` (full solution build with 0 warnings; KatLang.Tests 32,221/32,221, KatLang.CLI.Tests 338/338, the public-API suite 133/133 with the baseline unchanged; `git diff --check`; all seven Lean targets), the six-route X-49 corpora on the production front end (912 variants: 0 route disagreements, explicit-list twins identical, equal to the independent model on all 750 multi-marker programs), production mutation testing (23 of 23 mutants killed), deterministic language-spec regeneration, and a package-consumer smoke test of 0.8.232 (packed locally, not published).

> **Q-16 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-07 (historical inventory; superseded by the X-49 resolution above; committed as `f314cf6`):** the owner decided Q-16 — G-O, owner-local Grace: Q-16(1) E (eligibility, not movement) and Q-16(2) O (nested inferred owners retain their own Grace), the recommendation of `../q16-grace-investigation/final-report.md` (worktree `q16-grace-ownership-81ee86`). *The law (PAR-06):* Grace belongs to the owner whose written rows contain the marked occurrence — the root, a property body, a brace block in any position, a clause-branch body; output rows and deconstruction right-hand sides (`WrittenRows`), so hoisting never moves an occurrence to another owner. A marker is valid exactly when that occurrence becomes one of the owner's OWN inferred parameters; its weight is applied as far as the ordering allows, and saturation is never an error (`F = ~x + 1`, an already-first `~x`, an already-last `x~`, excess weight, a tie, a cancelled weight, and `A = p - q` / `F = A - z~` — `F(z, p, q)` — are valid). A fixed binding (explicit parameter or clause binder, enclosing parameter, property, builtin, opened name, structural member, `.string`) and every occurrence on a closed level (a written list; a clause branch's own rows) stay `InvalidGraceMarker`. *Branch nesting (PAT-04):* a branch is closed at its own level only; an algorithm nested in it is an ordinary owner in every spelling (`Apply(f) = f(1, 10)` / `F(0) = Apply({ y - ~x })` / `F(0)` is `9`, like the named `B = y - ~x`), its parameters never added to the branch, and a branch binder it captures is an enclosing parameter (`F(n) = Apply({ x - ~n })` is `InvalidGraceMarker`, one report). *Implementation (C#-only by design):* the parser's lexical branch-body Grace scan (`FindGraceSpan` / `ScanPendingForGraceSpan`) is deleted; the parameter detector's per-owner rewrite pass is the one authority (`GraceEffectPolicy.ClosedBranch` replaces `NotReported`); eligibility wording ("Grace cannot reorder 'x' because …"); `ApplyGraceReordering` unchanged; a load-bearing branch's own-level verdict comes when that deferred unit is selected (MOD-10). No evaluator, Lean-semantics, grammar or public-API change; Lean docstrings narrowed. **Q-16 RESOLVED BY DECISION; X-08 and X-39 RESOLVED; PV-28 RESOLVED BY DECISION;** oq LIFT-10 is closed; candidate **X-49** (PAR-06's ordering prose does not fully determine `ApplyGraceReordering`'s multi-marker order: `K = a * 100 + b~ * 10 + ~~c` is `K(a, c, b)`, without `b~` `K(c, a, b)`) is recorded open, not decided. Rules reconciled: PAR-06 (title, status, law, rule), PAT-04 (status, rule), DOT-06 and SYN-12 (wording), the PAR and PAT topic tails. 79 Q IDs: 40 resolved, 1 superseded, 1 partial, 37 fully open (38 with an open part; 1 before release). PV: 50 total, 0 open, 0 active settled violations, 0 partial, 21 resolved violations, 17 resolved by decision, 10 intentional, 2 refuted. X: 49 total, 37 resolved, 0 partial, 0 still-reproducing settled defects, 1 superseded, 11 open. § Decide before release: Q-33. Evidence: `../q16-grace-implementation/`.

> **Historical independent hostile review, 2026-10-07: NOT READY (superseded by the owner follow-up below).** Q-28's numeric equality and richest/first compatible representative decision (N-F), and Q-10's minimum display-filter decision (D-F), remain adopted. The blanket promise that permuting incompatible repeated-name contributions preserves the error category conflicts with NEED-04's immediate first-conflict rule. With `A = 1.0`, `B = 1.00`, `P(x,x,x) = x`, `P(A,B,2)` reports `TypeMismatch`, while `P(A,2,B)` reports `ArityMismatch` on all six C# routes. The integer instance reproduces in authoritative Lean Model-C too (`CoreTests.ModelC.mixedRepeatedConflicts`). The historical total helper theorem `repeated_name_failure_is_permutation_invariant` does not prove ordered Model-C failure invariance. This is a known unmet requirement/contradiction, not an alternative numeric equality or representation decision. The frozen rules have not been silently narrowed or changed; resolving the competing requirements needs an owner decision before readiness. Q-16 and Q-33 remain the two recorded pre-release Q IDs; this newly confirmed review blocker is additional to that inventory. Evidence: `../q28-q10-nf-df-codex-review/findings.md` and its final report.

> **Owner follow-up, 2026-10-07: prior review blocker resolved (its closing pre-release inventory is historical; superseded by the Q-16 resolution above, which leaves Q-33 alone before release).** Permuting all contributions of one repeated name preserves satisfiability. On success it preserves the bound VALUE up to `==`, callable identity and channel availability; among equally rich compatible contributions, the first in written traversal order supplies the retained representation. Failure reporting follows ordinary binding order (NEED-04, PAT-10, Q-27): when different incompatibilities coexist, permutation may change the error category, blamed contribution, payload, message or span, although every permutation still fails. Q-28 remains N-F, Q-10 remains D-F; NEED-04/PAT-10 runtime order and Q-27 taxonomy are unchanged. Only Q-16 and Q-33 remain decide-before-release questions; full closeout evidence: `../q28-q10-nf-df-codex-review/owner-decision-closeout/final-report.md`.

> **Q-28 + Q-10 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-07 (historical inventory; superseded by the Q-16 resolution above; committed as `66367a6`):** the owner decided Q-28 (N-F) and Q-10 (D-F); the decisions are independent. *Value, representation, presentation (Q-28 N-F; new VAL-11):* `==` — `Decimal128.Equals` at numeric leaves, recursively structural — is the language's one “same value” relation for `==`/`!=`, `contains`, `distinct`, numeric literal patterns, duplicate clause heads and repeated-name compatibility; a number's Decimal128 representation (quantum, zero sign, NaN sign) is data the value carries, never provenance, preserved by value-carrying operations and observable through `.string`; a repeated name keeps ONE richest compatible complete contribution (NEED-04) and, among equally rich ones, the FIRST in written traversal order, its representation included (`P(x, x) = x.string`: `P(1.5, 1.50)` is `'1.5'`, `P(1.50, 1.5)` is `'1.50'`; with `A = 1.50`, `P(1.5, A)` and `P(A, 1.5)` are `'1.50'`); Permuting all contributions of one repeated name preserves satisfiability. On success it preserves the bound VALUE up to `==`, callable identity and channel availability; among equally rich compatible contributions, the first in written traversal order supplies the retained representation. Failure reporting follows ordinary binding order (NEED-04, PAT-10, Q-27): when different incompatibilities coexist, permutation may change the error category, blamed contribution, payload, message or span, although every permutation still fails. Collections keep their own representative rules; COLL-06's `orderDesc` question and OP-13's same-signed quantum tie are not decided. No production change: N-F is the existing C# (`BindNeedPatterns`) and Lean (`bindNeedName`) behavior. *Composable display filters (Q-10 D-F):* every active `DisplayDecimals` setting is a presentation filter — the program root's own property (source) and `RunOptions.DefaultDisplayDecimals` / CLI `--display-decimals` (host) compose by their minimum; an absent filter imposes no limit, neither present is canonical display, and host `null` is no filter. The source filter stays an engine-level convention: `KatLangEngine` reads it after successful output with the ordinary cached value read in the same run, whatever the host filter is (it can trace, draw, spend steps, be cancelled or fail); raw `Evaluator.Run*` never reads it, an intentional boundary. The investigation recommended D-O (no source hook); the owner rejected D-O after the product-portability discussion and adopted D-F; the only production change is `KatLangEngine.CombineDisplayDecimals`. **Q-28 and Q-10 RESOLVED BY DECISION; X-28 and X-12 RESOLVED; X-35 RESOLVED BY DECISION (intentional engine/evaluator boundary);** oq NUM-01, FAM-09 and RUN-03 are closed. Rules reconciled: VAL-09, VAL-11 (new), PAT-05, PAT-09, PAT-10, CMP-05, COLL-06, COLL-07, OP-13, OP-14, FORMAL-04, the NEED-04 annex (wording), RUN-01, RUN-06 and RES-06; RUN-02 and RAND-01 already state the run-scoped read and are unchanged. 79 Q IDs: 39 resolved, 1 superseded, 1 partial, 38 fully open (39 with an open part; 2 before release). > **Q-19 + Q-29 + Q-31 + Q-32 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-06 (historical inventory; superseded by the Q-28 + Q-10 resolution above; committed as `83819f3`):** the owner decided Q-19 (D-I), Q-29 (A-U), Q-31 (H-P) and Q-32 (I-U) of `../q19-q29-q31-q32-open-module-identity/final-report.md` together as one law system for `open` and loaded modules. *Open providers (Q-19 D-I):* an `open` list counts the PROVIDERS its targets resolve to by the one callable identity — the declaration in its declaring scope, NEED-04's relation — never by spelling or position: `open Sub, Lib.Sub` for one declaration is one provider, `open M, M` and `open M, (M)` stay one, two written blocks or two declarations stay two however equal. *Static ambiguity (Q-29 A-U):* provider overlap is legal; a WRITTEN name whose lookup reaches an open level with two or more different providers is the front-end error `AmbiguousOpen` at the occurrence, whether or not evaluation demands it; a dot fallback name counts only when the edge certainly takes it (Q-75 MUST); a receiver-decided fallback and a host-built tree keep the run-time `AmbiguousOpen`. *Module hygiene (Q-31 H-P):* a loaded module is a source unit rooted at the prelude whatever holds it — its free names resolve in its own declarations and the prelude, never at the load site; a name it cannot resolve is its member's implicit parameter, and a site parameter reaches a member only by an explicit argument, bare forwarding or formula lifting — so `open 'u'` ≡ `M = load('u')` + `open M` ≡ `M.Member`. *Module identity (Q-32 I-U):* one canonical URL is ONE module declaration per compiled program — one elaborated module, one member binding, one callable identity, one open provider; holders stay bindings of their own for the module's output; distinct canonical URLs stay distinct. **Q-19, Q-29, Q-31 and Q-32 RESOLVED BY DECISION; X-20, X-26 and X-37 RESOLVED; PV-41 and PV-42 RESOLVED BY DECISION;** oq NAME-01, NAME-02, MOD-01 and MOD-02 are closed. The paired Lean/C# implementation: D-I in both languages; A-U in the C# front end (one new public `DiagnosticCode.AmbiguousOpen` mapped onto the existing `KatLangErrorCode.AmbiguousOpen`; Lean models no front-end rule, FORMAL-02); H-P and I-U in both (Lean `PropertyIdentity.module` wired at the chain root). Rules reconciled: RES-03, RES-04, RES-06, RES-07, MOD-06, MOD-07, MOD-08, MOD-09, MOD-10, PAR-03, PROP-07, PROP-08 and RAND-01; X-03 unchanged. 79 Q IDs: 37 resolved, 1 superseded, 1 partial, 40 fully open (41 with an open part; 4 before release). > **Q-17 + Q-18 + Q-75 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-05 (historical inventory; superseded by the Q-19 + Q-29 + Q-31 + Q-32 resolution above; committed as `8ead770`):** the owner decided Q-17 (S-C), Q-18 (C-B3) and Q-75 (F-A) of `../q17-q18-q75-dot-semantics/final-report.md` together as one identity-oriented dot semantics. *The route:* an ordinary non-spread edge `R.n(args?)` selects a DECLARED structural member `n` whatever its spelling — `string` included — with accessibility checked after selection; a member declared only in clause branches is `LocalOnlyProperty`; on a structural miss `n == string` is the number-to-text intrinsic, otherwise the edge is the extension fallback `n(R, args)`; a lexical `string` is never consulted by `.string`, and `R*.n(args)` stays `n(R*, args)`. *The identity:* a dot expression has ONE callable identity in every algorithm-capable position — supplied argument, forwarded parameter, builtin callback, loop step and callee: an argumentless structural path carries its member's own callable (aliases normalized), and every other dot expression is a calculation value; parentheses never change identity, so `(Box.G)(2)` is `Box.G(2)` and `(Box.V)()` is the fresh call `Box.V()`, and a call may follow only a name, a graced name, an argumentless dot edge or a redundant group of one (`X.Mk(1)()` is `UnseparatedSameLineItem`, like `Mk(1)()`). *The static obligation:* the existing `LexicalFallbackSelection` lattice decides — an inferring body collects a fallback name unless the selection is `Never`; a closed explicit list or clause-branch body checks it only when the selection is `Always` (`Get(obj) = 5.size` is the same `UndeclaredIdentifier` as `Get(obj) = size(5)`, at the member token, worded receiver-aware); a MAY-selected fallback stays a run-time question. **Q-17, Q-18 and Q-75 RESOLVED BY DECISION; X-21 RESOLVED; X-20 narrowed (its open-dedup row stays open for Q-19); PV-09, PV-15 and PV-22 RESOLVED BY DECISION;** oq DOT-01 and DOT-02 are closed. The paired Lean/C# implementation decides the route from one property lookup in both languages, routes the callee through the one CALLABLE projection (Lean `resolveCalleeAlg` over `projectNeedStructuralMember`; C# `ProjectDotPathCallable`, also read by the sequence optimizer), narrows the parser's call gate, retires the C#-only qualified-Math resolution, and checks a certain fallback name in the C# front end (Lean models no front-end rule, FORMAL-02). No public API member and no error or diagnostic code is added, removed or renumbered. Rules reconciled: DOT-01, DOT-02, DOT-03, DOT-04, DOT-05, DOT-08 and DOT-09 (rule texts), PAR-04 and ALG-02 (rule texts), RES-06, SYN-06, SYN-09, ALG-05 (rule text), ALG-07 (rule text), HO-01, PROP-06, PROP-07, RES-07, RES-09 and OP-11 (caveats, tensions, statuses); LOOP-08, NEED-06, CALL-05, FORMAL-02, FORMAL-08 and DOT-07 unchanged. 79 Q IDs: 33 resolved, 1 superseded, 1 partial, 44 fully open (45 with an open part; 8 before release). > **Q-06 + Q-27 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-05 (historical inventory; superseded by the Q-17 + Q-18 + Q-75 resolution above; committed as `ad3d7dd`):** the owner decided Q-06 (option (a)) and Q-27 (policy P2 of `../q27-q06-error-policy/final-report.md`) together as one evaluation-error taxonomy: *an evaluation error names the first semantic contract the executed operation cannot satisfy, in that operation's ordinary evaluation order.* Missing CALLABLE capability in any invoking position — a user higher-order parameter, a builtin callback, a `repeat`/`while` step, an invoking slot reached through a builtin used as a step — is `NotAnAlgorithm`, decided by CALLABLE projection only when execution needs to invoke the slot (never by demanding VALUE; an unused slot is never projected); a present VALUE of the wrong kind is `TypeMismatch` (`-()`, `sum((1, true))`, `A:[0]`, `range([1], 3)`, `take(xs, 'x')`); supply/output cardinality is `ArityMismatch`; a selection naming no position is `BadIndex`, `first`/`last` on an empty collection included, so `first(A) ≡ A:0` and `last(A) ≡ A:(count(A) - 1)` hold for every target, errors included; a zero divisor is `DivisionByZero` and every other valid-kind domain failure `IllegalInEval` (`min(())`, `take(xs, 1.5)`). A callable keeps its ordinary binder verdicts (`A = 7` / `map([1, 2], A)` is `ArityMismatch`), and NEED-04's repeated-name categories are unchanged. No public error-code member is added, removed or renumbered, and no successful program changes value, count, demand or effects. **Q-06 and Q-27 RESOLVED BY DECISION; X-11, X-30 and X-31 RESOLVED; PV-30 and PV-34 RESOLVED BY DECISION;** oq EFF-03 and ERR-01 are closed. The paired Lean/C# implementation changes the same functions in both languages (one invoking-slot projection, `projectInvokingSlot` / `ProjectInvokingSlot`; `expectInt`; unary minus; the collection-element check; the whole-number control; the empty policy as metadata, `requireSelectablePosition`); wording-only messages name the repeated-name and `while`-flag contracts, and builtin arity payloads carry their real minimum. Rules reconciled: FORMAL-06 (the taxonomy is constitutional), FORMAL-08, OP-04, OP-14, VAL-04, VAL-08, VAL-10, BOOL-03, CMP-05, SEQ-04 (the total selection law), SEQ-05, COLL-05 (the split empty policy), COLL-08, COLL-09, COLL-10, LOOP-05, LOOP-08, HO-01, HO-04, HO-05, SUP-07, CALL-03 and NEED-06 (a dated clarification, no new NEED law); wording-only quotes in PAT-09, PAT-10, LOOP-02, LOOP-03 and LOOP-06. 79 Q IDs: 30 resolved, 1 superseded, 2 partial, 46 fully open (48 with an open part; 11 before release). > **Q-23 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-04 (historical inventory; superseded by the Q-06 + Q-27 resolution above; committed as `927a8c4`):** the owner decided Q-23 (option Q23-B of `../q23-investigation/final-report.md`): *a loop step is an ordinary callable invoked over the current state supply* — any callable is eligible as a `while`/`repeat` step, and when an iteration executes, the current state slots are supplied to it through KatLang's ordinary invocation semantics; loops specialize only the iteration protocol and the row receiver. The paired Lean/C# implementation invokes each step through its own ordinary machinery — a user algorithm through its binder, a clause family through the ordinary family dispatcher (clause order, inspecting patterns, non-match versus failure), a builtin through its argument roles, an alias as its target — and reads a builtin's ONE result value as ONE next-state row (`()` included). Eligibility never depends on category, representation, clause count, spelling or planner support; a value with no callable identity is still no step (NEED-06), and a zero-iteration `repeat` never projects its step. **Q-23 RESOLVED BY DECISION; PV-12 RESOLVED;** X-23's message now describes only values and zero-parameter steps. Over the investigation's 1,796 probe variants no accepted program changed (461 formerly rejected programs now succeed, 189 fail with the callee's own ordinary failure, 19 are still rejected). Rules reconciled: LOOP-08 (replaced), LOOP-02 (title; binding generalized), LOOP-03 (a builtin's one row), LOOP-05 (never projected), LOOP-01 (Model-C cleanup: initial slots are supplied, not demanded), PAT-11 (a family is a step), HO-02 (caveat), RES-06, COLL-01 and SYN-05 (evidence, tension or the PV-12 reference). 79 Q IDs: 28 resolved, 1 superseded, 2 partial, 48 fully open (50 with an open part; 13 before release). > **Q-24 + Q-26 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-04 (historical inventory; superseded by the Q-23 resolution above; committed as `f6d978c`):** the owner decided Q-24 and Q-26 together as one loop cardinality law — *patterns bind, rows produce, `*` opens; none of the three changes the others* — and the paired Lean/C# implementation resolves PV-46, X-24 and X-33. **Q-24 RESOLVED BY DECISION — LOOP-04 packing removed:** a step's parameter patterns inspect and bind the incoming state only and never change how its output becomes the next state; an explicit spread in a step row has its ordinary meaning (it opens one value boundary and supplies the items), so `Dup(x, x) = { (x + 1, x + 1)* }` forms the same next state as `Dup(x, x) = { x + 1, x + 1 }`; repeated names, structural patterns and collectors constrain the next binding but never repack the outgoing state; LOOP-04 is retired into LOOP-03, and the pattern-derived packing flag and its machinery are deleted in Lean and C#. **Q-26 RESOLVED BY DECISION — loop results are ordinary value boundaries:** `while`/`repeat` return ONE value, the canonical capture of the final state's slots (`()`, the slot itself, or a sequence), with emitted count `Result.valueCount` like every other builtin and call result; a non-spread loop expression is one item at every receiver (root, body, capture, list, loop step, callback body) and only `L*` opens it — so `Fibonacci.repeat(10, 0, 1)` is one row `(55, 89)`, VAL-06/07/08/09 lose their loop exceptions and SUP-01 loses its "sole exception". The step's rows remain the loop's own next-state protocol, and `while` still reads its flag from their last item. **Q-23 stays OPEN**, narrowed to which callables may receive the incoming state and how they are dispatched; no callable category gained or lost acceptance. Rules reconciled: LOOP-02, LOOP-03 (LOOP-04 merged), LOOP-07, LOOP-08 (evidence), SUP-01, SUP-02, VAL-06, VAL-07, VAL-08, VAL-09, HO-03 (caveat), NEED-04 (clarification, no new law). 79 Q IDs: 27 resolved, 1 superseded, 2 partial, 49 fully open (51 with an open part; 14 before release). > **Q-09 resolution and implementation, 2026-10-04 (historical inventory; superseded by the Q-24 + Q-26 resolution above; committed as `3fbaad9`):** the owner decided Q-09 by splitting it, and the implementation fixes PV-07 and closes X-34. **Q-09a ACCEPTED — host-stack headroom is host policy:** whether the host-stack backstop stops a run may depend on the execution route, genuine suspension, the host thread and its stack size, the build, the runtime and its JIT state, and the platform; KatLang promises the language-visible failure kind `EvaluationStackExhausted`, never a recursion depth or equal physical-stack capacity across routes or environments. **Q-09b ACCEPTED — limits observe a run; they never choose how it runs:** configuring, raising or removing a limit that the run does not reach changes nothing about that run — not its value, effects, budget accounting, or whether it completes; the limit-selected execution strategy is rejected. **PV-07 RESOLVED:** `Evaluator.CreateRootCtx` no longer disables loop planning or sequence fusion because `MaxSteps`, `MaxMaterializedItems`, `MaxStringLength` or `MaxMaterializedStringChars` is configured; every strategy instead charges every budget identically (a planned loop charges the generic per-iteration step and exactly the generic expression-work checkpoints; the fused filter→count pipeline records the elided stage heads and reserves, without allocating, the generic composition's items), so steps, raw checkpoints, peak depth, materialized items and string units are equal on every strategy, exactly. The witness `R(n) = if(n > 0, range(n, n).filter({ R(x - 1) >= 0 }).count, 0)` / `R(35)` (Release, 1.5 MiB thread) is `ok 1` under all 17 probed configurations, formerly `EvaluationStackExhausted` under any of the four former selectors. **X-34 RESOLVED:** the public documentation states both directions of the twin's headroom and no longer calibrates `MaxSupportedDepth` against the host stack (mechanically pinned). RUN-01 (exceptions 1 and 2, boundary, known tension), RUN-02 (its determinism clause) and RUN-03 (strategy-neutral semantic limits; the host stack as an environmental backstop) are reconciled; no other rule text changes, and Lean is unchanged (it models no limits, strategies, host stack, async or JIT). 79 Q IDs: 25 resolved, 1 superseded, 2 partial, 51 fully open (53 with an open part; 16 before release). PV: 50 total, 3 open, 0 active settled violations, 0 partial, 19 resolved violations, 9 resolved by decision, 17 intentional, 2 refuted. X: 48 total, 23 resolved, 0 partial, 0 still-reproducing settled defects, 1 superseded, 24 open. Evidence: `../q09-implementation/final-report.md`, `../q09-implementation/evidence/constitution-check.json`. Earlier dated inventories below are historical snapshots.

> **Q-61 closeout, 2026-10-03 (historical inventory; superseded by the pre-release re-baseline above):** Q-61 is RESOLVED by the existing no-renaming law (FWD-02, unchanged) and the deferred compilation-unit law (MOD-10). A load-bearing branch is one deferred compilation unit: parsed eagerly, elaborated and validated as a whole when selected. Once a unit is elaborated, ordinary front-end rules apply everywhere in it, open targets included (MOD-06), and runtime call-by-need never turns declaration invalidity into laziness (RUN-04, NEED-05). The historical eager/deferred witness (`N(y) = y` / `public P(x) = N` / `public X = 1`) compared a module in PROPERTY position compiled eagerly with the same module in OPEN-TARGET position compiled deferred; with the position held fixed, eager and selected-deferred compilation agree. The open-target gap is the new X-48: the C# resolver walks open targets with its diagnostics sink disabled, so `UnforwardableParameter`, `UnforwardableCallable`, `UnliftableClauseFamily` and the inferred-signature `InvalidCollectingBinding` are suppressed there (parser, detector and validator diagnostics are not); it still reproduces and is not fixed by this closeout. 79 Q IDs: 19 resolved, 1 superseded, 1 partial, 58 fully open (59 with an open part; 25 before release). PV: 50 total, 4 open, 0 active settled violations, 0 partial, 18 resolved violations, 5 resolved by decision, 21 intentional, 2 refuted. X: 48 total, 18 resolved, 0 partial, 1 still-reproducing settled defect (X-48), 1 superseded, 28 open. All other dispositions are unchanged. Evidence: `../q61-eager-deferred-validation/final-report.md`, `../q61-closeout/final-report.md`, `../q61-closeout/evidence/constitution-check.json`. > **Revision 2026-10-01 (alias as binding indirection IMPLEMENTED; Q-77 resolved; X-45 and X-47 resolved; working tree over `main` @ `06bab50`, uncommitted).** The binding-indirection alias the alias-indirection investigation decided is implemented in C# and Lean: a closed `Algorithm` variant (`Algorithm.Alias`; Lean `Algorithm.alias`) whose target the evaluator normalizes once (`Evaluator.ResolveAliasTarget`; Lean `resolveAliasTarget`), identity-keyed lone-row eligibility (`DeclaresParameterizedStructure`; Lean `Algorithm.declaresParameterizedStructure`), and the wrapper machinery deleted (the inherited signature, the rebuilt call, `InheritsCalleeSignature`, Lean `sourceCall` and `aliasesLoneBareReference`). The owner decided Q-77 as option (a): a closed lone row whose callee — directly or through an alias — has no forwarding contract (an unnameable clause family) is the front-end error `UnforwardableCallable`. X-45 (with its closed-list half: Q-15's strict-value trigger follows the alias target) and X-47 are resolved; X-46 stays superseded. FWD-02's status and *Eligibility* bullet are updated in the draft and the summary; in the draft also its examples, two boundary bullets and the evidence. No rule ID, class or status category changes; the Summary counts are unchanged. Evidence: `alias-binding-implementation/implementation-report.md`.
>
> **Revision 2026-10-01 (alias as binding indirection; Q-78 resolved; Q-77 narrowed; investigation only, pushed `main` @ `06bab50`).** FWD-02's alias half is re-decided. `A = F` — an open, non-root lone row — whose resolved callable identity DECLARES PARAMETERIZED CALLABLE STRUCTURE (any builtin; any clause family, nameable or not; a user algorithm with at least one parameter pattern) is a CALLABLE ALIAS: a binding of its own (declaration, exposure, zero-argument cache entry, no members) whose callable target is that identity, so calls, argument roles, the lifting signature, the forwarding contract, arity, dispatch, async behavior and callable identity are the target's — no wrapper algorithm and no inherited signature. A zero-parameter target keeps the ordinary value read. The callable-capability round's wrapper-relative conclusion (no exact alias for builtins and families) is superseded for aliases; its bare-forwarding conclusions stand. Q-78 is resolved by the model (an alias's roles are its target's); Q-77 survives only for a closed lone row into a callable with no forwarding contract (an unnameable family). The C# wrapper is recorded as the new X-47 (still reproducing; it supersedes X-46). No rule ID, class or status category changes; the Summary counts are unchanged. Evidence: `alias-indirection/final-report.md` (6,273 programs on six routes at HEAD and on a scratch binding-indirection prototype; an alias-equivalence corpus of 1,320 points with 0 semantic differences; Lean 296/296 on identity-sharing encodings).
>
> **Revision 2026-10-01 (the callable-capability investigation; Q-12 and Q-13 resolved; investigation only, pushed `main` @ `06bab50`).** FWD-02's *Eligibility* is decided: which callables a lone row may alias or bare-forward is decided by the resolved callable identity's capabilities, never by the lookup route or spelling. A FORWARDING contract (a static, named top-level parameter interface — the identity's lifting signature) admits bare forwarding: every user algorithm (opened, dotted, module, Math-member and host-operation identities included), every builtin and every nameable clause family. An EXACT-ALIAS contract (the identity's acceptance and evaluation is the ordinary binder over one static pattern list) admits an exact alias: every user algorithm, whatever the route, but no builtin (argument roles: lazy `if`, never-demanded callbacks and steps, the arity verdict first, the loops' minimum) and no clause family (dispatch). The C# front end still applies the route-based eligibility (new X-46, settled oracle). New open questions: Q-77 (what a lone row means when its callee lacks the capability its body form needs) and Q-78 (whether an exact alias takes its callee's call-site argument roles; new contradiction X-45, pre-existing for Math aliases). No rule ID, class or status category changes; the Summary counts are unchanged. Evidence: `callable-capabilities/final-report.md` (3,613 programs on six routes, at HEAD and on a scratch tree with identity-keyed eligibility; Lean on the elaborated programs).

> **Revision 2026-09-28 (Q-01 decided, PV-04 fixed).** The owner decided open question Q-01: *within one call, a written argument slot is evaluated at most once for its value; if that evaluation succeeds, that is its value, and if it fails, that is its failure; reading the bound parameter never causes the argument expression to execute again. Argument evaluation uses the proper semantic route once; parameter reads reuse its outcome and never replay its source expression.* The rule was implemented in Lean and C# in the working tree over `3e57a08` (not yet committed): a failed slot's failure is now recorded beside its algorithm channel (Lean `AlgBinding.valueFailure?`, C# the `AlgEnv` `ValueError`, both built by `slotAlgorithmBinding` / `SlotAlgorithmBinding`) and every value read of the parameter reuses it. CALL-06 (retitled; same ID) now states the rule normatively; the text of CALL-02, PAR-01, VAL-10, VAR-04, PROP-04, PROP-05, PROP-07, PROP-09, HO-01, HO-04, RUN-07, RUN-08, RAND-01 and RAND-02 that treated the re-derivation as an exception or a tension now cross-references CALL-06 instead, and the CALL, PROP, ALGHO and RAND open-question lists and coverage row H06 record the resolution. Validation of the fixed tree (V3): `validate-all.ps1 -Phase DotNet` — solution build 0 warnings, KatLang.Tests 23,410, KatLang.CLI.Tests 296, KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests 133, all passed, `git diff --check` clean; `-Phase Lean` — all seven targets built; both phases left the repository unchanged. PV-04 is removed from PROP-04, PROP-07, PROP-09 and RAND-02, and its record moved to `open-questions.md` § Resolved violations. Every example changed by the decision was re-run with the five-path probe runner rebuilt from the fixed tree (`scratchpad/probes/q01-constitution/`); results quoted as "formerly" are the `a2700f4` observations. Every other line citation still refers to `a2700f4`. (The Q-01 implementation was later committed as `33c9971`.)
>
> **Revision 2026-09-28 (Q-02 decided, PV-06 fixed).** The owner decided open question Q-02 with its option (a): *every resource-limit failure actually encountered during evaluation is terminal for the run. Limits may stop a run; they must never redefine a successful result.* The rule was implemented in C# only — Lean models no resource limits, and its unlimited meaning is the reference that every successful limited run must equal, so Lean needed no edit — and committed as `c6813ef` on branch `claude/lucid-mccarthy-9yy1s2`; `main` carries the identical tree as `a4ae04a` (both over `33c9971`). One predicate, `Evaluator.IsDeferrableEvaluationFailure`, now decides every deferral of a failure (user-call argument assembly, the builtin adapter's eager attempt and value-slot demand, and the fused filter-count pipeline, in the synchronous evaluator and the async twin), and the former `RetainResourceLimitForAlgorithmBinding` is deleted.
> - RUN-03 (same ID and title) now owns the terminal-limit law. CALL-06 records only an ORDINARY failure as a slot's outcome. RUN-01, RUN-02, RUN-07, RUN-08, RAND-01, RAND-02 and VAL-10 (rule text), CALL-02, CALL-03 and MOD-10 (caveats), COLL-02 and DOT-09 (known tensions), the CALL, RUN and MOD non-constitutional lists and open questions, the consolidated § Deliberately non-constitutional areas, and the coverage rows E20, E22, H06, J11, K03 and K06 now defer to RUN-03 instead of describing the absorbed-limit tension. Q-01's at-most-once law is unchanged: an ordinary failure is still evaluated once, recorded and never re-derived.
> - PV-06 is removed from RUN-01, RUN-02, RUN-03, RAND-01 and RAND-02, and its record moved to `open-questions.md` § Resolved violations. PV-07 stays open, narrowed: a route or configuration can still stop with a resource-limit failure where another completes, but no longer with a different success; RAND-02 no longer lists it. The new PV-50 and open question Q-71 record that a source-processing limit refusing a deferred module at evaluation time is still an ordinary, deferrable load error (RUN-03, MOD-10).

> **Independent Q-02 hostile review, 2026-09-28:** RUN-03 now separates evaluator terminality, preflight/rendering, the still-open PV-07 headroom goal, and Q-71's unprobed source-loading boundary. > **Revision 2026-09-28 (Q-03 decided, PV-03 fixed).** The owner decided open question Q-03 with the equivalent of its option (a): *if a callable works with no arguments, using its name alone reads its cached value, even if it declares optional or collecting parameters; use `A()` when you want to evaluate it again and get a fresh value.* Formally, implicit lifting skips a callable in value context whenever an ordinary call supplying zero arguments can already bind it: its bare reference is a property-style value demand (CALL-05) that follows the zero-argument property cache (PROP-04, PROP-05), and it is never rewritten into a fresh forwarding call merely because the callable declares parameters. Lifting and forwarding are unchanged for callables that REQUIRE supplied arguments, and explicit calls stay fresh (PROP-06). The rule was implemented in the working tree over `df3f8b0` (not yet committed). One predicate, `ParameterPattern.AcceptsZeroSuppliedSlots`, now decides both the evaluator's acceptance (`Evaluator.AcceptsZeroSuppliedArguments`) and every implicit-lifting arm (`ImplicitArgumentResolver.RequiresSuppliedArguments` over `CallableSignature.AcceptsZeroSuppliedArguments`), replacing the resolver's `Parameters.Count > 0` gates; neither evaluator changed. Lean gained the surface specification `liftsBareValueReference`, the proved laws `bare_reference_lifts_iff_not_cacheable` and `unlifted_bare_reference_is_an_accepted_cacheable_demand`, and CoreTests guards that evaluate the Q-03 tree against the superseded lifted one.
> - PAR-07 and FWD-02 (both retitled; same IDs) state the rule. FWD-02's former clause "aliases of collecting callables are collecting" is superseded: a bare alias of a callable that works with no arguments is a zero-parameter property holding its value, and a forwarding alias is written (`Alias(*xs) = Cnt(xs*)`); forwarding by shape now reaches only a grouped stream `H((*xs))`. PAR-04, PROP-04 and PROP-05 cite the rule in their rule text; CALL-05 in a caveat; the CALL, VAR and PROP open-question lists and the coverage rows G05, G06 and H05 record the resolution. The two-collector tension of VAR-02/FWD-02 (X-02) is unchanged but its witness is re-stated with callees that require an argument, since the collecting-only witness no longer lifts. Clause families are unchanged: their lifting signature is empty, which the predicate accepts, so they are still never lifted (PV-14, Q-13 open).
> - PV-03 is removed from CALL-05, PAR-07, FWD-02, PROP-04 and PROP-05, and its record moved to `open-questions.md` § Resolved violations; X-15 (oq LIFT-01) is resolved and Q-03 moved to § Already resolved. PV-27 (the closed-list strict-value diagnostic rejecting a collecting-only callee) is resolved by the same mechanism — such a callee is never a forwarding candidate, so nothing is blocked — and is removed from CALL-05 and PAR-04.
> - The hostile review of the change found one more "declares parameters" test, in the evaluator: a collecting binding reported a FAILED read of a callable that accepts zero supplied arguments as "a supplied argument is a callable" (`TypeMismatch`) where a zero-parameter property surfaces its own error. > **Revision 2026-09-29 (Q-04 decided 2026-09-28, PV-01 resolved).** The owner decided open question Q-04 with its option (a) and fixed the boundary with the alternative called "parameters only" (the alternatives "also properties/opens" and "everything including builtins" were rejected): *automatic parameter forwarding must not change what an existing name refers to. Automatic forwarding reuses parameter bindings only. Properties, opened names and builtins are not forwarded as parameters.* When a lifted reference needs a callee parameter `n`, its argument is the binding a written parameter reference of `n` already denotes at the reference: the body's own written or inferred parameter, or an accessible captured parameter of an enclosing owner, a clause-branch binder included. Only an INFERRING body with no such binding receives a new (forwarded) parameter, and a forwarded parameter is never what a written name denotes. Name resolution (what a written identifier denotes) and automatic parameter forwarding (which existing parameter binding satisfies a callee parameter) are separate operations, and forwarding never re-selects a written name's binding. The rule was implemented in the working tree over `13196f8` (not yet committed): `ImplicitArgumentResolver` computes the reusable enclosing bindings of each level (`ForwardableParameters`) and marks the parameters it adds (`Algorithm.User.ForwardingParameterStart`); the ownership-completion pass `ParameterDetector.CompleteOwnership` (#158, `3f57517`), which re-selected written names against the completed signatures, is deleted; the editor model declares only name-resolution parameters. Neither evaluator changed. Lean gained `OwnerLevel.forwarded` (collision-only: it joins `conflictingOwnedNames`, never the owner walk), the surface specification `forwardingSource` with six proved laws in `KatLangArityLaws.lean`, and `CoreTests/ForwardingBindings.lean`, which evaluates the decided elaborations against the superseded ones.
> - PAR-07 (retitled; same ID) states the law and its boundary. PAR-04 (a closed list forwards an accessible enclosing parameter, superseding the K1-04 refusal), PAR-05 (a reused binding is not appended), FWD-02 (the reused source binding's kind decides the spread), RES-01, RES-04, RES-05 and RES-07 cite it; the PAR open-question list and § Deliberately non-constitutional areas record the resolution.

Its record moved to `open-questions.md` § Resolved violations as "resolved by decision"; X-16 (oq LIFT-02) is resolved and Q-04 moved to § Already resolved.

> **Revision 2026-09-29 (Q-05 decided, PV-13 and X-17 resolved).** The owner decided open question Q-05 with its option (b), extended to every occurrence: *repeated-name matching may restrict a binding; it must never manufacture a binding that no individual argument supplied. A repeated parameter name is a compatibility constraint over independently supplied arguments; it never merges incomplete contributions into a new binding.* Every occurrence supplies its own value; the values must be equal; a failed required value propagates (never replaced by another occurrence's value or by an unrelated pattern or type error); a callable-only contribution cannot satisfy the constraint; several algorithm channels must share one callable identity (unchanged); and channels from different occurrences are never spliced together. The rule was implemented in Lean and C# together in the working tree over `abe1383` (not yet committed): a top-level capture whose name repeats at its pattern level needs its own slot's value when it binds (`ParameterPattern.repeatsAtLevel` / `RepeatsAtLevel`), so a valueless contribution is a binding failure with its own recorded outcome, and the algorithm-only verdict is deleted.
> - PAT-09 (retitled; same ID, class and status category) states the law. PAT-10 (a valueless occurrence precedes every verdict; the within-merge tie-break is now value before callable identity) and CALL-06 (a repeated parameter name demands the slot's value; its former "binds the value" caveat) cite it. The PAT open questions on the family asymmetry (PV-43) and Decimal128 representatives (Q-28) stay open; the latter now also records the one residue of the no-splicing rule (`P(1.5, A)` with `A = 1.50` keeps `1.5` beside A's channel).

> **Revision 2026-09-29 (Q-72 decided; reversed the same day, see the next revision).** The owner decided the post-audit open question Q-72 with its option (c): *A callable whose parameter patterns contain repeated binding names must not participate in implicit parameter forwarding/lifting. The programmer must write an explicit wrapper/call.* The invariant it protects, stated beside Q-05: *Repeated occurrences represent independently supplied inputs whose compatibility is checked at binding time. No implicit transformation may collapse those occurrences into one input unless the source explicitly supplies that same input to each occurrence.* Automatic forwarding supplies arguments BY NAME, so for a callee such as `P(x, x) = x` it fed every occurrence from one binding (`Alias = P` elaborated to `Alias(x) = P(x, x)`; `Q(x) = P` to `Q(x) = P(x, x)`), and P's equality check could never fail through the alias. The C# front end now refuses such a reference in every lifting position, open and closed bodies alike, with the new front-end error `RepeatedParameterNotForwardable` (`DiagnosticCode` 47, `KatLangErrorCode` 69) at the reference, and the refused reference gives its owner no parameter. Lean specifies the decision (`refusesImplicitForwarding`, the three-way `bareValueReferenceTreatment`, four proved laws) and evaluates the written forms; it models no signature construction. Implemented in the working tree over `abe1383`, together with Q-05 (not yet committed).
> - PAR-07 (rule, examples, evidence) and FWD-02 (the lifted-parameters paragraph, why fundamental; its Q-72 known-tension bullet is removed) state the rule; PAR-05 (the lifted-name merge never meets two occurrences of one callee) and PAT-09 (the invariant) cite it. No rule's ID, class or status category changes, so the counts in the Summary below are unchanged.
> - Q-72 moved to `open-questions.md` § Already resolved. Examples added by this revision are rows of `RepeatedNameCalleeLiftingTests` or canonical spec cases (`repeated-name-callee-is-never-forwarded-implicitly`, `repeated-name-callee-is-called-explicitly`), re-run with the CLI built from the final tree; results marked "formerly" are observations on the Q-05 tree over `abe1383`.

> **Revision 2026-09-29 (Q-72 reversed: implicit forwarding is by binding name).** The owner reversed the Q-72 decision the same day in favor of the general rule: *Implicit forwarding is by binding name, regardless of how many times that name occurs in a callee's parameter patterns.* A caller owns one binding for a given parameter name; if a callee uses that name in several parameter positions, implicit forwarding supplies the caller's one binding to every occurrence — the composition rule formulas already follow across callees (`H = F + G` with `F(x)` and `G(x)` is `H(x) = F(x) + G(x)`), now without an exception within one callee (`P(x, x) = x` / `Some = P` is `Some(x) = P(x, x)`; `Some(7)` gives `7`). Its relation to Q-05, which stands unchanged: *Repeated names constrain independently supplied arguments. When implicit forwarding supplies the same caller binding to several occurrences, each occurrence receives that same complete binding, and no synthetic value/callable merge occurs.* The Q-05 and Q-72 work had meanwhile been committed as `515ed3c`; the reversal was committed as `c719f43`. The C# front end drops the refusal and everything that existed only for it — the resolver gate and its report, the signature query `CallableSignature.RepeatedParameterName` with its template fact, and the two public diagnostic codes (`DiagnosticCode` 47, `KatLangErrorCode` 69; KatLang is pre-release, and their numbers are left unused) — and its by-name merge now also skips a name repeated inside one group (`P((x, x))` lifts `(x)`, where the pre-Q-72 merge lifted one caller parameter per occurrence). Lean drops the refusal's specification (`refusesImplicitForwarding`, the three-way `bareValueReferenceTreatment` and its four laws), states the by-name rule on `forwardingSource`, and evaluates the elaborated forwarding trees in CoreTests. The Q-05 binder, including its linear-time repetition index, is untouched.
> - PAR-07 (rule condition, the by-name paragraph replacing "No forwarding into a repeated-name callee", examples, decision record, evidence), FWD-02 (lifted parameters, why fundamental), PAR-05 (one binding name, one lifted parameter) and PAT-09 (forwarded occurrences) state the rule. No rule's ID, class or status category changes, so the counts in the Summary below are unchanged.
> - Q-72's record in `open-questions.md` § Already resolved records the reversal. Examples added by this revision are rows of `ImplicitForwardingByBindingNameTests` or canonical spec cases (`implicit-forwarding-is-by-binding-name`, `repeated-name-wrapper-keeps-independent-arguments`), run with the CLI built from the final tree; results marked "under Q-72" are those pinned at `515ed3c` by the removed `RepeatedNameCalleeLiftingTests`.

The three were one defect family in the collection-builtin argument adapter and were repaired together in Lean and C# (*builtin call assembly respects argument roles*), in the working tree over `c719f43` (not yet committed): the adapter handles each written slot by the role its position has in the builtin's metadata — a VALUE slot is demanded once and its outcome is final, a CALLBACK slot is never value-evaluated merely because it was supplied, and a SPREAD slot either supplies exactly its items or its failure is the call's failure before the arity check.

> **Revision 2026-09-29 (PV-08 fixed; FWD-02 amended).** No new decision was needed, and no new rule is introduced: FWD-02 and PAR-07 already required an alias to mean the explicit call it abbreviates, and values-stay-values forbade forwarding from opening a value. FWD-02 — the owner of implicit forwarding — is amended so that it forbids PV-08 directly: *implicit forwarding preserves the source bindings it forwards and the exact structural item supply they stand for; when a forwarding pattern has opened an input into items, forwarding reconstructs exactly those items — each fixed binding one item, each collecting binding its collected items — preserving cardinality, zero and one item included, and never opening a matched item again.* The C# front end rebuilt a callee's sequence-value group as a normalizing capture, which collapses a one-item supply into its item, so the callee opened an already-matched value once more (`S((x)) = x` / `A = S` / `A([[7]])` was `7`, while `S([[7]])` is `[7]`). It now rebuilds the group from exactly its items — the C# carrier is an exact list (`ImplicitArgumentResolver.BuildPatternArgument`), which the rule does not prescribe — in the working tree over `bb0e543` (not yet committed). No syntax is added, `(…)` pattern semantics are unchanged, and VAL-03, SEQ-01, SEQ-03 and SUP-06 keep their meaning; Lean needed no semantic edit, because it models no argument construction.

> **Revision 2026-09-29 (the structural-pattern model decided; PV-08 superseded; Q-20 resolved).** The owner decided the final structural-pattern model: *structural pattern delimiters select the value kind they destructure. `(p1, …, pn)` matches sequence values only and `[p1, …, pn]` list values only; neither structurally matches the other kind, and neither treats a scalar as a one-item structure; a bare pattern binds the whole value; an explicit sequence pattern with exactly one non-collecting item is invalid, because KatLang has no one-item sequence value, while a collector-only `(*xs)` stays valid and sequence-only; `[x]` is the one-element pattern and `[*xs]` covers every list cardinality; ordinary patterns and clause families follow the same law.* Forwarding follows: *implicit forwarding preserves existing bindings without adding a structural boundary, reconstructs a copied structural pattern as its own kind, re-spreads collecting bindings only where the forwarding relation requires their items, and never converts between kinds.* Implemented in Lean and C# together in the working tree over `bb0e543` (not yet committed); the PV-08 exact-list repair of the previous revision is superseded in the same tree.
> - PAT-06 (retitled; same ID; class gains SYNTAX / GROUPING RULE; status "Current — see open question" → "Current") is now the ONE owner of the kind law and the singleton rule for both pattern languages; its former family-only text is superseded. PAT-08 (retitled; same ID, class and status) keeps one-slot, one-level opening and defers the kind to PAT-06; its list opening and #126's scalar one-item fallback are superseded. FWD-02 (retitled; same ID, class and status) replaces the exact-list reconstruction by same-kind reconstruction, adds "a binding the caller already owns fills the occurrence it names", and extends forwarding by shape to `([*xs])`. SEQ-03 (retitled; same ID, class and status) separates the kind-blind openers from the kind-specific structural patterns; its family exception and known tension are gone.

The coverage appendix row O07 names the new openers.

> **Revision 2026-09-29 (the alias and wrapper rules decided; FWD-02 rewritten; Q-73 resolved for aliases).** The owner decided the final alias, forwarding and parameter-inference model: *`A = F` aliases F exactly; `A(params) = F` calls F using A's own parameter supply; `A = F(exprs)` infers A's parameters from the free names written in `exprs`, then executes exactly `F(exprs)`; `A(params) = F(exprs)` is an explicit ordinary call.* A bare alias preserves F's callable signature and behavior; a definition with an explicit source parameter list and a bare callable body forwards that source parameter supply as an ordinary call; forwarding never inspects or reconstructs the destination callable's internal parameter patterns by matching binder names; explicit call expressions are authoritative, and parameter inference comes from the names written in them. The structural-pattern model is unchanged. The C# front end now completes a non-root body whose ONE written row is a bare reference to a callable that declares parameters before the closed-list gate and formula lifting (`ImplicitArgumentResolver.TryCompleteLoneCalleeRow`); Lean specifies the reconstruction as surface support (`aliasesLoneBareReference`, `ParameterPattern.sourceArguments`, `sourceCall`, ten laws, `CoreTests/AliasForwarding.lean`). Implemented in the working tree over `bb0e543`, on top of the uncommitted structural-pattern work (not yet committed).
> - FWD-02 (retitled; same ID, class and status category) is the ONE owner of the alias, wrapper and written-call rules and keeps the binding and kind preservation every synthesized call obeys; no separate alias rule is introduced. PAR-07 (retitled: formulas; same ID, class and status) excludes the lone row and states by-name forwarding for formulas only; PAR-04 (retitled; same ID, class and status) states the wrapper beside by-name formula forwarding; PAR-05 says an exact alias infers nothing, and its pattern-shape examples became formulas; RES-05 gains the alias exemption as a rule bullet (an exact alias's inherited names are checked against no property; Lean's `OwnerLevel` records the same modeling, with two new rows in the `CoreTests/NameOwnership.lean` conformance table), and FWD-02's boundary records that a deconstruction beside the bare callee makes the body a formula. Two sentences left stale by the structural-pattern revision are corrected on the way: PAR-07's lift lists for `P(x, (x, a))` and `P((x, x))`, and its `G((1, 2))` result (the `+` TypeMismatch, not `ArityMismatch`, since the singleton group became its binder). >
> **Revision 2026-09-30 (bare forwarding by name decided; FWD-02 finalized; Q-74 resolved).** The owner finalized the forwarding model (specification "Finalize KatLang alias, by-name forwarding, and formula-lifting semantics"), superseding the previous revision's WRAPPER half: *`A = F` is an exact alias; `A(p) = F` reuses an existing compatible binding named `p` — it does not rename and does not infer anything new; `A(p) = F(p)` is an explicit ordinary call, whose caller and callee parameter names need not match; `A = F(p)` infers `p` because `p` is written; `A = F + 1` is formula lifting.* Bare forwarding reuses already-existing compatible source bindings by name; it never renames a binding, never creates a new binding inside an explicit parameter list, never matches by position, and never converts an incompatible top-level parameter pattern because nested leaf names coincide. The C# front end (`ImplicitArgumentResolver.BareForwardingArgument` over `BareForwardingSources`, in `TryCompleteLoneCalleeRow`) supplies each callee parameter from the body's own top-level parameter of the same name and pattern or, for a name the body binds nowhere, the enclosing parameter binding (Q-04), and reports every unsupplied one as the new front-end error `UnforwardableParameter` (`DiagnosticCode` 49, `KatLangErrorCode` 71); the exact alias is unchanged; by-shape stream forwarding is now a formula rule only. Lean specifies bare forwarding as surface support (`ParameterPattern.sameContract(s)`, `bareForwardingArgument`, `bareForwardingArguments`, `bareForwardingRow`, `Pattern.bareForwardingOwn`, eighteen laws) and removes the positional branch reconstruction (`Pattern.sourceArgument(s)`, `Pattern.headSourceArguments` and its law). Implemented in the working tree over `bb0e543`, on top of the uncommitted structural-pattern and alias work (not yet committed).
> - FWD-02 (retitled; same ID, class and status category) states the final rule, with the owner's six-form table and the contributor rule *do not equate `A(p) = F` with `A(p) = F(p)`*. PAR-04 states *an explicit parameter list is closed; bare forwarding may reuse compatible existing bindings by name but never introduces a new binding into that list*, with the canonical examples and the closed formula (`F(q)` / `A(p) = F + 1` is the runtime `ArityMismatch`, nothing renamed or added). PAR-07 is formula-oriented and no longer explains a closed lone row. PAR-05 adds that a destination's parameter order never reorders an explicit source list. PAT-09 states that bare forwarding `Q(x) = P` reuses Q's one binding for both occurrences, `P(x, x)`. The FWD topic's open-question list resolves the composite exception and revises the `UseFixedOther` note. FORMAL-07's living counts are recounted (271 theorem declarations; CoreTests 1,609 guards; LanguageSpecCases 264 + 1; SemanticExplorerCases 2,445 + 2).
> - `open-questions.md`: Q-74 is resolved as the deliberate distinction (formula lifting → by-name dependency inference; an explicit closed parameter list → by-name reuse only, no inference; an explicit call → ordinary written argument mapping) and moved to § Already resolved with its original record; the inventory is recounted (74 IDs, 65 open, 9 resolved); PV-08 gains its final stage and the owner's six-stage history; PV-03, PV-14, PV-21, PV-26, Q-03, Q-04, Q-11, Q-12, Q-13, Q-14 and Q-72 are re-baselined (36 witnesses re-run with the CLI built from the final tree against the positional tree: 32 unchanged; the four that change are lone rows and change by decision, three of them back to their pre-wrapper values). No open PV or question was resolved or opened by fallout.
> - No rule ID is added or retired and no class or status category changes, so the counts in the Summary below are unchanged.
> - Examples added or changed by this revision were run with the CLI built from the final tree (`artifacts/validation/bare-forwarding/probes/`); results marked "formerly" or "the positional reading" are observations on the previous revision's tree (`artifacts/validation/alias-forwarding/probes/rebaseline.out`). The six-route C# suites `AliasAndBareForwardingTests` and `AliasAndBareForwardingHostileReviewTests` carry the route-equality evidence.
> - Validation (V3, on the final candidate): `validate-all.ps1 -Phase DotNet` — full solution build 0 warnings / 0 errors; KatLang.Formatting.PublicApi.Tests 133/133, KatLang.CLI.Tests 296/296, KatLang.Tests 26,126/26,128 — the two failures (`CallableSignatureTests.ImplicitResolver_VariadicForwarding_StillForwardsTheStream`, `StaticOpenOwnershipTests.ParameterOwnedOpenHead_IsRejectedStaticallyAtTheHead_AndNeverBypassed("enclosing")`) were expectations of the superseded reading (a differently named collector now forwards nothing and reads the cached `0`; a rejected open's unprovided name now makes the closed lone row `UnforwardableParameter`), repaired in the test files only and rerun (12/12); `git diff --check` clean; then `-Phase Lean` — all seven targets built from source with no warnings. Both phases left the repository unchanged. Logs: `../bare-forwarding/v3-dotnet.log`, `../bare-forwarding/rerun-failing.log`, `../bare-forwarding/v3-lean.log`.

## B16 reentrant deferred selection — 2026-11-10 (owner decision: fail fast)

Owner decision: B16 option (b), narrow fail-fast detection. This supersedes the earlier keep-and-link recommendation, which was never recorded as a rule. A selection made from inside a module download of its own loader's in-progress deferred materialization is now a `LoadCycle` refusal. It applies to the downloader and to whatever that downloader runs or starts in its flowed execution context. Before, the selection waited for the download, and only the nested evaluation's own token ended that wait. MOD gains the C#-host-policy bullet **Reentrant selection**. No rule ID is added or retired, no status changes, and no Lean change is needed (Lean has no external modules). The implementation is C#-only (`DeferredModuleRegion.HeldGateTurn`), pinned by `DeferredMaterializationCancellationTests` § 9/9b. Evidence: `../deferred-reentrancy-closure/`.

## B16 independent review correction — 2026-11-10

The owner approved narrow detection of actual recursive waits, not refusal of all flowed child work. The implementation's frame establishes only the child's wait for materialization; it cannot establish that the downloader waits for that child. The independent review reproduces false refusals in both same-unit and other-unit cases while a separate controller, not the child, controls downloader completion. Both required-success regression cases currently fail. MOD's Reentrant selection paragraph now distinguishes the approved rule from this incomplete implementation; no broader host contract is adopted and no rule IDs or counts change. The callback summary's host-overridable Message getter also escaped through Cancel(); the local B15 repair contains throwing/null messages and bounds type-name strings. Evidence, final check results, and unresolved findings: `../deferred-reentrancy-closure/reviewer-20261110/`.

## B16 host-contract closure — 2026-11-10 (owner decision: Option 1)

The owner approved Option 1 of the Reviewer 2 decision memo (`../deferred-reentrancy-closure/reviewer2-unified/report.md` § 3.4): the existing conservative fail-fast rule becomes the supported `RunOptions.DownloadCode` host contract. This is a deliberate host-contract change, not a finding that the guard detects only real deadlocks: no library-only rule can separate a real cycle from independent flowed work, because the reverse edge (the downloader waiting for the evaluation) lives in host code. Runtime behaviour is unchanged. Only the diagnostic message text changes; it now reads "possible load cycle" and no longer claims a detected cycle. MOD's **Reentrant selection** bullet now states the adopted contract (load-turn lifetime, the flowed-context rule, the refusal and its precedence, the effects, what is never refused, the supported `SuppressFlow` / after-the-load patterns, and the limits the library cannot catch) and replaces the "Implementation incomplete" paragraph. The two B16 sections above stay as history. The former required-success cases `IndependentEvaluationStartedByDownloader_WithFlowedContext_JoinsWithoutALoadCycle` are re-specified as contract pins (refused during the load, although nothing waits for them). Matching `SuppressFlow` success cases and the synchronous-call probe are added. No rule ID, status, class or count changes; no Lean change (Lean has no external modules). Evidence and final checks: `../deferred-reentrancy-closure/contract-closure/report.md`.

## B16 cross-program cycle characterization — 2026-11-11 (no owner decision)

Follow-up of the host-contract closure above; the adopted contract and runtime behaviour are unchanged. Two things about cycles through a second parsed program were untested or imprecise. First, the claim that the refusal also applies through nested loads of other programs this context started had no test: the only two-program test used an inner downloader that evaluates nothing. `DeferredMaterializationCancellationTests` § 9c now pins it (program 1's download evaluates program 2, whose download selects program 1 again: `LoadCycle` without a token, through two and three programs, with blocking, `Task.Run` and asynchronous downloaders, for the unit being loaded and for another unit), and mutations that drop the turn-frame chaining fail exactly those cases while every earlier case still passes. Second, MOD's **Reentrant selection** bullet listed "a cycle through another program's load that this context only joined" as outside the guard. The pins show the uncaught class is wider than "joined": it is every load this context did not start, whether its evaluation joins that load's run or queues for that program's loader turn. The clause is corrected and carries a dated note. Observed and pinned: such a cycle is neither refused nor bounded; cancelling the evaluations that started the loads need not end it, because a joined load keeps the other program's waiting evaluation as a consumer and its downloader token stays uncancelled; a waiting evaluation's own bounded token ends it as that one evaluation's cancellation, after which the loads publish or are abandoned and retried, the loader turns are free and every unit is loadable. Refusing such cycles would need the turns a waiting consumer carries to be registered on the load it waits for; that is an owner option, described in the task report, and nothing is decided here. No rule ID, status, class or count changes; the summary mirrors headings only and is unchanged; no Lean change (Lean has no external modules). Evidence: `../deferred-cross-tree-cycles/`.


## Front-end decision differential — 2026-11-15 (no owner decision)

Formal-evidence strengthening; no rule text changes what the law requires, and no Q, PV or X status changes. The independent adversarial audit of 2026-11-14 (§3 item 2) found that Lean receives the C# front end's decisions as input and that its own front-end specification functions were compared with nothing. `lean/CoreTests/FrontEndDecisionCases.lean` (generated by `FrontEndDecisionLeanArtifactTests` from `FrontEndDecisionCorpus`, imported by `CoreTests`) now guards five of them against the decisions C# made on the 3,098 parser-accepted programs of `SemanticExplorerCorpus` and `LanguageSpecCorpus`: `selectOwnedDeclaration` against the detection's owned-binding fact and `elaboratesToParameter` against the detected node (8,187 name occurrences), `elaborateOpenHead` (83 open heads, 4 parameter-owned), `familyLiftingSignature?` (58 clause families, 12 with none; 1 not guarded, a `1.5` head literal), and the whole `liftingSlotRole` table (26 callee kinds, 260 roles). All guards hold: no drift was found and none is recorded. A mutant in a scratch export (a captured ancestor parameter named `pi` no longer owns the name) is refused by the artifact test and fails two Lean guards. Three passages were made exact in `constitution-draft.md`: FORMAL-01's sentence on what each corpus's guards mean gains the new artifact; FORMAL-02's qualification "Conformance checks exist only for the owner walk … and … the signature selector" now names the new checks and what stays unchecked (implicit-parameter promotion, clause grouping, forwarding sources, alias formation, exposure classification); and its qualification on front-end mistakes that yield a well-formed wrong tree names the new guard beside the canonical expectations and the ownership table. Q-35 in `open-questions.md` gains a dated evidence bullet and keeps its status (the sketch-status question is the owner's; `elaborateClauseGroup`, `shouldTreatAsImplicitParam` and their known disagreements were not re-examined). `constitution-summary.md` and `issue-ledger.md` are untouched: neither states the superseded claim. `decision-lineages.md` is untouched as well: it holds a copy of Q-35's question as it was recorded ("The derived corpora bypass them"), a lineage record that the dated bullet in `open-questions.md` now qualifies for `selectOwnedDeclaration`; Q-35's own question text is likewise left as written. Evidence: `docs/design/formal-correspondence-ledger.md` § Front-end decisions, `artifacts/validation/front-end-lean-oracle/`.

Reconciliation with language changes already on `main` @ `9ec6bf3b`.
