Each item was verified against `main` (source, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/design/language-rules/`, tests) and against probe programs run on the CLI built from `main`, and only inaccurate or missing entries were changed.

1. **Strings (STR-03, owner decision 2026-11-11; `65885c4f`): applied.** The draft still stated string interpolation and double-quoted strings as law in 60 passages. VAL-02, OP-14, SYN-02 and SYN-15 are restated; VAL-12 is retired to a stub; the clauses in 31 other laws are removed or restated. No Constitution text had been proposed, so the wording follows `syntax.md` § Strings and `evaluator-and-hosting.md` § String values. A residual scan after the first pass found three more clauses (MOD-02's open-target list, MOD-07's recovery clause, RUN-08's order list); a second pass corrected them. No removed error kind is named in the rules any more.
2. **Decimal places (owner decision 2026-11-12 extending Q-10 D-F; `1d3e1354`): applied.** RUN-06 is restated from the task report's section 9 and the reviewer's follow-up list; every clause the list names is in Q-10's extension bullet. The names are replaced in nine other laws and in the Scope note. The RUN-06 example with `trace` keeps its host log from the unchanged engine read order; its display text and the minimum-before-maximum read order were re-probed (`tick()` gives `1.00`).

SYN-11, SYN-12 and SYN-13 gain the fourth star role, and DOT-05 and PAR-07 one cross-reference each. The family SUP was chosen because the law is about where an existing spread supplies items; the syntax stays in SYN.
4. **E-18 Q1 (owner decision 2026-11-14; `73d6256e`): applied** as one qualification of RES-08, in the closeout report's proposed words, with the spec case `impossible-read-in-untaken-branch-charges-nothing` as its example. Q2 and Q3 were not proposed as Constitution text and are not added.
5. **LOOP-05 (E-15 widening, `20742ba8`): checked, unchanged.** The claim was that the registry and summary mark LOOP-05 C#-only although Lean models the repeat driver. The body note already limits the marker to the rejection of a non-integral count. That is accurate: Lean rejects a negative count and counts in `Int`, and only C# has `ExpectWholeInt`. The index and summary markers follow the convention used for every law with a C#-only clause (VAL-11, OP-14, RES-08). Changing one marker alone would make the index inconsistent, so a scoped marker for all such laws is left as a proposal.

It does: `open-questions.md` § Resolved violations holds post-fix records, and PV-51 and PV-52 were recorded from commit evidence after their fixes. Both new records were re-probed on `main`. The owner may strike them; no rule depends on them.
8. **Totals: recounted mechanically, never by hand.** `count_open_questions.py` (the script of the earlier rounds) for Q and X, and a new `count_pv.py` for the PV classes, which reproduces the registry's stated 23 / 21 / 6 / 2 on the edition before this round. After the round: Q 84 = 55 + 1 + 2 + 26, unchanged; PV 54 = 25 + 21 + 6 + 2; X 52 = 47 + 2 + 3, unchanged. No Q or X entry changed status.

Rejected or not done: no new Q identifier was registered for STR-03 or for the decimal-places extension (Q-80, Q-64 and Q-10 carry them); Q-80 and Q-64 were not reclassified as superseded questions, because each question stays resolved by a decision (the PV-08 precedent "RESOLVED …, then SUPERSEDED BY DECISION" is followed); no dated entry of any file was rewritten; `issue-ledger.md` classifications are unchanged and #197 gets no entry.

Method and evidence: every edit is an assertion-checked replacement applied by script, and an edit whose anchor did not occur exactly once would have aborted its file. The scripts, their logs, the backup of the six files, the unified diff, the probe programs with their output and the counts are in `../constitution-catch-up-20261115/`. The repository was not changed.

## Demand-driven evaluation reconciliation — 2026-11-26 (owner decisions #202, #203 and #204 recorded from the record; none made here)

Reconciliation with language changes already on `main` @ `c9116ed6`, in preparation for the KatLang 0.9 release. The owner decided issues #202, #203 and #204, and they were implemented on 2026-11-23 (`65b1b136`, `66ebae56`, `108268c7`), followed by qualifications and host-policy work (`998870d8`, `76df3dc4`, `b2cd4f2b`, `81cb3f13`, `5a672ad0`, `e4398204`, `7cf1e7eb`, `d360fec2`). The Constitution, tracked in the repository since `dc43a0dd`, still stated the evaluation order they replaced. Each revised claim was checked against the issue texts, `docs/design/language-rules/`, `dotnet/src/KatLang/SEMANTIC-ALIGNMENT.md`, `docs/design/formal-correspondence-ledger.md`, `lean/KatLang.lean`, the hand-written cases of `conformance/features/` and the pinned C# tests. No program was probed, and no test, build or Lean target was run.

1. **#202, short-circuit `and`/`or`: applied.** BOOL-04 said that `and` and `or` evaluate both operands before validating either and never short-circuit. Its title, bullets, qualifications, examples, failure precedence and host-effect claims are restated, and it loses its *Open Q-57* marker; `xor`, `not` and comparison chains keep their rules. BOOL-02, OP-03, NEED-03, VAL-10, RUN-08, RAND-01 and the BOOLCMP preamble are amended. The example `trace(false) and trace(true), …` with four traces, the claim that `false and 1 / 0 == 0` is `DivisionByZero`, and the claim that the right operand's error wins over a non-Boolean left operand are replaced by the conformance cases `logical-*` and `hunt-logical-xor-operands-both-evaluated` and by `ShortCircuitLogicalOperatorTests`. The planned-loop example `S(a) = a + 1, a < 3 or 1 / 0 == 1` still fails, but only once the left operand turns `false`; it is replaced by that test class's `while-continuation-guard` and `evaluated-right-fails` programs.
2. **Q-57: narrowed, stays OPEN.** Its first half asked for the rationale of a rule that no longer exists: the owner decision of #202 removed that premise, and `xor` stays eager because its result needs both operands. Its second half, the position of `xor` on the ladder, still has no recorded reason, so Q-57 stays open with OP-01's marker. No status changed, so no count changed.
3. **#203, lazy `reduce` accumulators: applied.** HO-02 gains the demand rule of `reduce`: the collection's full top level first, `initial` once for an empty fold, only the last step demanded, deferred VALUE-only accumulators demanded through the ordinary roles, the initial-accumulator report and one step frame. HO-03 states the strengthened nested-call equivalence and keeps the value-only reading for `map` and for the elements. HO-04, CALL-03, CALL-06, COLL-02, PAR-01, VAR-03, VAL-10, RUN-08 and RAND-01 no longer say that `initial` is demanded whenever the builtin executes or that accumulators are ready values. RUN-03 records the C#-only fold-continuation accounting and the streamed route's later charging under a step limit. The stale outcomes `R(x, acc) = x` / `reduce([1, 2], R, 1 / 0)` = `DivisionByZero` and `R(x, acc) = tick() * x + acc` / `reduce([1, 2], R, 0)` = `5` are replaced by the pinned `2` and `4`.
4. **#204, lazy collections and pipelines: applied.** SEQ-02 and COLL-04 are restated under new titles. SEQ-02 states list positions, construction, sharing per list instance, demand order and the self-containing list; COLL-04 states the `map`, `filter` and `take` stages and what each consumer reads, and keeps the result-kind and emitted-count guarantees, which still hold. RUN-07 gains item (g), a run hands out only concrete values; RUN-05 reads host-operation arguments whole; RUN-01 limits a passive observation to what the run read; RUN-03 records the C#-only reservations and the read-generation bound; RUN-08's demand points and its strict list are rebuilt. The SEQ preamble, SEQ-04, SEQ-05, SUP-01, SUP-02, SUP-06, VAL-07, VAL-10, NEED-03, NEED-04, NEED-06, NEED-07, VAR-03, VAR-05, CMP-03, PAT-07, PAT-08, CALL-03, COLL-02, COLL-03, COLL-07, COLL-08, COLL-10, HO-02, HO-03, HO-04, RAND-01, FORMAL-02 and FORMAL-07 are amended; FORMAL-07 records the revised premises of formal ledger row E-24 (concrete values, `PlainRows`) and its open invariant. Stale examples replaced: the `first(map(…))` claims of COLL-04 and RUN-08, SUP-01's written-order log, HO-02's `[trace(1), …, trace(7)]` log, HO-03's `map(L, F)` = `L[3, 4]` (now `L[2, 4]`) and HO-04's `map([1, 2], A), A` = `S[L[1, 2], 3]` (now the conformance case `hunt-builtin-map-callbacks-run-in-collection-order`, `S[L[3, 4], 1]`).
5. **A pre-existing gap, corrected on the way: NEED-07.** "No shape-only count, lazy list node or lazy element rule exists" had been false since `772d936e` (2026-11-07), the owner-approved selective observers of a retained collector, which no Constitution record mentioned. Because the bullet had to change for #204, it now states the five observers with pinned witnesses (`selective-collector-*`, `hunt-need-collector-selection-demands-one-cell-once`). The rest of that change was not audited.
6. **R-1: checked, unchanged.** PROP-08 already judges an input read in a lazy position at the read, with the owner decision "the demand decides" of 2026-11-24 and the remaining qualification for the path step of an `open` target. SEQ-02 points to it; PROP-08 does not change.
7. **Records.** `constitution-summary.md` mirrors the revised titles and the removed marker. `open-questions.md` narrows Q-57. `decision-lineages.md` appends one section per issue and gives four lines that state a superseded rule dated pointers. `issue-ledger.md` maps #202, #203 and #204 to their current rules in a dated note, with three pointers, and changes no classification. Totals, recounted mechanically with the earlier rounds' scripts before and after: Q 84 = 55 + 1 + 2 + 26, PV 54 = 25 + 21 + 6 + 2, X 52 = 47 + 2 + 3, all unchanged.

Rejected or not done: no new law, because the list model fits SEQ-02 and COLL-04 and the run boundary fits RUN-07; no law was renumbered; no Q, PV or X was registered. Q-57 was not reclassified as partially resolved, because nothing in it was answered, only half of its premise removed; the owner may reclassify it. Dated pointers identify the superseded demand order in the October Q-25 records. The review updated the Authority and FORMAL pointers used by this reconciliation. Historical paths retain their original meaning.

The laws and their dated decision lineages cite the hand-written conformance cases, C# tests, Lean declarations and maintained rules. A revised example whose outcome no test pins as a whole was reduced to pinned witnesses (BOOL-04, SUP-01, HO-02, RUN-08), or kept where its outcome is unchanged and follows from pinned witnesses of its parts (SEQ-02's list example). `ModelCPropagationTests` pins the complete HO-04 `map([trace(1), 2], 5)` example.

**Independent review, 2026-11-26.** OP-03 now names the arithmetic rule in the index, and VAR-03 names the collector's selective reads. COLL-02/04/08 distinguish obtaining a stage's inputs from reading its positions, including `skip`'s collection-before-control order and the eager retained-collector prefix. HO-02/03 limit the fold comparison to VALUE demand with the same element values and distinguish the collection's full top level from nested lists. RUN-01/03/05/07 distinguish public values, withheld snapshots and terminal host exceptions. SEQ-03 and NEED-08 distinguish a list's deferred positions from the items of a sequence capture. The review checked the hand-written [collector cases](../../conformance/features/collector-demand.json), the [list and pipeline cases](../../conformance/features/collection-builtins.json), `LazyCollectionTests`, the three `LazyReduceAccumulatorTests.Initial*IsNotTransferredToTheReducer` controls, `PropertyObservationPositionReadTests`, and formal ledger rows E-19, E-22, E-24 and R-1.

**Documentation discrepancy outside the Constitution.** The retained-slice paragraph of [NEED-07](../design/language-rules/call-by-need.md) still says `[10, 1 / 0][0]` fails. The owner decision #204, `lazy-list-selection-reads-only-the-selected-position` and `CoreTests/LazyCollections.lean` all give `10`. The collector case `selective-collector-written-list-stays-eager` still correctly fails for `[xs*][0]` because the spread demands the items at construction. Its explanation attributes that outcome to ordinary lists generally. These are two stale descriptions of established behavior.

## Q-57 resolution — 2026-11-28 (owner decision of 2026-11-27 recorded from the brief; none made here)

The owner confirmed the Boolean order `or` < `xor` < `and` and adopted its rationale, which settles what the reconciliation of 2026-11-26 above left of Q-57. This round only records the decision, over `main` @ `a11ad7fa`. Its claims were checked against `KatLang.ebnf`, `dotnet/src/KatLang/Parser.cs`, the tests named below and the six records. No program was probed, and no test, build or Lean target was run.

1. **The decision agrees with the sources: applied.** The grammar nests `OrExpr` over `XorExpr` over `AndExpr`, and `Parser.BinaryOperatorPrecedence` gives `or` 1, `xor` 2 and `and` 3. No expression was found that groups otherwise, so no source changes.
2. **What the tests assert, as distinct from what the grammar specifies.** `ComparisonChainParserTests.PairwisePrecedenceContract_EveryOperatorPairParsesAsTheLadderPrescribes` asserts both boundaries as parse trees. In `ParserTests`, `Parse_NotOverComparisonThenLogical_GroupsLikePython` asserts the `or`/`xor` boundary (`a or not b xor c`) and `Parse_Precedence_AndBeforeOr` asserts `or` against `and`; none asserts the `xor`/`and` boundary. Three tests look like evidence of the grouping and are not. `BooleanValueTests.LogicalOperators_AreBooleanAlgebra` evaluates `1 > 2 or 2 > 1 xor false` to `true`, which both groupings give. `RestrictedLeadingContinuationTests.MixedPrecedenceAndComparisonSpines_AreTheOneLineAst` compares two spellings of one expression. `MaintainedDocumentationConsistencyTests.PrecedenceLadder_ListsTheFrozenTiersInOrder` reads prose. No tracked test or conformance case was found that evaluates an expression whose value separates either boundary.
3. **OP-01: applied.** The *Open Q-57* note becomes a dated *Resolved* note. The law gains the rationale as its second bullet, a qualification that BOOL-04 governs evaluation, and the brief's `Ready` example with the two expressions that separate the boundaries. Their values follow from the pinned trees and the pinned truth tables. The law-index row and `constitution-summary.md` keep *C#-only*.
4. **Records.** `open-questions.md` moves Q-57 to § Already resolved and keeps its row verbatim by cell, the narrowing of 2026-11-26 included. `decision-lineages.md` appends § Q-57 and gives dated pointers to three lines that call the rationale unrecorded. `issue-ledger.md` gains two dated pointers and changes no classification. Totals, recounted mechanically from the live checkout: Q 84 = 56 + 1 + 2 + 25 before and 57 + 1 + 2 + 24 after; PV 54 = 25 + 21 + 6 + 2 and X 52 = 47 + 2 + 3, unchanged.

Rejected or not done: the dated notes and sections of 2026-11-26 were not rewritten, because they describe that date. The rationale is not presented as the original reason for the parser's ordering, which stays undocumented. No conformance case or test was added for the two separating expressions. `syntax.md`, the tutorial and the generator guidance do not state the rationale and were not changed.

## Q-47 resolution — 2026-11-28 (owner decision of 2026-11-27 recorded from the brief and implemented; none made here)

The owner decided that a clause family has one fixed call arity and that, within any argument, a list or sequence pattern may contain collecting bindings under the ordinary structural-pattern rules. This round implements the decision in the Lean model and in C# and records it, over `main` @ `4fab2a56`. Which builds, tests and Lean targets ran is reported by the implementing task's result and its logs under `artifacts/validation/q47-family-collectors/`; this entry claims none beyond what it names.

1. **The decision needs a Lean change: applied.** Lean's clause binder had no way to collect, so the gate that refused a host-built collecting clause binder existed in C# alone (FORMAL-07 research). `Pattern.bind` now carries a collecting flag, `NeedPattern.ofClause` hands it to the one binder, and the allocation and the collector cells are the ordinary ones. No family-specific collector was added in either engine; the C# evaluator already carried the kind and needed no change.
2. **The restriction that stays is stated as a rule, with its reason.** A collector directly among a family's call arguments is refused because the family's call arity is fixed. Lean states why the premise is needed: a head that is itself a collecting binder has top-level arity 1 and would accept every supply count (`direct_family_collector_would_accept_any_count`), which is the disagreement the FORMAL-07 gate had closed. `clause_head_accepts_exactly_its_top_level_arity` and `family_zero_argument_acceptance_is_the_dispatch_gate` now carry that premise; the validation establishes it for every admitted tree.
3. **The collector rules of a family head are the ordinary ones, in both engines.** One collector per pattern level, no repeated name involving a collector, and the singleton rule (`(*xs)` valid, `((*xs))` not). The parser reports them per clause; both engines refuse a host-built head that breaks one, in the same order, as `IllegalInEval`.
4. **Duplicate heads.** The collecting flag is part of a head's shape: renamed collecting heads are duplicates, and `[x]` and `[*xs]` are distinct. The C# duplicate index tags a collecting binder apart from a fixed one, which keeps its key equal exactly when `IsMatchEquivalent` holds.
5. **Laws: applied.** PAT-04 and VAR-02 turn their *Open Q-47* notes into dated *Resolved* notes. PAT-04 states the decision and its reason and gains the `Tail` and `Payload` examples; VAR-02 names the family position; PAT-01, PAT-02, PAT-06, PAT-07 and PAT-08 lose the statements that the collecting forms belong to the ordinary language only; RUN-07 and FORMAL-02 no longer call the gate C#-only. The values of the new examples are pinned by `ClauseFamilyCollectingPatternTests.ConstitutionExamples_HoldAsWritten`.
6. **Records.** `open-questions.md` moves Q-47 to § Already resolved and keeps its row verbatim by cell. `decision-lineages.md` appends § Q-47 and gives dated pointers to five lines that state the old rejection. `issue-ledger.md` gains one dated pointer and changes no classification. Totals, recounted mechanically from the live checkout: Q 84 = 57 + 1 + 2 + 24 before and 58 + 1 + 2 + 23 after; PV 54 = 25 + 21 + 6 + 2 and X 52 = 47 + 2 + 3, unchanged.

Rejected or not done: Q-48 and Q-49 were not touched. `meta` binders stay rejected in family heads. No reachability diagnostic was added: `K([x, *r])` followed by `K([*r, x])` is accepted although the second clause can never be selected for a nonempty list, which is Q-49's subject. The value matcher of the Lean model (`matchPatternInto`), which production reaches with literal patterns only, was not given collector semantics: it matches nothing with a collecting binder, and the one binder remains the only place a collector is allocated. Dated notes, audit tables and lineage steps that describe the earlier rule were not rewritten.

## Q-49 resolution — 2026-11-28 (owner decision recorded from the brief and implemented; none made here)

The owner decided that, in a written clause family, each later, nonduplicate clause of the same arity after an unconditional catch-all gets one non-blocking warning. This round implements the decision in the C# front end and records it, over `main` @ `aac4b59d`. Which builds, tests and Lean targets ran is reported by the implementing task's result and its logs under `artifacts/validation/q49-unreachable-clause/`; this entry claims none beyond what it names.

1. **The decision needs no Lean change.** The warning is a static diagnostic of the front end, and those are C#-only (FORMAL-02). Lean has no parser, its first-match dispatch already makes the later clause unselectable, and the elaborated tree it receives is the tree of the same source without the warning. The evaluator is unchanged in both engines.
2. **The judgment reuses the family's ordered state.** The parser's family builder already held the clauses in written order and the duplicate verdict of each head. It now also remembers the arity of the family's reference clause and the index of its first catch-all, and returns one verdict per recorded clause. No earlier clause is rescanned, and a second catch-all needs no state of its own: every later catch-all of the arity is a binder renaming of the first, which is exactly the duplicate error.
3. **Three points the decision left to the implementation, and how each was read.** "The same arity" is the family's arity, that of its reference clause: a clause of another arity keeps its existing diagnostics, including `BranchArityMismatch`, and neither establishes a catch-all nor is warned, so `F(0, 1)` / `F(x)` / `F(0)` reports the two mismatches only. The catch-all a warning names is the family's first. The warning is reported where the duplicate error is, after the clause's body, so the two stand in the same place in the list and never meet on one clause.
4. **This is the first diagnostic KatLang reports that is not an error: checked.** The engine blocks on errors only, and a run's failure lists errors only. The closing entry of a truncated list takes the most severe omitted severity, so warning-only source runs at every diagnostic limit and an omitted error still blocks. A warning counts as a report and never as an error; the parser's recovery gates, which asked whether anything had been reported, now ask whether an error was, which changes nothing for the sources of earlier editions. Module caching and deferred materialization already read the error counters. A loaded module's warning is reported once, at its first load site, under the `[while loading url]` prefix, by the path that already carried non-error module diagnostics. A module that only a deferred branch loads is parsed when the branch is selected, and a selection that succeeds has no diagnostic list, so that module's warnings are not surfaced.
5. **Laws: applied.** PAT-02's *Open Q-49* note becomes a dated *Resolved* note. Its "No reachability check" bullet is restated as the rule, three qualifications are added (advisory and C#-only; which heads are no catch-all; what stays outside), and its example paragraph names the warning its `Loose` family gets and both orders of that family. No law title changed, and PAT-02 gains no *C#-only* marker, because the law is first-match dispatch. The values and the warning of the example are pinned by `UnreachableClauseAfterCatchAllTests.ConstitutionExamples_HoldAsWritten`.
6. **Records.** `open-questions.md` moves Q-49 to § Already resolved and keeps its row verbatim by cell. `decision-lineages.md` appends § Q-49 and gives dated pointers to two lines that state the old behavior as current. `issue-ledger.md` gains one dated pointer and changes no classification. Totals, recounted mechanically from the live checkout: Q 84 = 58 + 1 + 2 + 23 before and 59 + 1 + 2 + 22 after; PV 54 = 25 + 21 + 6 + 2 and X 52 = 47 + 2 + 3, unchanged.

Rejected or not done: Q-46 and Q-48 were not touched. General pattern coverage is not analysed, so the pair the Q-47 entry above called "Q-49's subject", `K([x, *r])` followed by `K([*r, x])`, still gets no diagnostic: that remark described the question as registered, and the decision limits the diagnostic to unconditional catch-alls. Nothing is reordered or removed, and a host-built tree gets no run-time diagnostic. No conformance case was added, because the corpus has no expectation for a warning and none of its programs writes a clause after a catch-all of the same arity. A run result carries no warnings, and the CLI's `check` prints a warning in the form it prints an error, without a severity label; both are left as follow-ups for the owner. Dated notes, audit tables and lineage steps that describe the earlier behavior were not rewritten.
